# Nothing is on fire, because almost nothing is switched on

Codebase evaluation of `AltivumInc-Admin/quantum-computing` at commit `4aec7f8`, 18 August 2026.

Eight readers went through the whole repository — the five Lambdas, the Next.js portal, the 45-notebook curriculum, the infrastructure and the documents — and raised 59 findings. Every one was then handed to two independent verifiers, under two different lenses, told to try to kill it. None survived at high severity, and that is not a clean bill of health: the wallet is unwired, the storefront is closed, and the tutor's metered path is unreachable in the shipped app, so the defects that would cost money are dormant rather than absent. Verification lowered 22 severities and raised none; 29 of 59 came back only partly true. 58 of 59 verdicts rest on something that was actually run or queried, not just read.

**59 findings raised. 30 confirmed, 29 partly true, 0 refuted.**
Published severity: 0 high, 25 medium, 34 low.

## Verification pressure

Nothing here shipped that was not refuted-by-default: every finding was handed to two independent verifiers,
under two different lenses, that had not seen the reader's other work and were instructed to try to kill it.

| | High | Medium | Low |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Claimed by the reader | 1 | 45 | 13 |
| Published after verification | 0 | 25 | 34 |

22 lowered, 0 raised. The two lenses assigned different severities on 11 of 59 findings and different verdicts on 23.
Where they differed the more severe verdict is published and the finding is marked **split**, with each lens printed.

## How to read a verdict

| Verdict | Means |
|---|---|
| **Confirmed** | An independent verifier opened the cited code and the defect is as described. |
| **Partly true** | The defect is real but the reader's framing overstated it. The Correction line carries what is wrong — read it before acting. |
| **Refuted** | It did not survive. Kept in the record so the same thing is not raised again. |

| Evidence | Means |
|---|---|
| **read** | The verdict rests on the source alone. |
| **ran locally** | Something was built, executed, or queried on this machine. The note cites the command. |
| **observed live** | A live host, deployed endpoint, or read-only cloud call. The note cites the command. |

No verifier deployed, mutated, or changed state anywhere. This document reads the system; it does not run it.
Nothing here claims a fix works.

## 1. The money path

8 findings — 5 medium, 3 low. 25 files read.

### How it actually works

The money path is one Lambda (`lambda/stripe/index.mjs`, 1072 lines) behind an HTTP API v2: three Cognito-authorized routes (`POST /checkout`, `POST /portal`, `GET /wallet`) and one public, signature-verified `POST /webhook` — the only route with `Authorizer: NONE` (template.yaml:186-190), pinned by template.test.mjs:53-63. One DynamoDB table carries three row families under a single `pk`: `WALLET#<sub>`, `EVENT#<stripeEventId>` (the only rows written with `expiresAt`, which is the table's TTL attribute, template.yaml:94-96), and `RECEIPT#<paymentIntentId>` (no TTL, because a dispute outlives the 30-day event marker).

Every wallet mutation funnels through `applyOnce` (index.mjs:200-298): a `TransactWriteItems` with a fixed leg order — EVENT(0), WALLET(1), RECEIPT(2) — whose catch branches on the cancellation reason *per leg index*, so a replay (EVENT) is never confused with a lost update (`expectedOwed` OCC on WALLET, `${field} = :seen` on RECEIPT). Grants run `splitAgainstDebt` → `grantThroughDebt` (index.mjs:487-508), garnishing `clawbackOwedCredits` to zero before any purchase becomes spendable, retrying four times against re-read state. Clawbacks run `reclaim` (index.mjs:520-690), whose arithmetic is absolute rather than incremental: `target = min(purchased, floor(purchased * fraction))` on a per-kind counter (`refundedCredits` / `disputedCredits`), each paired with a `${field}Unrecovered` field so a won dispute unwinds both the credit debit and the debt it created.

`CATALOG` (index.mjs:137-144) is the server-side truth for credit counts; `/checkout` stamps `spec.credits` into `subscription_data.metadata` (index.mjs:995) and the webhook reads it back verbatim from `subscriptions.retrieve`. `invoicePaymentIntent` re-retrieves the invoice with `expand:["payments"]` because Basil removed `Invoice.charge`/`payment_intent`; `invoiceSubscriptionId` reads both `parent.subscription_details.subscription` and the retired top-level field.

Landed 2026-08-17: two guards for what no repo test can see — `check-webhook-parity.mjs` (nine events plus a non-null `api_version`) and `check-catalog-parity.mjs` (prices, the 1-credit-equals-1-cent peg, product descriptions, retired at-cost claims) — both refusing to act unless `--expect-account` matches the authenticated account. `provision-sandbox.mjs` builds the mirror; `e2e-sandbox.mjs` drives real refunds, a real `pm_card_createDispute` chargeback, a test-clock renewal, and a genuine `stripe events resend`.

Nothing meters today (no `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_URL`, `WalletTableName=""` on both backends), so most defects below are latent — except the clawback arithmetic and the two runbooks, which are live procedure.

### Done well

- lambda/stripe/index.mjs:288-297 — `applyOnce` branches on the cancellation reason PER LEG INDEX (`failed(EVENT_LEG)` → replay, `failed(WALLET_LEG)` → CLAWBACK_RETRY, `failed(RECEIPT_LEG)` → CLAWBACK_RETRY) rather than on "the first reason". This is the difference between silently dropping a clawback and retrying it, and it is asserted positionally by the suite.
- lambda/stripe/index.mjs:247,274-276 — the `expectedOwed` optimistic-concurrency token now pins the wallet leg to the debt value read outside the transaction (`ConditionExpression: "clawbackOwedCredits = :expectedOwed"`). This closes the prior audit's finding that `clawbackOwedCredits` "has no floor and is computed from a read outside the transaction": a losing racer gets CLAWBACK_RETRY and re-reads instead of driving the field negative.
- lambda/stripe/index.mjs:784 — `invoice.paid` now calls `grantThroughDebt`, so a renewal garnishes `clawbackOwedCredits` before anything becomes spendable. This fixes the prior audit's "invoice.paid grants subscription credits without paying down clawback debt" (issue #218, now closed) — money-in behaves identically on every path.
- lambda/stripe/index.mjs:652 — a reinstated dispute clears exactly the debt it created, bounded by `Math.min(Math.max(0, owedNow), seenUnrecovered)` so a mid-dispute paydown cannot drive the field negative, with the remainder returned as credits. This fixes the prior audit's "a reinstated dispute returns credits but never clears the debt" (issue #217, now closed).
- lambda/stripe/index.mjs:918 — `GET /wallet` now returns `clawbackOwedCredits`, defaulting to 0 when absent. This fixes the prior audit's "no surface exposes the debt": a learner locked out with a positive balance can now be told why.
- lambda/stripe/template.test.mjs:243-255 — each alarm's `FilterPattern: '"..."'` literal is extracted and asserted to appear verbatim in `index.mjs` (`assert.ok(handlerSrc.includes(phrase))`). Editing a log line reddens CI instead of silently disconnecting the alarm from the branch it watches — the failure mode that made a 24-invocation dead webhook invisible on 2026-08-17.
- scripts/stripe/check-webhook-parity.mjs:68-75 — identity is asserted before any other read (`if (account.id !== expectAccount) … process.exit(1)`), and a null `api_version` is treated as a hard problem, not a note. Given that every `stripe` CLI profile on this machine resolves to the wrong account, asserting rather than inferring identity is the right default and is repeated in all four scripts.
- scripts/stripe/e2e-sandbox.mjs:83-88 — the harness refuses any `--table` whose name lacks "sandbox", with the stated reason that its seeds are absolute SET writes that destroy a balance rather than adjust it. `quantum-stripe-wallet` and `quantum-stripe-sandbox-wallet` live in one AWS account and differ by one word; enforcing the DynamoDB half of the isolation, not just the `sk_live_` half, is exactly right.

### Findings

#### `fd662bc2b` CLAUDE.md rule 8 says a test asserts TIERS/CATALOG lockstep; no such test exists — the only real comparison needs a live Stripe key

`lambda/stripe/index.test.mjs:122-131`

**medium** · Confirmed · rule-violation · ran locally · fix plan 04

**Claim.** The test named "CATALOG credit counts mirror the published pricing" asserts hand-copied literals (`assert.equal(CATALOG.ql_plus_monthly.credits, 1900)`) and never reads `web/src/lib/pricing.ts`. `grep -rn CATALOG` across `web/`, `lambda/` and `scripts/` shows the only place the two are actually compared is `scripts/stripe/check-catalog-parity.mjs:130`, which requires `STRIPE_API_KEY` and a network round trip and runs in neither `npm test`, `make test`, nor `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

> **Correction.** The guard is asymmetric rather than absent: because `CATALOG` is pinned to literals, editing `CATALOG.ql_plus_monthly.credits` in `index.mjs` DOES redden `node --test`. Only the pricing.ts→CATALOG direction (the more likely marketing-side edit) slips through green. Two extras the finder did not note: those literals are themselves a third restatement of numbers rule 8 says not to restate elsewhere, and `lambda/stripe/README.md:175-178` repeats CLAUDE.md's overclaim verbatim ("the offline test ... guards the first two"), so the false guarantee is recorded in two places.

> **Correction.** Two refinements that narrow the claim without unseating it. First, the literal test is a one-directional freeze, not a no-op: editing `CATALOG` server-side does turn the lambda suite red — it is only the `pricing.ts` direction that is unguarded, so "no test asserts it" holds for lockstep but not for the server side alone. Second, "leaves CI fully green" is not true for every value: my first mutation (1900 -> 2500) did fail CI, via `web/__tests__/lib/pricing.test.ts:118`, an unrelated credits-per-dollar monotonicity invariant. That invariant constrains Plus only to <= 2,093 (Pro is $59/6500 = 110.2 credits/$), so the realistic "round the grant up to 2,000" edit — and any upward Pro edit — passes clean, which is what I executed and confirmed.

**Impact.** Rule 8 states "Those two must stay in lockstep — a test asserts it." Editing `TIERS[].monthlyCredits` in pricing.ts to a new value leaves CI fully green while the server grants the old number: the page advertises one grant and the wallet receives another. That is the named failure mode of rule 13 on the one number that is simultaneously a price promise and a cost-to-serve bound (rule 12). The literals happen to agree today (pricing.ts:261 `monthlyCredits: 1900`, :277 `6500`), which is exactly why the gap is invisible.

**Evidence.** index.test.mjs:123-124 `assert.equal(CATALOG.ql_plus_monthly.credits, 1900); assert.equal(CATALOG.ql_pro_monthly.credits, 6500);` — no import of pricing.ts anywhere in the file. check-catalog-parity.mjs:130 `issues.push(\`pricing.ts grants ${tier.credits} but CATALOG grants ${spec.credits} (rule 8 lockstep)\`)` is the only real assertion, and Makefile:31-38 shows `stripe-parity` is a manual target requiring `op read`.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Confirmed, medium, ran locally.** Verified exactly as described. `lambda/stripe/index.test.mjs:122-128` asserts frozen literals and imports only `./index.mjs` (line 5); an exhaustive repo-wide `grep -rn CATALOG` finds no test that reads `pricing.ts` and `CATALOG` together. The only real comparison, `scripts/stripe/check-catalog-parity.mjs:130`, is unreachable offline (the script exits 2 without `STRIPE_API_KEY`, and line 130 runs inside a loop over prices fetched live from Stripe) and appears in neither `.github/workflows/ci.yml` nor `drift.yml` — only `Makefile:31-38`. #### `f0187a23b` Refund and dispute clawbacks are each capped at the purchase but never capped jointly, so a refunded-then-disputed charge reclaims up to twice what was bought

`lambda/stripe/index.mjs:589`

**medium** · Confirmed · correctness · ran locally · **split verdict** · fix plan 02

**Claim.** `reclaim` computes `const target = restore ? 0 : Math.min(purchased, Math.floor(purchased * effectiveFraction));` against a single per-kind counter (`refundedCredits` or `disputedCredits`). Each counter is individually bounded by `purchased`, but nothing bounds their SUM. A charge that is fully refunded (refundedCredits → purchased) and later disputed (disputedCredits → purchased, via `charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn`) reclaims `2 x purchased` credits.

> **Correction.** The headline scenario does not exist. Stripe's docs state plainly: "Disputes and chargebacks aren't possible on credit card charges that are fully refunded" (docs.stripe.com/refunds), so a fully-refunded-then-disputed card charge — the finder's stated route to `2 x purchased` — cannot occur, and my repro of it is a synthetic event sequence Stripe will not deliver. The reachable variant is a PARTIAL refund followed by a full-amount dispute, which Stripe documents as real but "uncommon" ("a customer can dispute a payment for the full amount even if they've already received a partial refund … it amounts to an over-refund of more than the original purchase" — docs.stripe.com/disputes/best-practices). Max over-reclaim is therefore (refundFraction + 1) x purchased, strictly under 2x, and the usual resolution unwinds it: on `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated` the restore leg zeroes `disputedCredits` and clears the debt it created (verified in the same run — credits return and `clawbackOwedCredits` goes to 0), so the phantom debt persists only on a dispute that is lost outright with no reinstatement.

**Impact.** The learner is debited for credits they never received; the excess lands in `clawbackOwedCredits`, which both metered backends treat as a hard spend lockout, payable only by buying their way clear. That is rule 7 inverted — where the outcome is uncertain the learner is charged MORE, not less. A fully refunded Stripe charge remains disputable, so the sequence is reachable, not theoretical.

**Evidence.** index.mjs:589 `const target = restore ? 0 : Math.min(purchased, Math.floor(purchased * effectiveFraction));` — the only cap is `purchased`, per field. index.test.mjs:1142-1161 ("R16") asserts the opposite invariant in its comment — "With both paths pro-rated the two counters can only ever sum to the purchase" — but only exercises amounts that happen to partition the charge (refund 1000 + dispute 1000 of a 2000c charge). Substituting `amount_refunded: 2000` then a 2000c dispute yields `refundedCredits=2000` and `disputedCredits=2000` on a 2000-credit receipt.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The arithmetic hole is real: `reclaim` at lambda/stripe/index.mjs:589 caps `target` at `purchased` per counter and never reads the other counter, so `refundedCredits + disputedCredits` can exceed the purchase. I reproduced it against the real handler with the repo's own ledger stub (`cd /Users/cperez/dev/altivum-inc/quantum/lambda/stripe && node --test /private/tmp/claude-501/.../scratchpad/repro3.mjs`): on a 2000-credit receipt, a 1000c refund followed by a 2000c `charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn` reclaims 3000 credits (wallet 6000 → 3000), which falsifies the invariant index.test.mjs:1141 asserts in prose ("the two counters can only ever sum to the purchase"). Severity is low because the path is dormant three ways: `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-stripe` shows it deployed but `aws dynamodb scan --table-name quantum-stripe-wallet --projection-expression pk` returns `Count: 0` (no wallets, no receipts, so every `reclaim` exits at the "no purchase receipt" branch), the storefront is closed, and the live endpoint is not subscribed to `charge.refunded` or the dispute events. Worth fixing before the storefront opens — the cap should be `min(purchased - otherCounter, ...)` — but it is not exposed today.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Confirmed, medium, ran locally.** I copied lambda/stripe/{index.mjs,index.test.mjs,package.json} into the scratchpad (node_modules symlinked), appended two scenario tests, and ran `node --test --test-name-pattern "VERIFY"`. A 2000-credit receipt fully refunded (amount_refunded 2000/2000) then hit with `charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn` amount 2000 ends at `refundedCredits=2000` AND `disputedCredits=2000` with `clawbackOwedCredits=2000` — a debt for credits never granted; with 1500 already spent the debt reaches 3500 on a 2000-credit purchase. The only cap is per-field at index.mjs:589 (`Math.min(purchased, …)`) and `purchasedCredits` is read exactly once (index.mjs:545), so nothing bounds the two counters jointly; index.test.mjs:1146 states the sum invariant that the code does not enforce. Both metered backends gate spend on `clawbackOwedCredits = 0` (qpu-core.mjs:446, tutor/index.mjs:478), so the phantom debt is a hard lockout payable only by buying clear. Severity medium, not high: metering is off and the storefront is closed (zero live volume), and a won dispute (`funds_reinstated`) unwinds the whole over-reclaim cleanly — I ran that leg too and the debt returned to 0, so permanent harm needs a refund followed by a LOST dispute.

*Split verdict: reproduces said Partly true; correct as written said Confirmed. This one needs a human.*

#### `f552e4181` Nothing prevents a second concurrent subscription, and cancelling either one unconditionally resets the still-paying subscriber to the free tier

`lambda/stripe/index.mjs:968`

**medium** · Partly true · correctness · observed live · fix plan 02

**Claim.** The `/checkout` tier gate is `if (spec.mode === "payment" && !(await hasPaidTier(sub)))` — it fires only for top-ups. A learner already on Plus who posts `{lookupKey: "ql_pro_monthly"}` gets a second live subscription rather than an upgrade. `customer.subscription.deleted` then does `applyOnce({ … setTier: "free", setSubStatus: "canceled" })` with no check that another subscription is still active.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the impact overstate live exposure. (1) "the tutor gates paid models on tier" — `aws lambda list-functions --region us-east-2` shows deployed `quantum-tutor` carries only `SECRET_ID`, no `WALLET_TABLE`/`USER_POOL_ID`, so `buildWallet` returns undefined (lambda/tutor/index.mjs:458) and the tier read at index.mjs:305 never executes; paid models refuse for everyone regardless of tier, so the flapping tier has no live consumer. (2) The unconditional reset passes `deltaCredits = 0` — the wrongly-downgraded subscriber keeps their credit balance; what they silently lose is the `tier` string and, through `hasPaidTier`, the top-up path (index.mjs:968), not their money.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the reasoning overreach. (1) index.test.mjs:311 does not pin the described gap as intended — its comment reads "subscribing is the entry point and must stay open," which pins that a FREE account may subscribe, not that an already-paying subscriber may open a second one; the duplicate-subscription case is simply unconsidered, which is a stronger position for the finding than "intended." (2) "the tutor gates paid models on tier" is true only in git, not in the deployed function: the tier check at lambda/tutor/index.mjs:305-306 sits behind `if (!metering) return refuse(METERING_UNAVAILABLE_MESSAGE)` at line 297, and `metering` is undefined live (WalletTableName ""), so today every paid-model request is refused regardless of tier and the tier flap has no current consequence.

**Impact.** Two subscriptions bill monthly and each `invoice.paid` writes `setTier: subscription.metadata?.tier`, so the stored tier flaps between plus and pro depending on which invoice arrived last — and the tutor gates paid models on tier. Cancelling the cheaper one downgrades a customer who is still paying $59/month to `free`, silently, with no error anywhere. The UI does not prevent it either: web/src/components/pricing/pricing-page-content.tsx:259-263 renders a `CheckoutButton` for every tier with a `checkoutLookupKey` whenever `billingLive`, reading no wallet state.

**Evidence.** index.mjs:968 `if (spec.mode === "payment" && !(await hasPaidTier(sub))) { return json(403, …); }` — subscriptions bypass the gate entirely (index.test.mjs:311 "POST /checkout still allows a SUBSCRIPTION from a free account" pins this as intended). index.mjs:794 `await applyOnce({ eventId: evt.id, sub, setTier: "free", setSubStatus: "canceled" });` — unconditional. No `stripe.subscriptions.list` call exists anywhere in the file.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, medium, observed live.** Both cited lines are verbatim (`awk 'NR==968||NR==794' lambda/stripe/index.mjs`): the `/checkout` gate is `spec.mode === "payment"` only, so a `ql_pro_monthly` POST from an existing Plus subscriber goes straight to `stripe.checkout.sessions.create({mode:"subscription"})` with no dedupe, and `customer.subscription.deleted` calls `applyOnce({setTier:"free", setSubStatus:"canceled"})` unconditionally. `grep -n "subscriptions.list" lambda/stripe/index.mjs` returns nothing, so nothing anywhere checks for a second active subscription — the defect is real in source and must be fixed before the storefront opens. But it cannot reproduce today: `aws amplify list-apps --query 'apps[].{name:name,env:environmentVariables}'` shows the live "Altivum Quantum Computing" app has NO `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_URL`, so `isBillingConfigured()` (web/src/lib/billing-client.ts:55) is false and pricing-page-content.tsx:259 renders "launching soon" instead of any `CheckoutButton`; and `aws dynamodb describe-table --table-name quantum-stripe-wallet --region us-east-2` reports ItemCount 0 / 0 bytes — there is not one live wallet, let alone a double subscriber.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, medium, read.** Both quoted lines are verbatim: lambda/stripe/index.mjs:968 gates only `spec.mode === "payment"`, and index.mjs:791-795 does `applyOnce({... setTier: "free", setSubStatus: "canceled" })` on any `customer.subscription.deleted` carrying a userId, with no re-check of remaining subscriptions. `hasPaidTier` (index.mjs:189-192) reads only the wallet's `tier` attribute; `grep -n "subscriptions.list" lambda/stripe/index.mjs` returns nothing, and the only `subscriptions.retrieve` (line 752) is the invoice.paid lookup — so no dedupe exists anywhere, and no proration/plan-switch code exists either (`/portal` at index.mjs:1012-1015 creates a bare billingPortal session with no `flow_data`, so whether plan switching is even offered is Dashboard state the repo cannot see). The UI claim also checks out: pricing-page-content.tsx:259-263 renders `CheckoutButton` on `billingLive && tier.checkoutLookupKey` with no wallet read. Nothing is exposed today — `isBillingConfigured()` (billing-client.ts:55-57) requires `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_URL`, which R15 keeps unset — so this is a latent gap that bites the day the storefront opens, not a live one.

#### `faecc93d8` The sandbox e2e harness never calls /checkout and hardcodes the grant, so the CATALOG-to-Stripe-metadata stamp and the custom top-up product remain unexercised end to end

`scripts/stripe/e2e-sandbox.mjs:281`

**medium** · Partly true · test-coverage · ran locally · fix plan 03

What the harness genuinely cannot prove is the Stripe-side behavior a stub encodes by assumption: that Stripe persists `subscription_data.metadata` onto the subscription so `invoice.paid` carries it. (2) "Nothing verifies the ql_credits product id" is too broad — index.test.mjs:259 asserts `price_data.product === "ql_credits"`, and `provision-sandbox.mjs:50,118` creates the product under that literal id from `CUSTOM_TOPUP_PRODUCT`. What survives is narrower and live-only: `check-catalog-parity.mjs` iterates CATALOG lookup keys and merely reports `price.product?.id` (lines 152-153) without asserting a product `ql_credits` exists, and provision-sandbox refuses `sk_live_` keys — so a custom `amountUsd` top-up in live would fail on a missing product with no guard to catch it first.

> **Correction.** "The only code that turns CATALOG.ql_plus_monthly.credits into the number the webhook grants … is never executed" is wrong. lambda/stripe/verify-live-checkout.mjs invokes the real `createHandlerCore` on POST /checkout with the production Stripe client for both subscription lookup keys (lines 47-79), creating real LIVE Checkout Sessions from CATALOG and asserting price, mode, livemode and session metadata.tier; its comment at lines 98-100 records honestly that Stripe does not echo `subscription_data` back on retrieve, so the credits stamp is unobservable there. The stamp is therefore executed-but-unverified against real Stripe, not unexecuted — and a repricing that broke it would not be silent repo-wide, since index.test.mjs:123 and :213 both fail. Relatedly, completing a Checkout Session is not something Stripe's API offers outside the hosted page, so CLAUDE.md's "drive the real /checkout route" reads as a browser step rather than something this headless harness could have done.

**Impact.** The harness's docstring claims it answers "Every claim of the form 'tests pass but this cannot be checked end to end'", and CLAUDE.md explicitly instructs "Drive the real `/checkout` route" because `stripe trigger` fixtures carry no `client_reference_id`/`metadata`. Hand-stamping the metadata takes the same shortcut one level up: the only code that turns `CATALOG.ql_plus_monthly.credits` into the number the webhook grants (index.mjs:995 `subscription_data: { metadata: { … credits: String(spec.credits) } }`) is never executed. A repricing that broke that stamp would leave this harness fully green. The custom top-up path — `price_data.product: CUSTOM_TOPUP_PRODUCT` — is likewise never bought, so the prior audit's finding that nothing verifies the `ql_credits` product id survives; `check-catalog-parity.mjs` imports only `CATALOG` (line 31) and never asserts the product id either. Rule 14 says buying must work before metering turns on; buying is the half still unexercised.

**Evidence.** e2e-sandbox.mjs:281 `"metadata[credits]": "1900",` (repeated at 318), 285 `await until("invoice.paid to credit the wallet", (x) => x.credits >= 1900)`, 295-296 `assert(r.purchased === 1900 …)`. `grep -n "checkout\|CATALOG" scripts/stripe/e2e-sandbox.mjs` returns no call site and no import.

The dispute/prorate steps likewise create raw PaymentIntents (`disputedCharge`, lines 199-217) and hand-seed the receipt, so there is no top-up step at all. Stronger than the finder stated: `checkout.session.completed`, `.async_payment_succeeded` and `.async_payment_failed` — 3 of the 9 `REQUIRED_WEBHOOK_EVENTS` (index.mjs:119-129), all handled at index.mjs:699-724 — are never delivered by this harness, so the whole top-up credit-granting leg is unexercised end to end against real Stripe. Medium, not high: the storefront is deliberately closed (R15) and metering is off, so nothing is broken now, but this is precisely the "buying must work" half R14 orders first, and it will bite at cutover.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The textual claims all hold: scripts/stripe/e2e-sandbox.mjs imports only node:child_process (line 62), hand-stamps "metadata[credits]": "1900" on subscriptions created directly via POST /v1/subscriptions (lines 281 and 318), has no /checkout call and no top-up step (grep for checkout|CATALOG|amountUsd returns nothing but a `topupPi` variable holding a disputed PaymentIntent at 259/377), and scripts/stripe/check-catalog-parity.mjs imports only CATALOG (line 31), never CUSTOM_TOPUP_PRODUCT, so the literal `ql_credits` product id is still unasserted against any account. The sharpest true consequence is one the finder understated: because no session is ever completed, the `checkout.session.completed` branch (index.mjs:485, 716) — the entire one-off top-up grant leg — gets no sandbox exercise at all. I ran `cd /Users/cperez/dev/altivum-inc/quantum/lambda/stripe && npm test` (74/74 pass); index.test.mjs:213-215 pins subscription_data.metadata.{credits,tier,userId} and :217 pins price_data.product === "ql_credits" against a stubbed Stripe, so this is a gap in live/sandbox wiring proof, not an unguarded stamp. Severity low, not medium: nothing is broken today, the storefront is closed by decision (rule 15), and the only shipped risk is that a first live custom top-up could 500 on a missing product id — which the prior audit already logged and its verifier already downgraded.

`scripts/stripe/rotate-webhook-endpoint.mjs:115-117`

> **Correction.** The impact paragraph is wrong about e2e-sandbox.mjs. Its refunds and chargeback are created by the script itself against api.stripe.com with its own `--expect-account`-verified sandbox key (`grep -n "execute-api|amazonaws.com|functionUrl" scripts/stripe/e2e-sandbox.mjs` returns nothing — it never calls the deployed function), so those never touch real money regardless of what key the Lambda holds. The genuine real-money surface is narrower: the handler's HTTP routes call `customers.create` (306), `checkout.sessions.create` (945, 1004) and `billingPortal.sessions.create` (1012), so a sandbox function holding a live key would mint real live Checkout Sessions if anyone hit its `/checkout` route. The sandbox Lambda would then transact against the live account, and `scripts/stripe/e2e-sandbox.mjs` — which creates real refunds and a real chargeback — would run against real money; its own `sk_live_` refusal only inspects `STRIPE_API_KEY`, not the key the function holds. CLAUDE.md's rule is "Confirm identity before any mutation; never infer it"; here identity is confirmed for one key and inferred for the other. Being untracked means this live-account mutation tool is unreviewed, absent from any clone, and outside every guard in the repo.

The trap is fully baited: the docstring's only worked example (lines 29-35) is the live invocation and never mentions `--secret-key-ref`, and `grep -rn "rotate-webhook-endpoint|secret-key-ref" --include="*.md" --include=Makefile .` returns no other documented invocation, so an operator rotating the sandbox swaps `--expect-account/--url/--secret-id/--function` and silently keeps the live 1Password ref. The target is real, not hypothetical: `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-stripe-sandbox --region us-east-2 --query '{name:FunctionName,secretId:Environment.Variables.SECRET_ID}'` returns `quantum-stripe-sandbox` reading secret `quantum-stripe-sandbox`, and `aws secretsmanager list-secrets --region us-east-2 --query 'SecretList[].Name'` lists both `quantum-stripe` and `quantum-stripe-sandbox`. The foot-gun is worse than the finder said in one respect: `grep -n "secret-key-ref"` returns exactly one hit, the definition at line 54 — the flag is absent from the usage header at lines 29-37, so an operator rotating the sandbox (`--secret-id quantum-stripe-sandbox`) has no signal it exists. I confirmed the untracked claim with `git log --all --oneline -- scripts/stripe/rotate-webhook-endpoint.mjs` (empty), `git ls-files scripts/stripe/` (the other four are tracked) and `git check-ignore -v` (exit 1, not ignored). Severity is low, not medium: the *documented* live invocation sources `STRIPE_API_KEY` from the same 1Password item as the default ref, so `--expect-account` transitively covers it, and the damaging case needs a specific undocumented mis-invocation plus someone subsequently driving the sandbox function's HTTP checkout route.

#### `f3c1bf7e2` The clawback's zero-floor on the balance is computed from a read outside the transaction and written with no condition, so a concurrent clawback drives the wallet negative

`lambda/stripe/index.mjs:622-638`

**low** · Confirmed · correctness · ran locally · **split verdict** · fix plan 02

**Claim.** `reclaim` reads the balance with a standalone `GetItem` (line 622), computes `const applied = Math.min(move, Math.max(0, balance))` (636) and issues `ADD credits -applied` through `applyOnce`. The wallet leg carries a `ConditionExpression` ONLY when `hasOwedGuard` is set (index.mjs:274-276), which happens only on the restore path (`owedDelta < 0`). On the clawback path there is no condition on `credits` at all, so the stale balance is the only floor.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the impact framing are overstated. (1) The lockout is not permanent and costs the house nothing: `splitAgainstDebt` (index.mjs:456-467) short-circuits on `owed <= 0`, so a later top-up ADDs its full credits to the negative balance and restores spendability once it exceeds the hole — the learner's economic position is the same as the intended debt, the difference is that it is invisible (`clawbackOwedCredits` reads 0 and the panel renders "unavailable" at qpu-submit-panel.tsx:331-338 rather than a shortfall). (2) It does not require two receipts: a refund and a dispute on the SAME receipt race identically, because the receipt legs guard `refundedCredits` and `disputedCredits` separately by design (index.mjs:517-521), so both conditions pass and both unconditional wallet ADDs land.

> **Correction.** "Permanently unable to spend ... so no surface can explain the lockout" overstates it. With `clawbackOwedCredits` at 0, splitAgainstDebt (index.mjs:463) takes the `!(owed > 0)` branch and returns the whole grant as `deltaCredits`, so the next renewal or top-up ADDs onto the negative balance and eventually restores spending. The real harm is a silent double-reclaim — the learner permanently forfeits the over-reclaimed credits they paid for, absorbed by a future grant with no ledger row saying so — plus a temporary period where the wallet chip and top-up prompt vanish, not an unrecoverable lockout. The finder is right, though, that the receipt-leg OCC (`${field} = :seen`, :679) closes the same-receipt race, so only cross-receipt races are exposed.

**Impact.** Two clawbacks against different receipts on the same wallet, delivered concurrently by Stripe (a refund of a subscription invoice and a dispute of a top-up), both read balance B, both compute `applied = min(move, B)`, and both commit: the balance lands at `B - 2*applied`, negative. The code's own comment at 617-620 says a negative `credits` "would read as 'metering unconfigured' to the client's counter()" — confirmed at web/src/lib/qpu-client.ts:191, `x >= 0 ? x : null`, and null renders the unconfigured branch at qpu-submit-panel.tsx:338. The learner is then permanently unable to spend (`credits >= :need` can never pass) with `clawbackOwedCredits` reading 0, so no surface can explain the lockout. Contrast lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:445, which does guard its debit with `credits >= :need`.

**Evidence.** index.mjs:622 `const balance = Number(walletRes.Item?.credits?.N ?? 0);`  ·  636-638 `const applied = Math.min(move, Math.max(0, balance)); deltaCredits = -applied; owedDelta = move - applied;`  ·  274-276 `...(hasOwedGuard ? { ConditionExpression: "clawbackOwedCredits = :expectedOwed" } : {})` — the wallet leg is otherwise unconditional. index.test.mjs:845 ("R4: the wallet floors at zero") runs against a single-threaded stub and cannot observe this.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, low, ran locally.** The mechanism is exactly as described: `reclaim` reads the balance with a standalone GetItem (lambda/stripe/index.mjs:621-623), floors with `Math.min(move, Math.max(0, balance))` (636), and `applyOnce` attaches a ConditionExpression to the wallet leg ONLY when `expectedOwed` is finite (index.mjs:247, 274-276) — i.e. only on the restore path — so the clawback's ADD is unconditional and the receipt-row OCC (`${field} = :seen`) guards a different item (and, for a refund racing a dispute, a different attribute on the same item), not the wallet. I reproduced it end-to-end against the real handler with a stateful in-memory DDB that evaluates the condition expressions the code actually sends: `node /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-cperez-dev-altivum-inc-quantum/960ed632-66f4-4518-9cd6-a7e8a35074be/scratchpad/race.mjs` → `status: 200 200 / credits: -2000  clawbackOwedCredits: 0`, while the same stub run sequentially (`seq.mjs`) gives the intended `credits: 0 / clawbackOwedCredits: 2000`. Severity is low, not medium, because the path is dormant: the storefront is closed (rule 15), there are no RECEIPT# rows to claw back against ("zero live volume", index.mjs:557), and the live QPU/tutor wallet gates are not deployed — but it should be fixed before the storefront opens, since it is the one wallet write in this file that lacks the OCC discipline the rest of it applies.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, read.** The mechanism is exactly as described. lambda/stripe/index.mjs:620-623 reads the balance with a standalone (eventually-consistent) GetItem, :636 computes `applied = Math.min(move, Math.max(0, balance))`, and the wallet leg in applyOnce gets a ConditionExpression only under `hasOwedGuard` (:274-276), which on the clawback path is never set because `owedDelta = move - applied >= 0`. `grep -n ConditionExpression lambda/stripe/index.mjs` returns exactly four, none of them a `credits >=` guard, so nothing serializes two clawbacks against *different* receipt rows on one wallet — and both invoice.paid (:781) and top-up checkout (:492) write receipts, so one subscriber holding two receipts is the normal case, not a contrivance. The `walletCredits: counter(raw.walletCredits)` parse at web/src/lib/qpu-client.ts:214 does turn a negative balance into `null`, and the panel then hides both the balance chip and the top-up prompt (`budget.walletCredits != null &&` at qpu-submit-panel.tsx:430 and :780), which is the harm the code's own comment at index.mjs:617-620 and test R4's comment (index.test.mjs:845-847) both name. I mark it low, not medium: the storefront is closed so no receipts exist to reclaim against, live is subscribed to 4 of 9 webhook events with charge.refunded and both dispute events absent, and reclaim() returns early on a missing receipt — the path is dormant in production and the window needs two clawbacks delivered concurrently against distinct receipts of one wallet.

*Split verdict: reproduces said Confirmed; correct as written said Partly true. This one needs a human.*

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*

## 2. Metering and the rate card

7 findings — 1 medium, 6 low. 34 files read.

### How it actually works

Two metering kernels share one injected scalar. `lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs` and `lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs` are both pure and both refuse rather than default: `creditsForMicros(micros, factor)` (qpu-core.mjs:96) and `creditsForUsage(model, usage, {free, factor})` (tutor-billing.mjs:276) throw `TypeError(RATE_FACTOR_REQUIRED)` on any non-finite or non-positive factor, and both multiply before `Math.ceil`, so the round-up stays bounded at one credit under any factor. The composition roots gate the *pair*: qpu-core.mjs:250-253 collapses `walletTable` to `undefined` unless the factor is usable, and lambda/tutor/index.mjs:191-195 does the same for `metering`; both emit a pinned `console.error` that CloudWatch metric filters turn into `quantum-qpu-rate-card-invalid` / `quantum-tutor-rate-card-invalid` (template.yaml:646-674 and 505-540). Both templates carry a byte-identical `RATE_CARD: !If [HasRateCard, !Sub "{{resolve:secretsmanager:${RateCardSecret}:SecretString:factor}}", !Ref AWS::NoValue]` defaulting to `""`, pinned as a literal by `web/__tests__/infra/rate-card-parity.test.ts:27-40`. `scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs` (run daily by `.github/workflows/drift.yml:78`) compares the two deployed values in memory and prints only PRESENT/ABSENT/MATCH — never a value, never a digest.

Money is integer micro-dollars. A QPU reservation is one `TransactWriteItems` with four legs — funding (`USER#` allowance `ADD spentMicros` or `WALLET#` conditional debit), the `DAY#` global counter, an idempotent task `Put`, and a `ConditionCheck` on `KILL` — so R16's four fences commit together or not at all; `MAX_SHOTS`/`DEVICE` are enforced in `validateSubmitBody`. `fitsAllowance = effectiveCap > 0 && ...` (qpu-core.mjs:558) is genuinely load-bearing: the allowance leg's `attribute_not_exists(spentMicros)` clause would otherwise hand a cap-0 learner one free real-money run. Refunds never re-derive a charge — both `releaseReservation` (qpu-core.mjs:655) and `reconcile.refund` (reconcile.mjs:133) return the `creditsCharged` recorded on the task row, and the reconciler deliberately carries no `RATE_CARD`.

The seam is the browser. `web/src/lib/qpu-budget.ts:34` re-derives credits with no factor; `qpu-client.ts` drops `clawbackOwedCredits` from the budget and `fundedBy`/`creditsCharged` from the 202; and `qpu-submit-panel.tsx` both quotes that factor-free figure and asserts, in prose, the exact conversion the server kernel exists to break.

### Done well

- Both kernels REQUIRE the injected factor instead of defaulting to raw cost, and prove it: `if (!Number.isFinite(factor) || factor <= 0) throw new TypeError(RATE_FACTOR_REQUIRED)` at lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:96 and lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:278, exercised over `[undefined, null, NaN, 0, -1, "1.3"]`-style tables at lambda/qpu/qpu-core.test.mjs:945 and lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.test.mjs:267.
- The composition gate makes "wallet configured, factor missing" degrade to metering-OFF rather than to raw cost, and makes it alarmable: lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:250-256 (`walletTable = walletTableRaw && factorUsable ? walletTableRaw : undefined`) plus the metric filter/alarm at lambda/qpu/template.yaml:646-674. qpu-core.test.mjs:951 asserts no reservation and no Braket call in that state.
- Scale-before-ceil in both kernels keeps the R7 round-up at one credit regardless of the factor — `Math.ceil((micros * factor) / MICROS_PER_CREDIT)` at lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:98 and `Math.ceil((micros * factor) / MICROS_PER_CREDIT)` at lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:289; the reserve rides the same expression via `maxCreditsFor` (tutor-billing.mjs:327) so reserve and settle cannot drift.
- R11's `effectiveCap > 0` guard is present and its necessity is written down: lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:558, with the early `return json(402, ...)` at 560-563 that stops a cap-0 learner from being routed onto the allowance leg, whose `attribute_not_exists(spentMicros)` clause would have passed.
- Provider usage is merged across BOTH stream events rather than read from one — lambda/tutor/index.mjs:400-403 merges `message_start.message.usage` with `message_delta.usage` — and `readUsage` returns `null` (not zeroes) for a Bedrock-shaped report at lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:239, so a provider field rename alarms via USAGE_SHAPE_UNRECOGNIZED instead of billing zero forever.
- The `expiresAt` landmine is pinned by file, count AND row identity: web/__tests__/infra/wallet-ttl.test.ts:56-71 allows exactly one occurrence in lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs (the `DAY#` leg on `ledgerTable`) and one in lambda/stripe/index.mjs, walking backwards to the nearest row key and TableName — so a copy-paste of the day-cap leg onto a `WALLET#` or `USER#` row reddens.
- The rule-6 scanner has real teeth and no factor value leaks past it: web/__tests__/infra/no-commercial-terms.test.ts:110-122 bans `RATE_CARD`-with-a-numeric-RHS and any `rate_factor`-family identifier with a decimal RHS, and every factor in every test is a labelled fictional 1, 1.5 or 3 (lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.test.mjs:251-257, lambda/qpu/qpu-core.test.mjs:938-942).
- Refunds read the recorded charge instead of re-deriving it, on both paths — lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:655-666 (`ConsistentRead: true` on the task row, with a loud `qpu-release-credits-unrecorded` log if it is missing) and lambda/qpu/reconcile.mjs:133-147 — and both refund legs carry `ConditionExpression: "attribute_exists(pk)"` so a refund can never mint a wallet row.

### Findings

#### `f5a2a88f1` The QPU panel tells the learner the credit debit is the AWS price converted at the peg — the exact formula the metering kernel is built to override

`web/src/components/quantum/qpu-submit-panel.tsx:313-315`

**medium** · Confirmed · rule-violation · observed live · **split verdict** · fix plan 01

**Claim.** The banner renders "Every run bills the platform's AWS account at the exact Amazon Braket price — {PER_TASK_USD} per task + {PER_SHOT_USD} per shot — with no markup" and then, on the wallet branch, "That exact figure is what comes out of your wallet — the credit cost below is the AWS price converted at $0.01 per credit." The server's conversion is `Math.ceil((micros * factor) / MICROS_PER_CREDIT)` (lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:98), where `factor` is precisely the markup this sentence denies. Four sibling strings say the same (lines 305, 348, 355, 786).

> **Correction.** Two parts of the framing overstate it. First, rules 5 and 9 govern debit parity across metered surfaces, not copy — no surface debits at a different markup because of this sentence; the rules actually violated are 13 (advertising what the deployed system cannot do) and PRODUCT.md:144, which already orders the exact string "You pay for these runs at cost. We add nothing on top." retired. Second, the money path is dormant, not mispricing anyone now: `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-qpu-submit --query Environment.Variables` returns only LEDGER_TABLE/EDGE_SECRET/TASKS_TABLE/RESULTS_BUCKET — no WalletTableName, no RATE_CARD — so nothing is debited from any wallet today, and the sentence is a false promise about a path that is off rather than a live overcharge. It is also already tracked: `gh issue view 210` is OPEN as of 2026-08-17 with this exact title; the new fact is only that the kernel now makes the claim falsifiable from inside the repo.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the reasoning are off. (1) Rules 5 and 9 govern debit parity across metered surfaces; UI copy cannot violate them — nothing here creates a divergent markup. The rules actually broken are PRODUCT.md's 2026-08-02 retirement order and rule 13 (never advertise what the deployed system cannot do). (2) "Now falsified by a kernel in the same repo" overstates: `creditsForMicros` requires `factor > 0`, not `> 1`, and the gate at `qpu-core.mjs:253-256` sets `walletTable = undefined` unless BOTH the table and a usable factor are present, so `creditsForMicros(cost, rateFactor)` at line 431 never runs on the live function today. No learner is currently overcharged; the copy is false against the founder's stated intent and becomes false in effect at cutover.

**Impact.** Rules 5, 9 and 13. The 2026-08-16 audit filed these as a policy contradiction (open issue #210, "Five shipped UI strings still say 'no markup'"); what changed since is that the mechanism landed, so the claim is now falsified by a kernel in the same repo, and by the same component that computes the quote. Line 786 in particular — "credits fund every run at the same Braket rates, with no markup" — is the newest of the five and is the copy that ships specifically on the credit-metering path.

**Evidence.** qpu-submit-panel.tsx:305 "You pay for these runs at cost. We add nothing on top."; :313-315 as quoted; :786 "credits fund every run at the same Braket rates, with no markup." Confirmed present today by reading the file, not by the prior report.

`web/src/lib/qpu-client.ts:207-216`

#### `fcbbf15c1` RATES overstates sonnet-5's current cost, and the comment labels the resulting overcharge "the safe direction"

`lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:66-74`

**low** · Partly true · rule-violation · observed live · **split verdict** · fix plan 07

**Claim.** The file states outright that `"sonnet-5": { in: 3_000_000, out: 15_000_000 }` is not sonnet-5's cost today — "sonnet-5 carries introductory pricing of $2/$10 per MTok through 2026-08-31, below the $3/$15 stated here" — and concludes "sonnet-5 briefly carries a higher effective markup than its neighbours — a transient rule 5 divergence in the safe direction." Rule 7 defines the safe direction as the learner being charged LESS, never more; charging against a cost basis 50% above the real one charges the learner more.

> **Correction.** The finder attributes "the safe direction" to rule 7, but the sentence scopes it to rule 5, and rule 5's stated hazard in CLAUDE.md is the opposite direction: "the *lowest* markup sets the effective margin ... every rational learner spends their credits exclusively on whichever is cheaper." An upward divergence on one model does not lower the margin floor and does not break rule 12's bound, so calling it safe *for rule 5* is defensible. Rule 7's text is also conditional — "Where metering is uncertain, the learner is charged less, never more" — and metering is not uncertain here; the figure is a deliberate steady-state choice with a stated expiry. What survives is narrower than "a rule 7 violation": an unqualified "safe" reads as safe for the learner when it is only safe for the margin, and CLAUDE.md's claim that RATES is now "a verified cost basis" is momentarily overstated for one of its four rows.

**Impact.** While the introductory period runs, one model on a shared wallet debits at a materially different effective markup from the other three — the divergence rule 5 exists to forbid — and it does so against the learner. Nothing in code, test or CI is keyed to 2026-08-31, so the condition ends only because the provider's rate rises to meet the table.

**Evidence.** lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:66-71 (the caveat) and line 74 (the literal). Practically inert today because metering is off and `sonnet-5` is unreachable without a wallet (lambda/tutor/index.mjs:294 `if (!metering) return refuse(METERING_UNAVAILABLE_MESSAGE)`), which is why this is low rather than higher.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Refuted, low, observed live.** The arithmetic is right but the defect does not reproduce. (1) The finder's rule-7 reading misparses the sentence: line 69-70 says "a transient rule 5 divergence in the safe direction," and rule 5's stated harm is arbitrage — "the lowest markup sets the effective margin... every rational learner spends their credits exclusively on whichever is cheaper" — so a HIGH markup on one surface is genuinely the non-harmful direction for the rule the comment names. Rule 7 is conditioned on uncertainty ("Where metering is uncertain"), and there is no uncertainty here: the provider publishes both figures (I loaded the `claude-api` skill's model table, which lists Sonnet 5 as "$3.00 ($2.00 intro through 2026-08-31) / $15.00 ($10.00 intro)"), and the code discloses the gap in the same comment rather than hiding it. (2) It is unreachable: `sonnet-5` is not in `ROSTER.free`, so lambda/tutor/index.mjs:294 refuses it with `METERING_UNAVAILABLE_MESSAGE` whenever `metering` (wallet AND factor) is absent, and `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-tutor --query 'Environment.Variables'` returns exactly `{"SECRET_ID": "quantum-tutor"}` — no `RATE_CARD`, no wallet table — so `RATES["sonnet-5"]` is never consulted in production and no learner has been charged a cent through it. (3) The "nothing is keyed to 2026-08-31" complaint is the design, stated at line 70-71: the literals are the post-intro steady state, the window closes in 13 days, and rules 14/15 keep metering off until buying works, so the condition self-clears with no edit. `cd lambda/tutor && node --test tutor-billing.test.mjs` passes 25/25.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, read.** The quoted text is verbatim at lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:66-74 (mirrored into the public web bundle at web/src/lib/tutor-billing.generated.ts:69-77), and the caveat is factually right: the claude-api skill's model table lists Claude Sonnet 5 at "$3.00 ($2.00 intro through 2026-08-31) / $15.00 ($10.00 intro)", so the committed literal is exactly 1.5x today's true rate and creditsForUsage (line ~275, `Math.ceil((micros * factor) / MICROS_PER_CREDIT)`) would debit sonnet-5 at 1.5x the shared markup. The divergence is real and the file concedes it, but it cannot bite: `factor` is required and throws without a deployed RATE_CARD, and sonnet-5 is unreachable without metering (lambda/tutor/index.mjs:297, `if (!metering) return refuse(METERING_UNAVAILABLE_MESSAGE)`), while every blocker to cutover (storefront closed, quantum-tutor secret uncreated, tier gate unbuilt) outlasts the 13 days left in the introductory window. `grep -rn "2026-08-31"` finds it only in that comment and its generated mirror, so the finder is right that nothing in code or CI is keyed to the date — but nothing needs to be, since the table is already the post-window steady state.

*Split verdict: reproduces said Refuted; correct as written said Partly true. This one needs a human.*

#### `f251543c8` The deployed rate-card guard checks only RATE_CARD, so a deploy where one surface is metered and the other is free reports MATCH

`scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs:33-54`

**low** · Partly true · rule-violation · observed live · **split verdict** · fix plan 06

**Claim.** `readRateCard` queries only `Environment.Variables.RATE_CARD` for `FUNCTIONS = ["quantum-tutor", "quantum-qpu-submit"]` and never reads `WALLET_TABLE`. But both kernels define metering as the PAIR: lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:249 ("Metering is the PAIR (wallet table, rate factor)") and lambda/tutor/index.mjs:190 ("Metering is the PAIR (wallet, factor)"). A deploy that sets `WalletTableName` on the tutor stack but not the QPU stack (or vice versa) leaves one surface debiting and the other free, and this script prints "OK: both surfaces carry the identical rate configuration (MATCH)" and exits 0.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the reasoning do not hold. (1) The rule attribution is wrong: rule 5 governs the markup at which *metered* surfaces debit, and an unmetered surface debits nothing at all, so there is no "zero-markup surface on one wallet" and none of rule 5's arbitrage (learners spending credits through the cheaper surface) can occur — the real hooks are rule 12 (an unmetered surface breaks the credit bound, and only in the tutor direction) and rule 4/one-wallet (the script also cannot see two DIFFERENT wallet tables). (2) "one surface debiting and the other free" is false for the QPU direction: with `walletTable` unset, `lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:563-568` refuses with `402 over-lifetime-budget` for every learner whose `effectiveCap` is 0 (`LIFETIME_CAP_MICROS = 0`), so an unmetered QPU is a refusing surface, not a free one. Also note the script's other OK branch ("metering is off on both surfaces") is honest regardless of WALLET_TABLE, because both kernels degrade metering to OFF when the factor is absent (qpu-core.mjs:249-256, lambda/tutor/index.mjs:185-195), and the wallet-set-without-factor asymmetry is already alarmed live (`quantum-qpu-rate-card-invalid`, lambda/qpu/template.yaml:657-676). Only factor-present-without-wallet is unobserved.

**Impact.** This is the only guard that can see the deployed half of rule 5, and it is blind to the most extreme form of the divergence it exists to catch — a metered surface next to a zero-markup surface on one wallet. Its own header claims to close the gap that "three functions can run three different configured values with a green drift report every morning"; the wallet half of the pair still has exactly that gap. The `FUNCTIONS` list is also hand-maintained, so a third metered surface escapes silently.

**Evidence.** scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs:44-48 passes `"--query", "Environment.Variables.RATE_CARD"`; nothing in the file mentions WALLET_TABLE. web/__tests__/infra/rate-card-parity.test.ts asserts only that both templates carry the same `RATE_CARD` resolve line and the same `RateCardSecret` default — the `WalletTableName` parameters are checked per-stack (lambda/qpu/template.test.mjs:125, lambda/tutor/template.test.mjs:144) but never against each other.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Refuted, low, observed live.** The literal code claim is true (scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs:33-54 queries only Environment.Variables.RATE_CARD), but nothing it prints is false and the scenario is not the rule 5 failure the finder describes. Because both kernels degrade the PAIR to unmetered when the factor is unusable (lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:257 `walletTable = walletTableRaw && factorUsable ? ... : undefined`; lambda/tutor/index.mjs:190-192), a RATE_CARD-absent function is provably not metering whatever WALLET_TABLE says, so the ABSENT branch's "metering is off on both surfaces" is sound; and the MATCH branch's wording is scoped to "identical rate configuration", which the script's own header (line 3: "Do the two DEPLOYED pricing functions carry the same rate configuration?") states as its whole contract. A wallet-less surface never debits credits at a divergent markup — the QPU returns 402 for any cap-0 learner with no wallet (qpu-core.mjs:563-567) and the tutor refuses paid models — so there is no one-wallet arbitrage between a metered and a "zero-markup" surface; the asymmetry is in fact a prescribed intermediate state (docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md:290-303 says deploy the tutor "with them empty first ... add them in a second deploy", with its own manual `get-function-configuration` verify step, while §2.3 sets WalletTableName on the QPU), so a guard failing on it would cry wolf on the founder's own sequencing. Nothing is exposed: `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-{tutor,qpu-submit} --region us-east-2 --query 'Environment.Variables.{RATE_CARD:RATE_CARD,WALLET_TABLE:WALLET_TABLE,USER_POOL_ID:USER_POOL_ID}'` returns null for all three on both functions, and `node scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs` prints ABSENT/ABSENT, exit 0.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, observed live.** The mechanic is exactly as described: `scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs:33-54` queries only `Environment.Variables.RATE_CARD` for two functions, and `WALLET_TABLE` appears nowhere in the file, so a half-cutover that sets `RateCardSecret` on both stacks but `WalletTableName` on only one still prints MATCH and exits 0 — and the cutover CLAUDE.md describes ("set RateCardSecret + WalletTableName on both stacks in one deploy, re-run parity") sets both parameters, so the re-run verifies half of it. I ran `node scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs` (ABSENT/ABSENT, exit 0) and `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name {quantum-tutor,quantum-qpu-submit,quantum-qpu-reconcile} --region us-east-2 --query 'Environment.Variables.WALLET_TABLE'` (all `None`), so nothing is metered today and the gap is entirely prospective, biting only at a future flip and only in the undercharge direction. Severity is low, not medium: the money-losing half of the scenario (tutor wallet forgotten → free tutor) is identical to the deliberate current live state, and the opposite half fails safe.

*Split verdict: reproduces said Refuted; correct as written said Partly true. This one needs a human.*

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*

#### `ff69eae9c` The reconcile sweep has no per-row error isolation, and wallet-funded rows widen what a single failure can abort

`lambda/qpu/reconcile.mjs:207-231`

**low** · Partly true · correctness · observed live · fix plan 06

**Claim.** `for (const row of rows) { ... await braket.send(new GetQuantumTaskCommand(...)); ... await markCompleted(...) / await refund(...) }` runs with no try/catch around the body. Any throw — a Braket throttle on `GetQuantumTask`, a `TransactionConflict` that `isAlreadyTerminal` correctly declines to swallow (line 187/200), or a wallet-funded row whose `refundLeg` gets `TableName: walletTable === undefined` because `WALLET_TABLE` is unset on the reconciler (lines 138-147) — propagates out of `run()` and abandons every row after it.

> **Correction.** What survives is narrower than the write-up: not "one bad row loses refunds for every other learner," but "a *persistently* failing row (one that throws on every tick) wedges the sweep for every row after it in Dynamo scan order, indefinitely." Transient failures self-heal on the next 5-minute tick because the sweep is idempotent and status-guarded, and the Lambda Errors alarm already fires. The `TableName: undefined` variant requires an operator to unset `WalletTableName` in a redeploy while a wallet-funded SUBMITTED row is in flight — both env vars derive from one parameter and one condition, so they cannot diverge by drift alone. The fix is still worth having (per-row try/catch that logs, records a failure count, and rethrows once after the loop, preserving the fail-loud alarm while bounding blast radius), but nothing is exposed today.

> **Correction.** Three parts of the framing overstate it. (1) It is not silent: `quantum-qpu-reconcile-errors` (template.yaml:495) fires on any thrown error within 5 minutes and its own description names this exact stall, so the missing `qpu-reconcile: {...}` summary line is not the only signal. (2) For transient errors (throttles, TransactionConflict) nothing is lost or double-applied — the skipped rows stay SUBMITTED and are re-scanned on the next `rate(5 minutes)` tick; only a deterministic failure is a true poison pill. (3) WALLET_TABLE cannot be "unset on the reconciler" independently — template.yaml:191 and :306 both derive it from the SAME `!If [HasWalletTable, !Ref WalletTableName, ...]` on one stack parameter, so reaching `TableName: undefined` requires metering to be switched ON, a wallet-funded row submitted, and then a metering-OFF deploy inside that row's SUBMITTED lifetime. Today it is unreachable in both directions: metering is off and `LIFETIME_CAP_MICROS = 0` (qpu-core.mjs:47) with the `effectiveCap > 0` guard (:563) means no hardware run is fundable at all.

**Impact.** One bad row stops medal counting and refunds for every other learner in that tick, and the summary log never emits, so the sweep's own `qpu-reconcile: {...}` line is absent rather than wrong. The `WALLET_TABLE`-unset case is newly reachable: the reconciler now branches on `fundedBy` read off the task row, so a row written while metering was on can outlive the env var that makes its refund expressible.

**Evidence.** reconcile.mjs:217 `const res = await braket.send(new GetQuantumTaskCommand({ quantumTaskArn: taskArn }));` sits bare in the loop; reconcile.mjs:138 `TableName: walletTable` inside `refundLeg` with `walletTable` documented at line 70-71 as "Unset = metering disabled". Confirmed still present today; raised as money-path finding #16 by the 2026-08-16 audit and not among issues #209-#230.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, observed live.** The code shape is exactly as described: `reconcile.mjs:203-231` iterates `rows` with a bare `await braket.send(...)` at :217 and bare `await markCompleted(...)` / `await refund(...)`, so one throw abandons the remaining rows and skips the `qpu-reconcile: {...}` summary at :230. But three of the finder's supports do not hold. (1) It is not silent: `template.yaml:495` `ReconcileErrorsAlarm` fires on Lambda `Errors > 0` per 5 min to the alert topic, and its own text says "Refunds ... are stalled until it recovers" — the abort is the designed signal. (2) It is not lossy: every write is status-guarded, aborted rows stay SUBMITTED, and the `rate(5 minutes)` schedule re-scans, so a transient throttle or `TransactionConflict` costs one tick of latency, not a lost refund or a lost medal. The rethrow is deliberate and tested — `reconcile.test.mjs:143/156/167/263` assert `rejects`, and :234 comments "It must NOT throw — one unpayable refund must not abort the whole sweep," i.e. the author already carved out the one case they judged should continue (`node --test lambda/qpu/reconcile.test.mjs` → 14/14 pass). (3) The `WALLET_TABLE`-undefined case is not "newly reachable": `template.yaml:191` and `:306` wire both the submit and reconcile env vars from the SAME `WalletTableName` parameter under the SAME `HasWalletTable` condition in one stack, so only an operator rolling metering back off mid-flight can diverge them — and live, `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-qpu-reconcile` and `--function-name quantum-qpu-submit` both show no `WALLET_TABLE`, while `aws dynamodb scan --table-name quantum-qpu-tasks --projection-expression "#s,createdAt,fundedBy,taskArn"` returns 5 rows, all COMPLETED/RELEASED, none carrying `fundedBy`. Zero rows enter the loop body per tick today.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The mechanism is real: reconcile.mjs:207-231 has no try/catch, so a rethrow from markCompleted/refund (reconcile.mjs:124, 200) exits run() and skips every remaining row — and I confirmed the WALLET_TABLE-unset leg would be a permanent, non-retryable poison, since the SDK does no client-side validation (`cd lambda/qpu && node --input-type=module -e "...TransactWriteItemsCommand({TransactItems:[{Update:{TableName:undefined,...}}]})..."` with an intercepting requestHandler printed `SERIALIZED BODY: {"TransactItems":[{"Update":{"Key":...}}]}` — TableName silently dropped, so DynamoDB answers ValidationException every tick). But the abort is a deliberate, test-locked choice, not an oversight: reconcile.test.mjs:136-171 and :256-267 assert `run()` itself rejects on any non-guard cancellation, reconcile.mjs:188-199 (test :231-254) carves out the one case the author judged non-fatal ("must NOT throw — one unpayable refund must not abort the whole sweep"), and template.yaml:495-513 alarms it at Threshold 0 / 300s with the description "Refunds for failed or cancelled runs and hardware-badge completion are stalled until it recovers." The residual real defect is narrow: a deterministically-failing row starves rows after it across ticks (a transient throttle only defers them 5 minutes, since every write is status-guarded and idempotent).

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*

#### `fcb02bbf1` The wallet-funded confirm step quotes a client-derived credit figure while the server's authoritative charge, already returned on the 202, is discarded by the client

`web/src/lib/qpu-budget.ts:34`

**low** · Partly true · correctness · observed live · fix plan 06

What the unparsed fields genuinely cost is post-hoc disclosure of what was charged; the 402 path does already surface the server's own figure (qpu-client.ts parses `creditsNeeded`, rendered at qpu-submit-panel.tsx:1412).

The founder recorded the first half of this; the new part is that the fix is already server-side and simply unparsed.
