## 3. The tutor after Bedrock

8 findings — 3 medium, 5 low. 38 files read.

### How it actually works

The tutor is one response-streaming Lambda (quantum-tutor) behind a Function URL, fronted by the CloudFront+WAF stack in edge.yaml (OAC SigV4, AuthType AWS_IAM, 300 req/min/IP, ReservedConcurrentExecutions 5). index.mjs builds createHandlerCore({client, corpus, verifier, wallet, rateFactor}) once at module scope; the production client (index.mjs:561) defers the credential to first model call, reading {"apiKey"} from Secrets Manager (SECRET_ID, default quantum-tutor) and deliberately not memoizing a rejection (index.mjs:544-557). The key never enters the environment and is never logged.

Per request: parseBody gates at 16 KiB, the slug is looked up with hasOwnProperty against corpus.json, and buildSystemPrompt(section) (tutor-core.mjs:196-213) — a pure function of the corpus entry — becomes a single system block carrying cache_control {type: ephemeral} (index.mjs:375). The cacheable prefix is genuinely invariant per lesson: nothing per-request is interpolated ahead of it. max_tokens comes from MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS[model] (800; 4,000 for fable-5, which cannot be told not to think) and modelRequestOptions(model) supplies the per-model thinking/output_config, because every knob 400s on some roster model.

Model selection: ROSTER.free (haiku-4-5) needs no auth and is never metered; anything else requires x-tutor-auth (a Cognito ID token verified in-process by aws-jwt-verify), a wallet tier read, isAllowed, then a pre-flight debit sized by maxCreditsFor at the cache-WRITE worst case. After the stream, settle() refunds down to real usage, capped at the reserve. Every metered price flows through creditsForUsage(model, usage, {factor}), which throws rather than default to raw cost — the RATE_CARD mechanism, injected by CloudFormation from Secrets Manager, alarmed by quantum-tutor-rate-card-invalid.

The migration off Bedrock is real in the hot path: MODEL_IDS are bare first-party ids, readUsage reads snake_case, deploy-check.mjs rejects us./anthropic./-v1:0 ids, template.test.mjs:172 forbids a bedrock: grant in template.yaml, and probe-tokens.mjs/probe-cache.mjs re-measure the cache minimums and the input_tokens semantics on the new provider. It is not complete outside that path: policy.json is still a Bedrock-only IAM policy the tutor README tells you to deploy, and README.md, PRODUCT.md, docs/pricing-cost-basis.md and the TUTOR_RATES docblock in pricing.ts all still describe a Bedrock tutor with no caching and no wallet. AskTutor is mounted propless outside AuthWall, so the tier picker, the meta trailer and the whole metered client path are unreachable in the shipped app; there is no free-trial counter anywhere.

### Done well

- index.mjs:544-557 — the Anthropic key is read from Secrets Manager on FIRST MODEL CALL, not at module load, and a rejection resets `keyed` so it is never memoized onto a warm container. The key never touches the environment (so `GetFunctionConfiguration` cannot show it) and appears in no log line; every `console.error` carries only `err?.name`/`err?.message`.
- tutor-billing.mjs:276-278 and index.mjs:191-195 — the metering kernel THROWS `RATE_FACTOR_REQUIRED` rather than defaulting to a unity factor, and the composition root treats a wallet-without-factor as metering-off and emits `RATE_CARD_INVALID` once per cold start. Raw cost is itself a divergent rate (rule 5), and this makes defaulting to it structurally impossible rather than merely discouraged. Guarded from the outside by web/__tests__/infra/rate-card-parity.test.ts:96-106.
- index.mjs:375 — prompt caching is genuinely shipped, with the breakpoint at the END of the `system` array so the varying question stays outside the prefix, and `buildSystemPrompt` (tutor-core.mjs:196-213) is a pure function of `{title, headings, text}` with no timestamp, user id or request-scoped value anywhere ahead of it. The prefix really is byte-identical across every question on a lesson.
- lambda/tutor/probe-tokens.mjs:23-29 and probe-cache.mjs:81-95 — the per-model, non-monotonic minimum cacheable prefix (4096 haiku / 1024 sonnet / 512 opus and fable) and the input_tokens-is-the-uncached-remainder question are RE-MEASURED against the first-party API rather than inherited from the Bedrock era, and probe-tokens uses `messages.countTokens` so the measurement bills nothing.
- lambda/tutor/deploy-check.mjs:50-58 — `isValidAnthropicModelId` is a migration-specific preflight that rejects exactly the three shapes a half-finished move leaves behind (`us.`/`global.` inference-profile prefixes, the `anthropic.` provider prefix, the `-v1:0` version suffix). It runs offline in `predeploy`, and it is the reason a surviving Bedrock id cannot ship as a runtime 404 disguised as a model outage.
- web/__tests__/infra/tutor-kernel-purity.test.ts:34-50 — asserts that tutor-core.mjs and tutor-billing.mjs contain no `process`, no imports and no `require`, precisely because both are copied verbatim into the public web bundle. This makes reading the markup inside the kernel impossible rather than forbidden — rule 6 enforced as structure. Verified: `web/out/` contains none of the cost model, so the copy does tree-shake away.
- index.mjs:111,147-155,383-388 — every await races `deadlineGuard(45_000)`, so a stalled model stream becomes a normal throw the catch can convert into the in-band sentinel, instead of a runtime kill that writes nothing and leaves the client rendering a truncated answer as authoritative. Paired with the reserve/settle orphan alarms at template.yaml:396-475, which compare `tutorReserve` and `tutorSettle` line counts hourly AND daily.
- tutor-billing.mjs:317-335 with index.mjs:227 — the reserve is sized at the cache-WRITE worst case (1.25x input) while the settle caps the charge at the reserve, so both legs scale through one expression and any output beyond the assumption is absorbed by the platform rather than billed. This is rule 7 implemented, not asserted.

### Findings

#### `f04de5935` AskTutor is mounted propless outside AuthWall, so the entire metered client path is unreachable in the shipped app

`web/src/app/layout.tsx:106-109`

**medium** · Confirmed · dead-code · observed live · **split verdict** · fix plan 07

**Claim.** `<AskTutor />` is rendered as a sibling of `<main>` (which is what wraps `<AuthWall>`), with no `wallet` and no `authToken`, and the component never calls `useAuth`. Its only gate is `NEXT_PUBLIC_TUTOR_URL` plus a `/learn/<slug>` regex (ask-tutor.tsx:221). Because `wallet` is always undefined, `models` is null (ask-tutor.tsx:114), `metered` is always false (ask-tutor.tsx:258), so the model `<select>`, the `x-tutor-auth` header, the `meta: true` opt-in and the cost trailer renderer are all unreachable in production. Every prop-passing case in web/__tests__/components/ask-tutor.test.tsx (lines 349, 360, 372, 419-449) exercises a call shape no caller makes.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the framing overstate. (1) Wiring `useAuth`/wallet into `AskTutor` today would change nothing — the wallet source is dark in production (no `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_URL`) and the deployed tutor has no metering env, so `metered` would still be false end to end; the mount location is a cosmetic symptom of metering being off by design (rule 14), not the cause. (2) "The sign-up wall does not cover the one surface that costs real money" reads as a breach it is not: the tutor is a public CloudFront/Function-URL endpoint and free-roster models take no auth by design (`lambda/tutor/index.mjs:294-296`, "the funnel model: free for everyone, no auth"), so anyone can POST to it with no browser and no pill — the control is WAF per-IP rate limiting, never AuthWall. The missing tier gate and trial counter are verbatim recorded open work, not a newly discovered gap.

**Impact.** Two consequences. First, the one surface that costs real money per use is the one surface the sign-up wall does not cover: a signed-out visitor deep-linking a lesson gets the working Ask pill painted over the gate screen, with no tier gate and no free-trial counter (CLAUDE.md lists both as open work; a repo-wide grep for a trial counter returns nothing). Second, the justification for copying tutor-billing.mjs into the public bundle — "the picker MUST offer exactly what the server allows" (web/src/lib/tutor.ts:33-37) — currently protects a picker that never renders.

**Evidence.** web/src/app/layout.tsx:105-109 `<main id="main" ...><AuthWall>{children}</AuthWall></main>` then `<Footer />` then `<AskTutor />` — outside the wall, no props. web/src/components/ask-tutor.tsx:114 `const models = wallet ? TUTOR_ROSTER[wallet.tier] ?? TUTOR_ROSTER.free : null;` and :258 `const metered = Boolean(models && authToken);`. Repo-wide grep for `AskTutor` finds exactly one non-test call site, with no attributes.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, observed live.** The mechanical claim reproduces: `web/src/app/layout.tsx:105-110` renders `<AskTutor />` as a sibling of `<main><AuthWall>…</AuthWall></main>` with no props, `grep -rn "AskTutor" web/src` returns that one call site, and ask-tutor.tsx:114/258 make `models` null and `metered` false whenever `wallet` is absent, so the picker, `x-tutor-auth`, `meta:true` and the trailer never run. I confirmed it ships that way: `curl -s https://quantum.altivum.ai/learn/01-foundations` returns HTML containing BOTH `id="ask-tutor-trigger"` and the AuthWall gate copy "Checking your access…", with zero occurrences of the model `<select>`. But the mount site is not what makes the metered path dead: `aws amplify list-apps` shows the live env has `NEXT_PUBLIC_TUTOR_URL` set and **no** `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_URL` (rule 15), so `billing-client.ts` `isBillingConfigured()` is false and no wallet exists to pass in; `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-tutor --region us-east-2` shows only `SECRET_ID`, no user pool or wallet table, so `lambda/tutor/index.mjs:292` refuses every paid-model request with METERING_UNAVAILABLE regardless. This is staged-but-unwired code on a deliberately dormant money path, which CLAUDE.md already records as open work ("Gate `<AskTutor />` on tier; add the free-trial question counter").

**Verifier (correct as written) — Confirmed, medium, ran locally.** Every cited line checks out: web/src/app/layout.tsx:105-109 puts `<AskTutor />` after `</main>` and `<Footer />`, outside `<AuthWall>`; ask-tutor.tsx imports no `useAuth` and its only early return is line 221 `if (!url || !slug) return null;`, so with no caller passing props `models` (114) is null and `metered` (258) is false — making the picker (501, gated on `models && models.length > 1`), the `x-tutor-auth` header (272), `meta:true` and the cost trailer all unreachable (the server confirms the trailer is opt-in: `if (wantsMeta)` at lambda/tutor/index.mjs:412, `wantsMeta = body.meta === true` at :291). Live: `curl -s -o /tmp/ql2.html -w "%{http_code}" https://quantum.altivum.ai/learn/01-foundations` returns 200 and the HTML contains BOTH the AuthWall gate ("Checking your access…") and `<button id="ask-tutor-trigger" ... aria-label="Ask about this lesson">` emitted after `</footer>` — the pill genuinely paints outside the wall in production, and a repo-wide grep finds no free-trial question counter. Corroborating: docs/feature-optimization.md:1052 records this identical finding ticked `[x]` "DONE 2026-07-20 (Category D)", yet `git show 39e1009:web/src/components/ask-tutor.tsx | grep useAuth` is empty and that PR never touched layout.tsx — the fix was recorded, not shipped. I hold it at medium rather than high because the tutor is deliberately free and unmetered today, the Function URL sits behind CloudFront/WAF per-IP rate limiting, and auth-wall.tsx's own comment states the wall is a product gate, not a confidentiality boundary.

*Split verdict: reproduces said Partly true; correct as written said Confirmed. This one needs a human.*

#### `fe64ef2d0` docs/pricing-cost-basis.md — CLAUDE.md's designated cost-driver source of truth — still teaches Bedrock cost derivation and calls prompt caching "not yet shipped"

`docs/pricing-cost-basis.md:54-56`

**medium** · Confirmed · docs-drift · ran locally · fix plan 08

**Claim.** The doc says "Prompt caching is not optional. ... a `cachePoint` on the system block cuts cost dramatically. Not yet shipped; it is the highest-leverage unshipped change in the repo." Caching shipped: index.mjs:375 sets `cache_control: { type: "ephemeral" }` and tutor-billing.mjs:207-208 prices the read and write multipliers. `cachePoint` is the Bedrock Converse spelling, not the first-party field. The whole "Reading Claude's real cost on Bedrock" section (lines 23-42) instructs deriving cost from Cost Explorer against "Claude Haiku 4.5 (Amazon Bedrock Edition)", which is no longer the tutor's provider. Line 47 says output "is capped at `MAX_TOKENS` (800)" — the constant is `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` and it is 4,000 for fable-5.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the impact framing are overstated. First, the "Reading Claude's real cost on Bedrock" section (lines 23-42) never claims the tutor runs on Bedrock — it is a generic note on Bedrock's Marketplace-SKU billing taxonomy, and CLAUDE.md deliberately preserves that caveat "for anything else in this account that uses Bedrock". Second, CLAUDE.md itself — which is always in context — already carries both corrections an engineer needs: it states the tutor moved to Anthropic's first-party API, that the old Bedrock/Cost-Explorer note "is no longer the tutor's problem", that prompt caching is not optional, and the per-model non-monotonic minimum cacheable prefix (4,096 on haiku-4-5, 512 on opus-5) with `probe-tokens.mjs` as the measurement tool. So the drift is real and unguarded by any test, but a reader is unlikely to be sent to Cost Explorer or to re-ship an existing cache. This is documentation-only with no runtime effect.

**Impact.** CLAUDE.md explicitly points here rather than restating figures, so this file is the one place a future engineer is told to look before touching tutor economics. It will tell them to go re-derive cost from Cost Explorer for a provider that is not billed on AWS at all, to ship a cache that already ships, and that the output ceiling is one number when it is per-model and 5x larger on the most expensive model. It also never mentions the per-model, non-monotonic minimum cacheable prefix — the single fact that determines whether the cache engages, which CLAUDE.md considers essential.

**Evidence.** docs/pricing-cost-basis.md:54-56 "**Prompt caching is not optional.** ... a `cachePoint` on the system block cuts cost dramatically. Not yet shipped; it is the highest-leverage unshipped change in the repo." versus lambda/tutor/index.mjs:375 `system: [{ type: "text", text: systemText, cache_control: { type: "ephemeral" } }],`. Line 47: "while output is capped at `MAX_TOKENS` (800)" versus tutor-billing.mjs:161-166 `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS = { "haiku-4-5": 800, ..., "fable-5": 4_000 }`.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, medium, ran locally.** All three sub-claims verify verbatim. `cat -n docs/pricing-cost-basis.md` shows lines 54-56 still say a `cachePoint` on the system block is "Not yet shipped; it is the highest-leverage unshipped change in the repo", while `grep -n "cache_control\|MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS" lambda/tutor/index.mjs` returns line 375 `system: [{ type: "text", text: systemText, cache_control: { type: "ephemeral" } }]` and tutor-billing.mjs:207-208/286-287 price CACHE_READ_MULTIPLIER=0.1 and CACHE_WRITE_MULTIPLIER=1.25 — caching ships and is billed, and `cachePoint` is the Bedrock Converse spelling that appears nowhere in code (`grep -rn "cachePoint" --include='*.mjs'` hits only this doc and an old audit file). Line 47's `MAX_TOKENS` (800) names a constant that does not exist anywhere in the repo; the real one is `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (tutor-billing.mjs:161-166) with fable-5 at 4,000. `git log -1 -- docs/pricing-cost-basis.md` returns 6c8ab06 (2026-08-03), while the off-Bedrock migration is 4b027bd (2026-08-17) whose `--stat` touched no markdown at all — the doc was simply left behind by the migration.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Confirmed, medium, read.** I opened all four cited locations and every sub-claim holds. docs/pricing-cost-basis.md:54-56 still reads "a `cachePoint` on the system block cuts cost dramatically. Not yet shipped; it is the highest-leverage unshipped change in the repo," while lambda/tutor/index.mjs:375 already ships `cache_control: { type: "ephemeral" }` on the system block (with a 15-line comment above it at :362-374 documenting the per-model, non-monotonic minimum prefix — 4,096 haiku-4-5 / 1,024 sonnet-5 / 512 opus-5+fable-5) and lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:207-208 prices `CACHE_READ_MULTIPLIER = 0.1` / `CACHE_WRITE_MULTIPLIER = 1.25`; `cachePoint` is indeed the Bedrock Converse spelling, not the first-party field. Line 47 says output "is capped at `MAX_TOKENS` (800)" — the constant is `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (tutor-billing.mjs:161-166) and it is 4,000 for fable-5, whose thinking bills as output against that same ceiling. The whole section head at line 23 is "Reading Claude's real cost on Bedrock" and `grep -ni anthropic docs/pricing-cost-basis.md` returns only line 40's incidental mention: the file never says the tutor left Bedrock. Staleness is explained by history — the doc was last touched in 6c8ab06 (2026-08-03) while the move and the cache shipped in 4b027bd (#235); CLAUDE.md names this file as the cost-driver source of truth precisely so figures are not restated, which is why the drift matters. Docs-only, no runtime or money-path effect (metering is off), hence medium not high; partially mitigated because CLAUDE.md's own tutor bullet already carries the corrected provider and the min-prefix fact, and the Bedrock Cost-Explorer method retains value for other Bedrock usage in the account and produced the two cache multipliers still in force (tutor-billing.mjs:198-206).

#### `f2d21e271` The public repo's front page and PRODUCT.md still sell a Bedrock tutor and name a blocker that CLAUDE.md records as closed

`README.md:31`

**medium** · Confirmed · docs-drift · observed live · **split verdict** · fix plan 01

**Claim.** Four places in the root README still describe the tutor as Bedrock-backed, and PRODUCT.md — which CLAUDE.md designates as product truth — states at 216-218 that there are "no verified Bedrock inference rates — the tutor's rate table is an explicitly unverified placeholder and is the single named blocker on reopening the storefront." CLAUDE.md records that item as done on 2026-08-17 (the move to Anthropic made RATES a verified cost basis). PRODUCT.md:118 also says metering settles "on real Bedrock token usage", and PRODUCT.md:120 says "Charged at cost — the margin is the subscription, not a markup on inference," which the shared RATE_CARD factor (rule 5) contradicts by construction.

> **Correction.** The impact line "a reader with only this repo cannot tell which provider processes learner questions" overstates it. The repo does say, in the authoritative places: `lambda/tutor/index.mjs:6` and `lambda/tutor/README.md:7` both name Anthropic and explicitly record the move off Bedrock, and `web/__tests__/app/privacy-page.test.tsx:48` asserts "Bedrock" is absent from the privacy page in both locales. The real defect is narrower and still worth fixing: the root README and PRODUCT.md are the two files that were not swept, so the repo now contradicts itself, with the front page and the designated product-truth doc on the losing side. Also note README:627's stack-table entry is the weakest of the four — Bedrock is still what the currently *deployed* (un-redeployed) tutor runs on, per CLAUDE.md's own drift note — but no tracked code path calls Bedrock any more, so it is stale too.

> **Correction.** Two pieces of the finder's framing overreach. (1) "A reader with only this repo cannot tell which provider processes learner questions" is false — `lambda/tutor/README.md:4,7` (linked from the very README row at line 473) states the move off Bedrock in a callout, and `web/__tests__/app/privacy-page.test.tsx:39-48` test-guards the user-facing privacy page in both locales to name Anthropic and to contain no "Bedrock". The repo is self-contradictory, not silent, and the legally-relevant surface is the correct one. (2) The no-markup sentence is not contradicted by rule 5 "by construction" — rule 5 mandates a *uniform* markup across metered surfaces, which a factor of exactly 1.0 would satisfy. What contradicts it is rule 6 (the spread lives in deployed config), rule 9's supersession note that the Lambda "debits raw cost today, capturing no margin at all" as a defect to close, and CLAUDE.md's open-work item that `TUTOR_RATES.typicalCreditsPerQuestion` "goes stale under any factor above 1."

**Impact.** The repo is public. Its front page and its product document describe a provider the code no longer calls, and PRODUCT.md advertises a storefront blocker that no longer exists while simultaneously making a no-markup claim the metering design has abandoned — the same class of false claim the prior audit's findings 1-3 raised on the pricing page (issues #209-#211, still open). A reader with only this repo cannot tell which provider processes learner questions.

**Evidence.** README.md:31 "the streaming lesson tutor (Bedrock)"; README.md:327 "streaming Bedrock Lambda"; README.md:473 "grounded in the current lesson via Amazon Bedrock"; README.md:627 lists Bedrock in the cloud stack. PRODUCT.md:118 "settle on real Bedrock token usage"; PRODUCT.md:120 "Charged at cost — the margin is the subscription, not a markup on inference."; PRODUCT.md:216-218 "no verified Bedrock inference rates ... the single named blocker on reopening the storefront."

**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, medium, ran locally.** All six cited lines reproduce verbatim: `grep -n -i bedrock README.md` returns exactly lines 31, 327, 473, 627, and `sed -n '110,125p;205,220p' PRODUCT.md` shows "settle on real Bedrock token usage" (118), "Charged at cost — the margin is the subscription, not a markup on inference" (120), and "no verified Bedrock inference rates … the single named blocker on reopening the storefront" (216-218). The code contradicts all of it: `lambda/tutor/index.mjs:6` states "PROVIDER, 2026-08-17: Anthropic's first-party Messages API, not Amazon Bedrock", line 27 imports `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, the settle path reads Anthropic `message_start`/`cache_creation_input_tokens` (index.mjs:366,395), and `lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:276-289` requires an injected `factor` (`RATE_FACTOR_REQUIRED`, throws rather than default to raw cost) — so "charged at cost, not a markup on inference" is false by construction under the settled rule 5 design. `git log --oneline -3 -- README.md PRODUCT.md` ends at 95dc909, strictly before 4b027bd ("move off Bedrock to the Anthropic API"), and no test scans either file (`tests/test_pricing_prose.py:29` explicitly exempts PRODUCT.md as an "internal design doc", which it is not — the repo is public and CLAUDE.md designates it product truth).

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, medium, observed live.** Every cited line is exactly as quoted (verified with `awk 'NR==31||NR==327||NR==473||NR==627' README.md` and `sed -n '116,121p;214,218p' PRODUCT.md`), and a repo-wide grep shows Bedrock now survives only in README.md, PRODUCT.md, CLAUDE.md's own "we moved off it" note, and two tests — `lambda/tutor/index.mjs:1-27` imports `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, and `lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:59` says the rate table "is verifiable rather than asserted," directly contradicting PRODUCT.md:216-218's "explicitly unverified placeholder … single named blocker." I also confirmed production has moved: `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-tutor --region us-east-2` returns `Env: {SECRET_ID: "quantum-tutor"}` with no Bedrock model parameters, LastModified 2026-08-18T01:08:34Z — so the README is stale against both git and the live function, killing the only defense that it still described production. PRODUCT.md:120's "Charged at cost — the margin is the subscription, not a markup on inference" is likewise abandoned: `lambda/tutor/index.mjs:183-195` and `lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:84-93` both require an injected `rateFactor` and refuse rather than price at raw cost.

*Split verdict: reproduces said Confirmed; correct as written said Partly true. This one needs a human.*

#### `f25112b4a` resolveModel/TIER_DEFAULT are dead code that encode the opposite policy from the handler, kept alive only by their own tests

`lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:185-193`

**low** · Confirmed · dead-code · ran locally · **split verdict** · fix plan 07

**Claim.** `resolveModel` documents and implements a silent downgrade — "an unknown or disallowed request quietly serves the tier default rather than failing a learner's question" — and `tutor-billing.test.mjs:116` pins `resolveModel("free", "opus-5") === "haiku-4-5"`. index.mjs does the exact opposite at :306 (`if (!isAllowed(tier, requested)) return refuse(NOT_IN_PLAN_MESSAGE);`) and its comment at :286-288 says why: "serving Haiku to someone who asked for (and expected to pay for) Opus is a mis-serve, not a graceful degrade." Neither `resolveModel` nor `TIER_DEFAULT` is imported by index.mjs, ask-tutor.tsx, or any other non-test file.

> **Correction.** Two framing corrections. (1) The contradiction is narrower than "the exact opposite" but the finder's own example is the right one: for an UNKNOWN model name the handler also falls through to a default (index.mjs:199 `let model = "haiku-4-5"`, the guard at :293 being `requested && MODEL_IDS[requested]`), so the genuine conflict is the KNOWN-but-disallowed case — precisely what test line 116 pins. The finder also missed a second divergence that strengthens the case: `TIER_DEFAULT.pro === "opus-5"` (verified by execution) while the handler hard-defaults every caller, pro included, to haiku-4-5 when no model is named, so TIER_DEFAULT describes a per-tier default the shipped handler has never had. (2) "copied verbatim into the public web bundle" overstates: web/src/lib/tutor-billing.generated.ts is gitignored (.gitignore:68), generated at build time, and web/src/lib/tutor.ts:38 imports only `ROSTER` and `MODEL_LABELS` from it — the dead exports reach no web consumer, and I could not confirm they survive tree-shaking into the shipped chunks. The defect is repo hygiene and a misleading green test, not shipped behavior or a public-surface leak.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the finder's framing overreach. (1) "index.mjs does the exact opposite" holds only for a known-but-disallowed model; for an unknown model name the handler's own comment (index.mjs:288-289) endorses falling through to the default, which is what resolveModel does — so of the four assertions cited, only tutor-billing.test.mjs:116 encodes the contradicted policy. (2) "both are copied verbatim into the public web bundle" overstates the exposure: gen-tutor-core.mjs copies the kernel into web/src/lib/tutor-billing.generated.ts, which is gitignored and re-generated, and only ROSTER/MODEL_LABELS are imported from it; the functions carry no markup, cost basis, or margin math, so nothing rule-6 relevant is published either way (a grep of the existing static export in web/out for these identifiers finds nothing).

**Impact.** Two contradictory policies live in the same module, one of them test-asserted, and both are copied verbatim into the public web bundle by scripts/gen-tutor-core.mjs. The green test makes the wrong one look load-bearing, which is how a future editor "restores consistency" by making the handler downgrade silently — the exact mis-serve the handler comment forbids. Same shape as the prior audit's finding 39 (a dead branch kept alive by a test that fabricates its trigger).

**Evidence.** tutor-billing.mjs:189-193 `export function resolveModel(tier, requested) { const t = ROSTER[tier] ? tier : "free"; if (requested && isAllowed(t, requested)) return requested; return TIER_DEFAULT[t]; }` — grep across lambda/, web/src, web/__tests__ and scripts/ finds it referenced only in tutor-billing.test.mjs (lines 109-118) and in the .aws-sam build copy.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, low, ran locally.** Reproduces exactly. `grep -rn "resolveModel\|TIER_DEFAULT" web/src web/__tests__ scripts lambda` (excluding node_modules) returns only lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:45,189-192, its own test at :109-118, the gitignored build copy web/src/lib/tutor-billing.generated.ts, and the .aws-sam build — no non-test caller. `git log -S"resolveModel" -- lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs lambda/tutor/index.mjs` returns a single commit (4504100), i.e. it was born unused. `node -e "import('./lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs')…"` printed `resolveModel('free','opus-5') = haiku-4-5`, the silent downgrade its doc comment (tutor-billing.mjs:185-188) advertises, while index.mjs:306 does `if (!isAllowed(tier, requested)) return refuse(NOT_IN_PLAN_MESSAGE)` for exactly that case, with the comment at :284-288 calling the downgrade "a mis-serve, not a graceful degrade"; `node --test lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.test.mjs` passes 25/25, so the wrong policy is the test-asserted one.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, read.** The dead-code core stands: `grep -rn "resolveModel\|TIER_DEFAULT" . --include=*.mjs --include=*.ts --include=*.tsx` (excluding node_modules and the audit's own .claude/worktrees copies) returns only lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:45,189-193 and its own test at tutor-billing.test.mjs:109-118 — index.mjs imports ROSTER/MODEL_IDS/isAllowed but never resolveModel or TIER_DEFAULT (index.mjs:42-54), and web/src/lib/tutor.ts:38 imports only `{ ROSTER, MODEL_LABELS }` from the generated copy. The policy clash is real but narrower than claimed: index.mjs:290-308 refuses only a KNOWN-but-unauthorized model (`if (!isAllowed(tier, requested)) return refuse(NOT_IN_PLAN_MESSAGE)` at :306) while its own comment at :288-289 says "Unknown names fall through to the default" — which is exactly what resolveModel does, so only test line 116 (`resolveModel("free","opus-5") === "haiku-4-5"`) pins a behaviour the handler contradicts; lines 109 and 118 agree with the handler's stated intent. Severity stays low: no live path executes it, and the sharper unnoticed divergence is that index.mjs:199 hardcodes `let model = "haiku-4-5"` for every tier, so TIER_DEFAULT's documented per-tier defaults (plus→sonnet-5, pro→opus-5) are unimplemented in the rule-7 safe direction.

*Split verdict: reproduces said Confirmed; correct as written said Partly true. This one needs a human.*

#### `f3b8514db` The TUTOR_RATES docblock in pricing.ts states three things about the tutor path that are all false since the migration

`web/src/lib/pricing.ts:162-168`

**low** · Confirmed · docs-drift · observed live · **split verdict** · fix plan 08

**Claim.** The comment above the published tutor rates asserts that index.mjs "binds one `process.env.TUTOR_MODEL_ID` (the deployed profile resolves to Claude Haiku 4.5)" and that "`<AskTutor />` posts only `{slug, question}` — there is no model parameter, no tier lookup, and no wallet call anywhere on the tutor path." All three are wrong today: TUTOR_MODEL_ID no longer exists (handler-wiring.test.mjs:83 asserts it is a "Bedrock-era leftover"), "the deployed profile" is the retired Bedrock inference profile, ask-tutor.tsx:260 posts `{slug, question, model, meta: true}` when metered, and index.mjs:458-515 is an entire wallet with readTier/debit/credit.

> **Correction.** "All three are wrong today" overstates it. Two of the three remain true of the running system: the only production mount is a bare `<AskTutor />` at web/src/app/layout.tsx:109 (no `wallet`, no `authToken`), so `metered` at ask-tutor.tsx:258 is always false and the shipped client really does post only `{slug, question}`; and the live function's env carries no `WALLET_TABLE`, so `buildWallet()` (index.mjs:458-459) returns undefined and no wallet call executes. What is unambiguously false is the mechanism half — `TUTOR_MODEL_ID` and "the deployed profile" — plus the implication that the tier/model machinery does not exist in the codebase.

> **Correction.** Severity lowered to low, and one qualifier the finder omitted: the docblock's operative conclusion is still true. WALLET_TABLE is unset on the deployed function, so `buildWallet` returns `undefined` (index.mjs:458), paid models refuse, and every live question is still answered free by haiku-4-5 (ROSTER.free, tutor-billing.mjs:36). No published price, credit figure, or user-facing string is wrong — this is stale mechanism prose in a comment, not a false honesty claim, and it touches no rule 6 or rule 13 surface. Worth folding into the same fix: the identical stale sentence is duplicated as a comment justifying a copy-honesty regex at web/__tests__/app/pricing-page.test.tsx:346-350 ("the tutor takes no model parameter and no tier is consulted").

**Impact.** pricing.ts is the file CLAUDE.md rule 8 names as the source of truth for published rates, so this docblock is the first thing a future editor reads before changing a price. It tells them the tutor has no model parameter and no wallet, which would lead them to conclude the tier/model machinery does not exist and reintroduce exactly the divergence the roster was built to prevent. No test pins prose in this file — pricing.test.ts:128-132 checks only that each rate is a positive number.

**Evidence.** web/src/lib/pricing.ts:164-168 "`lambda/tutor/index.mjs` binds one `process.env.TUTOR_MODEL_ID` (the deployed profile resolves to Claude Haiku 4.5) and `<AskTutor />` posts only `{slug, question}` — there is no model parameter, no tier lookup, and no wallet call anywhere on the tutor path." Contradicted by web/src/components/ask-tutor.tsx:260 `metered ? { slug, question: q, model, meta: true } : { slug, question: q }` and lambda/tutor/index.mjs:458 `export function buildWallet(...)`.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, observed live.** The drift is real: web/src/lib/pricing.ts:163-168 still describes the Bedrock-era tutor, and that block was last touched in 6c8ab06 (Aug 3) while the migration + roster landed in dd7abb7 (Aug 17) — `grep -rn TUTOR_MODEL_ID lambda/tutor/` returns only handler-wiring.test.mjs:82-83 asserting the var must NOT appear in index.mjs (model ids now come from MODEL_IDS/ROSTER in tutor-billing.mjs), and ask-tutor.tsx:114 does do a tier lookup (`TUTOR_ROSTER[wallet.tier]`) while index.mjs:458 defines a full wallet. `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-tutor --query "{Env:Environment.Variables,LastModified:LastModified}"` returns `{"SECRET_ID":"quantum-tutor"}`, LastModified 2026-08-18 — so the "deployed profile" sentence is false live as well as in git, and no test pins the prose (web/__tests__/lib/pricing.test.ts:128-132 checks only positivity). Severity is low, not medium: this is a comment with no runtime effect, its operative conclusions ("nothing debits the wallet yet", the deliberate absence of a tier field) are still correct, and the same stale rationale is duplicated at web/__tests__/app/pricing-page.test.tsx:347 where the guard it justifies is still the right guard under R13.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Confirmed, low, read.** All three assertions in the docblock at web/src/lib/pricing.ts:164-168 are false as descriptions of the current code. (1) `TUTOR_MODEL_ID` appears nowhere in lambda/ except lambda/tutor/handler-wiring.test.mjs:82-83, which asserts its ABSENCE ("Bedrock-era leftover — model ids come from MODEL_IDS now"); lambda/tutor/index.mjs:293/351 selects from `MODEL_IDS` per request, and "the deployed profile" was the retired Bedrock inference profile. (2) web/src/components/ask-tutor.tsx:258-260 posts `{slug, question, model, meta: true}` whenever `metered` (wallet + authToken), with a tier lookup at line 114 (`TUTOR_ROSTER[wallet.tier]`). (3) lambda/tutor/index.mjs:458 `buildWallet()` is a full wallet with `readTier`/`debit`/`credit`. Nothing in web/__tests__/lib/pricing.test.ts pins prose — lines 128-132 assert only `typicalCreditsPerQuestion > 0`, as the finder said.

#### `fd41306fa` policy.json is still an un-migrated Bedrock IAM policy with the production account id hardcoded, and the tutor README documents deploying with it

`lambda/tutor/policy.json:5-13`

**low** · Partly true · rule-violation · observed live · fix plan 08

**Claim.** The raw-CLI deploy path the README presents as a peer option (`--policy-document file://lambda/tutor/policy.json`) attaches a policy that grants `bedrock:InvokeModelWithResponseStream` on an application-inference-profile ARN and three haiku foundation models — and grants NO `secretsmanager:GetSecretValue` and no DynamoDB. A role built from it cannot read the Anthropic key at all. It also publishes AWS account `$AWS_ACCOUNT` in a public repo; the prior audit's finding 50 ("public repo publishes the AWS organization topology and account IDs") is marked RESOLVED 2026-08-16, and this file (unchanged since 18 Jun) falsifies that resolution.

> **Correction.** Two of the three claims do not survive. (1) "Real standing privilege on any role that still carries it" — no role carries it: `quantum-tutor-role` does not exist in account $AWS_ACCOUNT (NoSuchEntity), and the deployed SAM role's two inline policies contain no `bedrock:` action at all. (2) The account-id claim misidentifies the prior item: it is finding #10 of the security section in docs/codebase-audit-2026-08-16.md:517, not "finding 50", its verdict was **Partly true**, and its own verifier concluded AWS account IDs are identifiers rather than credentials — "disclosure hygiene worth a scrub, with no exploitable step." That scrub landed where it was scoped (docs/account-migration-runbook.md:5-7 now reads `$SRC_ACCOUNT`/`$DST_ACCOUNT`); `git grep -l $AWS_ACCOUNT` over tracked files returns just two hits, policy.json and scripts/founding-credit/cohort-2026-08.json:9, where `expectedAccountId` is a deliberate wrong-account guard. policy.json is a leftover of that scrub, not a falsification of it. Finally, README.md:95 heads the section "Deploy (raw CLI, fallback)" and line 34 marks SAM "(recommended)" — a documented fallback, not the "peer option" the finding describes.

> **Correction.** The account-ID half is refuted by the very document the finder cites. docs/codebase-audit-2026-08-16.md:76-79, in "Resolved while preparing this document," states: "Two literals were deliberately left in place: the `expectedAccountId` in `scripts/founding-credit/cohort-2026-08.json` is a spend guard ... and `lambda/tutor/policy.json` is a live IAM document." The resolution names this exact file as a deliberate exception, so policy.json does not falsify it — and that finding's own verifier concluded AWS account IDs are identifiers, not secrets. The finder's "real standing privilege on any role that still carries it" is also empty: `quantum-tutor-role` does not exist (NoSuchEntity). What is genuinely stale is the audit's rationale — policy.json is no longer "a live IAM document," it is a dead Bedrock one — which is a reason to retire the file (already logged as done-but-undone at docs/feature-optimization.md:1027), not a public-repo disclosure finding.

**Impact.** Anyone following the documented fallback stands up a tutor whose every request fails at the first model call with AccessDenied, surfaced only as the in-band error sentinel inside a committed HTTP 200. The dead Bedrock grant is real standing privilege on any role that still carries it. template.test.mjs:172 asserts `!/bedrock:/` against the TutorFunction block of template.yaml ONLY, so no test in the repo can see this file.

**Evidence.** policy.json:7-12 `"Action": ["bedrock:InvokeModelWithResponseStream"], "Resource": ["$ARN", ...]`. lambda/tutor/README.md:102-103 `aws iam put-role-policy --role-name quantum-tutor-role --policy-name tutor --policy-document file://lambda/tutor/policy.json`, and README.md:34 lists it as a supported path ("`trust.json` / `policy.json` — for the raw AWS CLI path").

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, observed live.** The file is exactly as quoted: lambda/tutor/policy.json:5-13 grants only `bedrock:InvokeModelWithResponseStream` plus logs, with no Secrets Manager statement, and `git log -1 -- lambda/tutor/policy.json` shows it untouched since 566c370 (2026-06-18) — while README.md:34 and 100-103 still feed it to `put-role-policy`, and the create-function four lines later sets `SECRET_ID=quantum-tutor`, which index.mjs:548-549 reads with that same execution role on first model call and index.mjs:421-431 converts into the in-band `TUTOR_ERROR_SENTINEL` inside an already-committed 200 (index.mjs:580). The no-test claim also holds: `git grep -n "policy\.json"` returns only docs and the README, and template.test.mjs:172's `!/bedrock:/` reads the template.yaml TutorFunction block only. But the live account refutes the exposure half — `aws iam get-role --role-name quantum-tutor-role` returns NoSuchEntity, and the function's actual role (`aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-tutor --region us-east-2 --query Role` → `quantum-tutor-TutorFunctionRole-7jw2v0kn4pDq`) carries exactly two inline policies (`aws iam get-role-policy` for both): secretsmanager scoped to `quantum-tutor-??????` and DynamoDB on `quantum-tutor-metering-disabled`, with zero bedrock. So this is dead documentation rot on a fallback nobody has ever run — already logged and mis-marked done at docs/feature-optimization.md:1027 — not a live grant or a live secret.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, observed live.** The un-migrated half is real: lambda/tutor/policy.json:7-13 still grants only `bedrock:InvokeModelWithResponseStream` plus logs, index.mjs:548-550 reads the Anthropic key via `GetSecretValueCommand`, and template.yaml:163-165 has the `secretsmanager:GetSecretValue` grant that policy.json lacks — so the README's raw-CLI fallback (README.md:97-107, listed at :34) does build a role that cannot fetch the key, and no test touches the file (`grep -rn "policy.json"` across the repo's .mjs/.ts/.js/.py/Makefile returns nothing; template.test.mjs:172 scopes `!/bedrock:/` to `body("TutorFunction")`). But the two aggravating claims do not survive: `aws iam get-role --role-name quantum-tutor-role` returns NoSuchEntity and `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-tutor --region us-east-2` shows the live function on the SAM-created `quantum-tutor-TutorFunctionRole-7jw2v0kn4pDq` with `SECRET_ID=quantum-tutor`, so no role anywhere carries this Bedrock grant — it is standing privilege on nothing. That leaves a broken, unused, explicitly-labeled fallback in a public README, which is a docs-hygiene defect, not an exposure.

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*

#### `fb40af359` The pricing page charges 1 credit per Haiku question; the server charges zero for Haiku on every tier, including Pro

`lambda/tutor/index.mjs:293-309`

**low** · Partly true · consistency · ran locally · fix plan 04

#### `f07ce0f4d` The usage merge overwrites the input legs with nulls, pricing a generation at output-only, and readUsage's `?? 0` keeps the migration alarm silent

`lambda/tutor/index.mjs:401-405`

**low** · Partly true · correctness · ran locally · fix plan 07

**Claim.** `usage = { ...usage, ...ev.usage }` on `message_delta` is a REPLACE, not the merge the comment above it demands. The SDK's own `MessageDeltaUsage` type declares `input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens` as `number | null`, and the SDK's own accumulator null-guards every one of them before assigning. This handler does not. A `message_delta` carrying `input_tokens: null` erases the `message_start` input legs; `readUsage` then coerces `null ?? 0` to a finite 0 (tutor-billing.mjs:241-244), so it returns a VALID report and `USAGE_SHAPE_UNRECOGNIZED` — the alarm built for exactly this class of provider-shape change — never fires.

> **Correction.** The finder presents the null delta as what the API sends; it is not an observed first-party shape. The documented cumulative delta carries the same numbers `message_start` did, so the spread is harmless (and under server-tool turns, more correct). The one public report of literal nulls (litellm #11920) traces to the Python SDK's `None` field defaults being re-serialized by an intermediary proxy — this handler constructs `new Anthropic({ apiKey })` with no baseURL (index.mjs:550) and talks to the API directly. "A REPLACE, not the merge the comment demands" is also imprecise: `{ ...usage, ...ev.usage }` is a shallow merge that only clobbers keys present in the event. Treat this as latent hardening (mirror the SDK's `!= null` guard), not a charge that collapses today.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the framing overreach. (1) The finder shows a type declaration and an SDK guard, not an observed payload: the documented `message_delta` SSE frame carries `usage:{"output_tokens":12}` with the input keys ABSENT (Anthropic streaming docs and the SDK's own examples), and against that shape `{...usage, ...ev.usage}` is a correct merge — the SDK's `!= null` guard also covers the absent case, so its existence is not proof the service emits explicit nulls. This is a latent robustness gap against a legal-but-unobserved shape, not a defect firing today. (2) "The expensive half of every metered generation bills at zero" does not survive arithmetic against the real `RATES`. Running `creditsForUsage` on a 5,529-token cached prefix + 400 output tokens: on a cache-READ request (the steady state caching is designed to produce) the zeroed input legs are only ~20% of true cost and the round-up to whole credits often erases the difference entirely — haiku-4-5 and sonnet-5 charge the SAME 1 credit either way, opus-5 drops 2 to 1. Only the first question of a cache window (the cache-WRITE leg, at 1.25x) loses the expensive half: sonnet-5 3 to 1, opus-5 5 to 1.

**Impact.** On an input-dominated workload with a ~5,500-token cached prefix, the charge collapses to the output leg alone: the expensive half of every metered generation bills at zero, silently, with HTTP 200, a flat Errors metric, and quantum-tutor-usage-shape-unrecognized at zero. Metering is deliberately OFF today, so no money moves yet — but this is the precise failure mode the migration alarm exists for, and it evades it.

**Evidence.** index.mjs:403-405 `} else if (ev?.type === "message_delta" && ev.usage) { usage = { ...usage, ...ev.usage }; }` — versus node_modules/@anthropic-ai/sdk/lib/MessageStream.js: `// Update other usage fields if they exist in the event` / `if (event.usage.input_tokens != null) { snapshot.usage.input_tokens = event.usage.input_tokens; }` (and the same guard for both cache fields). The test cannot see it: index.test.mjs:309 yields `{ type: "message_delta", usage: { output_tokens } }` — the key is absent, never null, so the fake encodes an assumption the real SDK explicitly defends against.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The missing null-guard is real: index.mjs:403-405 spreads `ev.usage` unconditionally while node_modules/@anthropic-ai/sdk/lib/MessageStream.js:445-453 guards each of `input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens` with `!= null`, and messages.d.ts:319-334 declares all three as `number | null`. But the failure only materializes on a wire shape nobody has shown the first-party API emitting. I ran `node -e` in lambda/tutor against the real `readUsage` over the two `message_delta` shapes the current docs actually show (fetched https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/streaming): `usage:{"output_tokens":15}` (keys ABSENT, so the merge preserves the input legs) and the cumulative form `usage:{"input_tokens":10682,"cache_creation_input_tokens":0,"cache_read_input_tokens":0,"output_tokens":510,...}` — both price to `{input:14,output:400,cacheRead:5529,cacheWrite:0}`; only a hand-written explicit-null delta collapses to `{input:0,...,cacheRead:0}`. Reachability is also narrow: `settle()` early-returns at index.mjs:212 unless `reserved > 0`, which only the metered branch sets (index.mjs:320), and metering is off in the deployed function — and this Anthropic path is not deployed at all yet.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The mechanism is real and I reproduced it: `node scratchpad/probe.mjs` (imports the real `lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs`) replays the handler's two-event merge from index.mjs:401-404 with a `message_delta` carrying `input_tokens: null`, and the merged object comes back `{input_tokens: null, output_tokens: 400, cache_read_input_tokens: null, ...}`; `readUsage` (tutor-billing.mjs:239-247) then returns a VALID report `{input: 0, output: 400, cacheRead: 0, cacheWrite: 0}` because `null ?? 0` is finite, so `USAGE_SHAPE_UNRECOGNIZED` at index.mjs:222-225 never fires. The nullable contract is genuine — `node_modules/@anthropic-ai/sdk/resources/messages/messages.d.ts:319-340` declares all three input legs `number | null`, and `lib/MessageStream.js:443-456` null-guards each one — and no test anywhere covers it (`grep -rn "_tokens: null" lambda/tutor/*.test.mjs` returns nothing). I am lowering severity to low: this is dormant (metering is the pair wallet+factor at index.mjs:191-195, and the live tutor has `WalletTableName ""` and no `RATE_CARD`), the error direction is undercharging (rule 7's stated safe direction), and the trigger shape is permitted by the type but not what the wire is documented to carry.

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*
