The metered branch is unreachable code today. (2) The rule-13 copy decision is NOT inverted. Rule 13 asks what the *deployed* system can do; the live function carries no `WALLET_TABLE` and no `USER_POOL_ID`, so index.mjs:296-298 refuses every non-free model with `METERING_UNAVAILABLE_MESSAGE`. Dropping the Tier column remains correct — only the mechanism the comment cites went stale, not the conclusion. (3) The "hidden gap" is not hidden: `ROSTER.free = ["haiku-4-5"]` and the missing trial counter are verbatim open work in CLAUDE.md ("Gate `<AskTutor />` on tier; add the free-trial question counter; drop `free` from the tutor `ROSTER`").

> **Correction.** Two parts of the finder's reasoning are wrong. (1) Misattribution: `git blame -L 162,168 web/src/lib/pricing.ts` dates the block to fc4d20b (2026-07-26), and `git log -S'{ slug, question: q, model, meta: true }' -- web/src/components/ask-tutor.tsx` shows the model parameter, tier lookup and wallet call all landed in 4504100 / PR #189 on 2026-07-27 — the day after. The 2026-08-17 Anthropic migration (4b027bd) only invalidated the `TUTOR_MODEL_ID` sentence; the other two sentences had already been false for three weeks. (2) The copy decision is not "inverted." Rule 13 governs what the *deployed* system can do, and `lambda/tutor/template.yaml:75-87` defaults `WalletTableName` and `UserPoolId` to `""`, so `metering` is undefined and `index.mjs:288` refuses every paid-model request with `METERING_UNAVAILABLE_MESSAGE`. Dropping the Tier column and barring `/unlock|desbloquea/` remains the right rule-13 call; only the stated reason is stale. Finally, the "hidden real gap" is not hidden — CLAUDE.md's open-work list already says verbatim "Gate `<AskTutor />` on tier; add the free-trial question counter; drop `free` from the tutor `ROSTER`."

**Impact.** That paragraph is not decoration — pricing-page-content.tsx repeats it verbatim to justify deleting the 'Tier' column from the published tutor rate table ('an unlock nothing in the codebase performs (the tutor lambda binds one model id and takes no model parameter)'). The justification is now inverted: the roster, the entitlement check and the per-model refusal copy all exist. The next person to reason about whether the tier column may return will read a comment that describes the pre-migration system, and the copy decision it anchors is a rule-13 surface. Separately it hides the real gap: TUTOR_ROSTER.free is still ['haiku-4-5'] (tutor-billing.generated.ts:39) and no free-trial counter exists anywhere in web/src, so rule 2's 10-lifetime-question cap has no client-side existence at all.

**Evidence.** pricing.ts:164-166 `lambda/tutor/index.mjs` binds one `process.env.TUTOR_MODEL_ID` ... and `<AskTutor />` posts only `{slug, question}` — there is no model parameter, no tier lookup, and no wallet call anywhere on the tutor path.` Contradicted by lambda/tutor/index.mjs:199 `let model = "haiku-4-5";` with the refusal strings at :67-73, and by ask-tutor.tsx:262 `metered ? { slug, question: q, model, meta: true } : { slug, question: q }` plus the tier lookup at ask-tutor.tsx:114 `const models = wallet ? TUTOR_ROSTER[wallet.tier] ?? TUTOR_ROSTER.free : null;`.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, observed live.** The drift is real but narrower than framed. `grep -rn TUTOR_MODEL_ID . --exclude-dir=node_modules --exclude-dir=.git` returns zero hits in `lambda/` (only pricing.ts:165, a comment in web/__tests__/app/pricing-page.test.tsx:347, and untracked `.claude/worktrees/` copies), and `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-tutor` returns `Environment.Variables = {"SECRET_ID":"quantum-tutor"}`, LastModified 2026-08-18 — so "binds one `process.env.TUTOR_MODEL_ID`" is false of git *and* of production. "No model parameter / no tier lookup" is likewise false as source (index.mjs:290 `body.model`, :305 `metering.wallet.readTier(sub)`; ask-tutor.tsx:114,262). This is a stale comment plus a stale test comment; no rendered copy is wrong and no money path is touched, hence low.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** All three factual claims in the doc block are stale, verified in source: `grep -rn "TUTOR_MODEL_ID" . --exclude-dir=node_modules --exclude-dir=.git` returns hits only in `web/src/lib/pricing.ts:165`, the `why`-comment at `web/__tests__/app/pricing-page.test.tsx:347`, and untracked `.claude/worktrees/` scratch — the tracked `lambda/tutor/index.mjs` binds no such env var; `web/src/components/ask-tutor.tsx:262` posts `{ slug, question: q, model, meta: true }` when metered and does a tier lookup at :114; and `lambda/tutor/index.mjs:290-310` reads `body.model`, calls `metering.wallet.readTier(sub)` and `isAllowed(tier, requested)`. So the docs-drift defect stands. But it is a JSDoc comment and a test rationale string — nothing renders it, no behavior depends on it, and the rule-13 conclusion it anchors is still correct, which is why I rate this low rather than medium.

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*

#### `fc25d9412` src/app contains no error.tsx, global-error.tsx or not-found.tsx, and the code relies on that absence being known rather than fixed

`web/src/components/review-dashboard.tsx:106`

**low** · Partly true · correctness · observed live · fix plan 11

**Claim.** A recursive search of web/src/app for error.tsx, global-error.tsx, not-found.tsx and loading.tsx returns nothing. The only error boundary in the entire tree is WidgetErrorBoundary at widget-fence.tsx:137, which covers lesson fences only.

> **Correction.** Two of the finder's supporting claims are wrong. (1) There is no "blank page": Next 16 ships DefaultGlobalError as the automatic root boundary, verified present in the committed build at web/out/_next/static/chunks/03edqrb4zdj~g.js. The real defect is narrower — the /review throw escalates to the *root*, replacing the whole document and losing site chrome and locale, where the same widget inside a lesson fence is contained to one card. (2) "notFound() ... with no not-found.tsx to render" is doubly wrong: Next has `builtin/not-found.js`, `web/out/404.html` already exists carrying the standard 404 UI, and under `output: "export"` all three callers use generateStaticParams (glossary and founding-ten additionally set `dynamicParams = false`), so notFound() is a build-time guard — at runtime the host serves 404.html and the code path never executes. Also note the trigger the finder quotes from the source comment (a corrupt `kind` resolving through the prototype chain) is already defended at review-dashboard.tsx:93-94 by `Object.hasOwn`; the comment documents a guard that works, not a live bug.

> **Correction.** Three parts of the reasoning do not hold. (1) "Blank page with no message and no recovery affordance" is false — Next 16.2.6 ships a built-in default global-error boundary (`node_modules/next/dist/client/components/builtin/global-error.js`) rendering "This page couldn't load" plus Reload and Back buttons. (2) "notFound() is called ... with no not-found.tsx to render" is false — Next's built-in not-found renders, the export already emits `web/out/404.html` and `web/out/_not-found.html` themed with the app's root layout and noindexed, and in a static export `notFound()` is unreachable at runtime because `generateStaticParams` enumerates exactly the valid slugs. (3) "prior audit findings #42 and #224" misattributes #42, which is a MERGED PR titled "refactor(vqe): use the shared BlochDial for the ansatz indicator", not an audit finding. What survives is the narrower, already-tracked #224: the /review route's lazy widgets lack the boundary their lesson-side twins have.

**Impact.** Any throw from a client component outside a lesson fence unmounts the whole App Router subtree to a blank page with no message and no recovery affordance. /review is the documented example — it mounts the same six lazy widgets as lessons through LIVE_WIDGETS but renders them bare at review-dashboard.tsx:271 with no boundary, so a stale content-hashed chunk after a deploy (the file's own stated realistic trigger) blanks the route. notFound() is called from learn/[section]/page.tsx:46 and glossary/[term]/page.tsx with no not-found.tsx to render. This is prior audit findings #42 and #224 (issue #224 OPEN); confirmed still present today by direct search, and the review-dashboard source comment at :106 openly cites 'src/app carries no error boundary' as a live constraint it must code around.

**Evidence.** `find web/src/app -name 'error.tsx' -o -name 'not-found.tsx' -o -name 'global-error.tsx'` returns no results. review-dashboard.tsx:104-106 `...throwing "Objects are not valid as a React child" and taking out the whole /review route (src/app carries no error boundary).` review-dashboard.tsx:271 `<Live source={content.source!} surface="review" />` — no WidgetErrorBoundary wrapper, unlike widget-fence.tsx:205.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The structural half checks out: `find web/src/app \( -name 'error.tsx' -o -name 'not-found.tsx' -o -name 'global-error.tsx' -o -name 'loading.tsx' \)` returns nothing, `grep -rn "getDerivedStateFromError" web/src` finds only widget-fence.tsx:137, and review-dashboard.tsx:271 does render `<Live source={...} surface="review" />` bare while widget-fence.tsx:205 wraps its identical widget in WidgetErrorBoundary. But the stated impact does not reproduce. Next 16.2.6 (package.json) supplies a built-in root fallback — `node_modules/next/dist/build/webpack/loaders/next-app-loader/index.js:104,128` sets `let globalError = defaultGlobalErrorPath` unconditionally, and `grep -o "This page couldn[^\"]*" web/out/_next/static/chunks/03edqrb4zdj~g.js` confirms the *already-built* static export ships that UI: "This page couldn't load" / "Reload to try again, or go back." plus `children:"Reload"` and `children:"Back"` buttons. So the outcome is an unbranded English-only error card with two recovery affordances, not "a blank page with no message and no recovery affordance."

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, observed live.** The file-absence half is true — `find web/src/app -type f \( -name error.tsx -o -name global-error.tsx -o -name not-found.tsx -o -name loading.tsx \)` returns nothing, and `web/src/components/review-dashboard.tsx:271` does render `<Live source={content.source!} surface="review" />` bare while `web/src/components/quantum/widget-fence.tsx:205` wraps its equivalent in `WidgetErrorBoundary`. But that exact defect is already filed and open as issue #224 (`gh issue view 224 --json state` → OPEN), whose own verification note says "Next still renders its built-in production error fallback rather than a blank page" and explicitly scopes the missing `error.tsx` out as "a separate decision, not part of this fix"; the identical broader claim was already adjudicated in `docs/codebase-audit-2026-08-16.md:463-466`. I read `web/node_modules/next/dist/client/components/builtin/global-error.js` (Next 16.2.6): it renders a styled card reading "This page couldn't load" / "Reload to try again, or go back." with **Reload** and **Back** buttons, so the stated impact of "a blank page with no message and no recovery affordance" is factually wrong. `ls web/out/404.html web/out/_not-found.html` shows both already ship (24k each, inside the app's own root layout, `name="robots" content="noindex"`), and under `output: "export"` with `generateStaticParams` enumerating all 7 sections and 273 glossary terms there is no runtime server for `notFound()` at `learn/[section]/page.tsx:46` to fire on at all.

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*

#### `f65b9f6f4` The nav due-count badge runs a full localStorage scan plus a JSON.parse per card inside getSnapshot, on every page of the site

`web/src/components/review-nav-badge.tsx:16`

**low** · Partly true · performance · ran locally · fix plan 11

**Claim.** dueCount() walks every key in localStorage via ownedLocalKeys(), then re-reads and JSON.parses each qc:card: record. React invokes getSnapshot on every render of the component and again after every store notification, and ReviewNavBadge is rendered twice in the root Nav, which is in the root layout.

> **Correction.** The finder states "the use-workspace multiplication is not recorded anywhere." It is: docs/codebase-audit-2026-08-16.md:417 already says verbatim "`use-workspace.ts:24-39` compounds it: one snapshot call runs `getAllCardStates()`, `dueCount()` and `getAllMeasurements()`, i.e. three more full scans." That prior entry (#8, lines 413-419) is this same finding, already adjudicated **Partly true / low**, and docs/feature-optimization.md:929 has tracked it OPEN since 2026-06-17. Two further framing errors: `useWorkspace` is imported only by src/app/workspace/page.tsx, so its four scans are scoped to one route, not "every route in the app"; and the badge's number-valued snapshot is a deliberate guard (documented at review-nav-badge.tsx:9-10) that keeps React's identity check stable, so there is no re-render loop — this is memoization advice, not a defect.

> **Correction.** Three parts of the framing are wrong. (1) This is not a new observation and the novelty claim is false: it is a near-verbatim resubmission of finding **#8** (not #38) in docs/codebase-audit-2026-08-16.md:414-419, already adjudicated **Partly true / low** with no tracked issue — and line 417 of that same finding explicitly names `use-workspace.ts:24-39` and its extra scans, which the new claim asserts "is not recorded anywhere." (The only genuine refinement is the count: use-workspace runs four scans, not the three previously recorded, because `activeDays()` walks the bucket too.) (2) "Two full storage scans plus 2N parses" overstates the scan half — `ownedLocalKeys` parses nothing; it is `localStorage.key(i)` plus a `startsWith` prefix test, so the "hundreds of qc: keys across content, solved flags, circuits, day flags" the impact leans on cost a string compare each, not a parse. (3) N is bounded by the curriculum at ~102 card-producing fences, so "hundreds" of parsed card records is not reachable today.

**Impact.** Two full storage scans plus 2N parses per render pass on every route in the app, including the public funnel. use-workspace.ts:24-39 compounds it: one getSnapshot call does getAllCardStates() (scan + N parses), activeDays() (scan), dueCount() (scan + N parses) and getAllMeasurements() (scan + M parses) — four scans and 2N+M parses, on the main thread, synchronously during render. localStorage is a synchronous blocking API, so for an established learner (hundreds of qc: keys across cards, content, solved flags, circuits, day flags and measurements) this lands directly on INP. Prior finding #38 raised the nav badge and is tracked OPEN as part of that audit; the use-workspace multiplication is not recorded anywhere.

**Evidence.** review-nav-badge.tsx:16 `const count = useSyncExternalStore(subscribe, () => dueCount(), () => 0);`, reached from nav.tsx:95 and nav.tsx:113 (two instances). review-store.ts:170-176 `dueCardIds` filters `getAllCardIds()` calling `getCardState(id)` per id; getAllCardIds walks `ownedLocalKeys()` which loops `for (let i = 0; i < localStorage.length; i++)` (progress-owner.ts:130). use-workspace.ts:39 returns a snapshot built from four such scans.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The call chain reproduces exactly as quoted — review-nav-badge.tsx:16, nav.tsx:95 and :113 (two mounts), review-store.ts `dueCount`→`dueCardIds`→`getAllCardIds`→`ownedLocalKeys` (progress-owner.ts:130) plus a `getItem`+`JSON.parse` per card — but the magnitude and the novelty claims do not hold. I benchmarked the exact walk with an array-backed key index (what Blink's cached storage area uses) at 1,200 keys / 300 cards: `node /private/tmp/.../scratchpad/bench2.mjs` → scan 0.03 ms + 300 getItem/parse 0.14 ms = **0.17 ms per call**, ~0.35 ms for both badge instances; the 3.0 ms I first measured is jsdom's own O(n) `key(i)`, not a browser. N is bounded too — `for k in qchallenge qpredict qblochtarget qcostestimate qdebug qexpect qcard; do grep -rIo -- "\`\`\`$k" --include="*.ipynb" 0*/ | wc -l; done` totals **204** card-bearing fences in the whole curriculum, so a maximal learner sits two orders of magnitude below the 200 ms INP threshold. Nav is also a stateless client component under AuthProvider in the persistent root layout with no `usePathname`, so it does not re-render per navigation — getSnapshot fires on mount and per qc-progress/storage notification, not "on every route."

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The call chain is exactly as quoted: review-nav-badge.tsx:16 passes `() => dueCount()` inline to useSyncExternalStore, nav.tsx:95 and :113 mount the badge twice, Nav is in app/layout.tsx:104, and review-store.ts:170-176 → getAllCardIds() → ownedLocalKeys() (progress-owner.ts:126-142) walks `localStorage.length` then re-reads each `qc:card:` record. But the magnitude claim does not survive measurement or a key count. I built a maximal-learner store (799 `qc:` keys, 102 card states — the ceiling set by the curriculum: `for k in qchallenge qpredict qblochtarget qcostestimate qdebug qexpect qcard; do grep -rho "^\`\`\`$k" 0*/GUIDE.md | wc -l; done` totals 102) and timed the real loop shape against jsdom's localStorage (`node scratchpad/bench.mjs`): dueCount() = 1.42 ms, so the full 2-badges × 2-getSnapshot pass is 5.7 ms — under jsdom, which is markedly slower than Chrome's renderer-cached localStorage, and it fires on route change and on `qc-progress` (grade / section toggle / circuit save), not per interaction frame. That is a memoization cleanup, not an INP-class defect.

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*

## 6. Claims vs capability

8 findings — 3 medium, 5 low. 41 files read.

### How it actually works

Claims about this product live on four surfaces, and only one is guarded. (1) Rendered copy: web/src/i18n/locales/{en,es}.ts supply every tier bullet, FAQ answer and footnote; pricing-page-content.tsx:226 renders the credit grant from TIERS.monthlyCredits while the same number is ALSO typed into en.ts:892 ("1,900 credits every month") and es.ts:820. (2) Next metadata: web/src/app/pricing/page.tsx:14 PAGE_DESCRIPTION, shipped verbatim into meta description, og:description and twitter:description. (3) Repo docs: README.md, PRODUCT.md, docs/pricing-cost-basis.md, docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md, docs/account-migration-runbook.md — all public. (4) Source comments that operators and future agents read as fact.

The guard is web/__tests__/app/pricing-page.test.tsx: 13 UNDELIVERABLE_CLAIMS regexes run over container.textContent in both locales AND over a recursive flatten of the metadata export. It is unusually honest about its own limits (lines 208-260 enumerate what a denylist cannot catch) and it now bars the PRESENCE of /sponsor|patrocin/, the correct inversion of the old test that locked the promise in. web/__tests__/infra/no-commercial-terms.test.ts scans every git-tracked .md/.ts/.mjs/.ipynb for rule-6 disclosure, with teeth tests on both edges.

Three days of commits (#234/#235/#236/#237) moved the tutor off Bedrock to Anthropic's first-party API, landed the RATE_CARD factor (both kernels THROW rather than debit raw cost — qpu-core.mjs:97, tutor/index.mjs:191), and added Stripe sandbox provisioning plus two live-account parity scripts. The docs did not move with them. README.md names Bedrock in four places and still headlines sponsored QPU runs. docs/pricing-cost-basis.md:54 calls prompt caching "not yet shipped" (it ships at tutor/index.mjs:375) and cites MAX_TOKENS (800), an identifier replaced by per-model MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS. docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md:186-197 tells the operator to correct RATES against Bedrock's pricing page and asserts "charging at cost is deliberate," which rules 5/9 reversed.

Most consequentially, the tutor path itself changed under the copy guard: ask-tutor.tsx now posts {slug, question, model, meta}, reads TUTOR_ROSTER[wallet.tier], and renders a per-answer credit trailer. Three of the guard's stated rationales ("the tutor takes no model parameter and no tier is consulted", "ask-tutor.tsx renders no cost, credit, or price") are now falsified by grep — while the bans they justify remain correct, because the DEPLOYED function is still free and unmetered. The guard locks the right outcome for a reason a maintainer can now disprove.

### Done well

- web/__tests__/app/pricing-page.test.tsx:322 inverts the failure that let sponsorship copy outlive its withdrawal: the pattern /\b(sponsor\w*|patrocin\w*)\b/i now BARS the promise in both locales instead of asserting its presence, and it is stem-matched so 'sponsoring'/'patrocinamos' cannot slip a suffix past it. This is the prior audit's finding 8 fixed.
- web/__tests__/app/pricing-page.test.tsx:196-206 extends the scan to the metadata EXPORT via a recursive metadataStrings() flatten, closing the surface where 'one credit wallet METERS the only two things that cost real money' survived four review rounds. Prior audit's blind spot, now covered.
- web/__tests__/infra/wallet-ttl.test.ts:1-40 is the strongest guard in the repo: it does not count occurrences of expiresAt, it walks backwards from each write to the nearest row key and nearest TableName and refuses anything that is not provably the EVENT# marker or the DAY# ledger row — exactly the copy-paste that would arm DynamoDB TTL on a learner's WALLET# row and silently delete their paid balance.
- web/__tests__/infra/rate-card-parity.test.ts:28 pins one LITERAL resolve line that both lambda/tutor/template.yaml:151 and lambda/qpu/template.yaml:196 must carry verbatim, then asserts (line 72) that lambda/qpu/reconcile.mjs carries NO RATE_CARD — so the refund path can never re-derive a price instead of returning the recorded creditsCharged.
- lambda/tutor/index.mjs:191-195 makes rule 5 unbypassable at runtime: `const factorUsable = Number.isFinite(rateFactor) && rateFactor > 0` and a wallet without a usable factor refuses exactly like no wallet at all, logging RATE_CARD_INVALID once per cold start so the misconfiguration is alarmable rather than silently identical to metering-off.
- web/src/components/credentials-wall.tsx:192 `topUpAvailable: b.capMicros === 0` makes CLAUDE.md's claim verifiable: credentialsUi.outOfReachDetail ('remaining sponsored budget') is unreachable for any learner without a stamped allowance, because a zero cap forces tierReachable() true and the medal renders 'locked', not 'out-of-reach'.
- web/src/i18n/locales/en.ts:931 and es.ts:859 both name Anthropic, not AWS Bedrock, as the tutor's downstream processor — the privacy disclosure was corrected in BOTH locales in the same pass as the provider move.
- scripts/stripe/check-catalog-parity.mjs:130 is the only place rule 8 lockstep is actually computed: it parses TIERS out of pricing.ts and fails with 'pricing.ts grants X but CATALOG grants Y (rule 8 lockstep)', and refuses to run at all without --expect-account matching the live account id (line 86).

### Findings

#### `fc5a367e4` README.md still describes the tutor as a Bedrock Lambda in four places and omits lambda/stripe entirely from the architecture it documents

`README.md:31`

**medium** · Confirmed · docs-drift · ran locally · fix plan 01

**Claim.** Line 31 ('the streaming lesson tutor (Bedrock)'), line 327 ('streaming Bedrock Lambda'), line 473 ('grounded in the current lesson via Amazon Bedrock') and line 627 (tech stack lists Bedrock) all describe a provider the tutor left on 2026-08-17. The same three lists say 'Four serverless backends' while lambda/ contains five — qpu, review-email, stripe, sync, tutor — with the entire money path absent from the tree at 326-331 and the table at 471-478.

> **Correction.** Two trims to the framing. The impact line overstates: the README's Bedrock mentions are one-clause descriptors, not instructions to reason about "entitlement, IAM model grants and Cost Explorer attribution" — a reader is misdirected about the provider, not walked into that analysis. And the sponsorship half (lines 7 and 34) is prior finding #212, already tracked OPEN; folding it in here double-counts it. The genuinely new drift is the Bedrock provider (created 2026-08-17 by PR #235, one day old) and the lambda/stripe omission plus the stale "four" count (a month old, since e0a5729 on 2026-07-17).

> **Correction.** Two parts of the framing overreach. (1) Omitting lambda/stripe *understates* capability, which is the R13-safe direction and is arguably consistent with R15 (the storefront is deliberately closed); the real defect there is the numeric "four vs five" inconsistency, not a claims-vs-capability breach. (2) The sponsorship headline at README.md:7 and :34 is prior finding #212, already tracked OPEN — folding it into this card's impact double-counts it. The genuinely NEW drift is the Bedrock half alone. Separately, lambda/tutor/policy.json:7-12 still grants `bedrock:InvokeModelWithResponseStream`, a dead artifact not cited by the finder and not part of the deployed template.

**Impact.** This is the public repo's front page. A reader (or an agent onboarding from it) is told to reason about Bedrock entitlement, IAM model grants and Cost Explorer attribution for a function that now calls api.anthropic.com with a Secrets Manager key, and is given no pointer at all to the Stripe handler that owns every wallet write. Line 7 additionally still headlines 'on a real 20-qubit QPU — sponsored, so a learner never pays' and line 34 'On quantum.altivum.ai the platform pays for QPU runs', both withdrawn 2026-07-28 (LIFETIME_CAP_MICROS = 0 at qpu-core.mjs:47).

**Evidence.** README.md:473 `| [`lambda/tutor/`](lambda/tutor/) | "Ask the margin" — a stateless, response-streaming lesson tutor grounded in the current lesson via Amazon Bedrock |`, versus lambda/tutor/README.md:7 '**Provider, 2026-08-17: this moved off Amazon Bedrock.**' and lambda/tutor/template.yaml:26 'There is no ModelId parameter any more'. The Bedrock half is NEW drift created by PR #235 and is not in the prior audit; the sponsorship headline is prior finding 5, tracked OPEN as #212, and confirmed still present at README.md:7.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, medium, ran locally.** `grep -n -i "bedrock" README.md` returns exactly the four cited lines (31, 327, 473, 627), while `grep -n "@anthropic-ai\|SECRET_ID" lambda/tutor/index.mjs lambda/tutor/template.yaml` shows the tutor now imports `@anthropic-ai/sdk` and pulls its key from Secrets Manager, with no Bedrock SDK in `lambda/tutor/package.json` and no Bedrock IAM in its template — so line 627's "Cloud" stack lists a service the repo no longer calls anywhere (the only non-worktree Bedrock hits outside `lambda/tutor` are `web/__tests__/app/privacy-page.test.tsx:48`, which *asserts* Bedrock is absent from the UI). `ls -1 lambda/` returns five directories and `git ls-files lambda/stripe` returns 9 tracked files (added 2026-07-17, e0a5729), yet `grep -c -i "stripe" README.md` returns **0** and the count "four" appears in three places (31, 192, 469) with the tree at 326-331 and the table at 471-478 both listing only tutor/sync/qpu/review-email. Line 7's sponsorship headline and line 34's "the platform pays for QPU runs" are present as quoted, against `LIFETIME_CAP_MICROS = 0` at lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:47. No test guards README content, so nothing catches this.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Confirmed, medium, read.** Every cited line verified verbatim: README.md:31, :327, :473 and :627 all still name Bedrock, while lambda/tutor/index.mjs:385 calls `client.messages.create()` on the Anthropic SDK with the key read from Secrets Manager at :549, and `grep -n "bedrock:" lambda/tutor/template.yaml` returns nothing — lambda/tutor/template.test.mjs:172 even asserts `!/bedrock:/.test(fn)`, so a committed test already encodes the opposite of the README. The count is also wrong: `ls lambda/` returns five dirs (qpu, review-email, stripe, sync, tutor) against "four" at README.md:31, :192 and :469, and `grep -n "stripe\|Stripe" README.md` returns zero hits, so the entire money path is missing from the tree at 326-331 and the table at 471-478. `git log -1 -- README.md` returns 2ef2f61, which predates both the Stripe work and PR #235, confirming the drift rather than a deliberate edit. Medium, not high: this is front-page documentation on a public repo — actively misleading about provider, IAM and cost attribution — but nothing is broken or exposed at runtime.

*Raised independently by 1 other reader(s) on a different dimension: `f2d21e271` (The tutor after Bedrock).*

#### `f6ba1ef91` The pricing page's meta description and five shipped UI strings still promise 'at cost, no markup' — the mechanism that landed 2026-08-17 throws rather than charge at cost

`web/src/app/pricing/page.tsx:14`

**medium** · Confirmed · rule-violation · observed live · fix plan 01

**Claim.** PAGE_DESCRIPTION ends '...billed at cost with no markup.' and is shipped verbatim into meta description, og:description and twitter:description. Five more instances render in the app: qpu-submit-panel.tsx:305 ('You pay for these runs at cost. We add nothing on top.'), :313 ('with no markup'), :348, :355, :786, and hardware-panel.tsx:119 ('billed to your credits at cost, no markup').

> **Correction.** Two reproduction details the finder's framing glosses. (1) The live meta description is served only at `/pricing` — `https://quantum.altivum.ai/pricing/` (trailing slash) falls back to the home document (byte-identical to `/`, title "Quantum Learner"), so a maintainer spot-checking with a slash will wrongly conclude the finding is stale. On the real `/pricing` document all six "at cost"/"no markup" hits are metadata (three tags plus the RSC flight payload); the rendered pricing body contains neither phrase. (2) The five UI strings are not unconditionally rendered: the four in qpu-submit-panel sit behind `qpuOn = authed && isQpuConfigured()` (workspace/page.tsx:102) and hardware-panel:119 behind the same `isQpuConfigured()`. That gate is satisfied on the live build — the deployed bundle inlines both `NEXT_PUBLIC_QPU_URL` (https://$API_HOST) and a Cognito pool ($USER_POOL_ID), verified by downloading the 15 workspace chunks and grepping them — so the copy does reach real users, but only signed-in ones, not anonymous visitors.

> **Correction.** Three details in the finder's framing are off, none fatal. The component paths are web/src/components/quantum/qpu-submit-panel.tsx and web/src/components/playground/hardware-panel.tsx, not components/qpu/ (line numbers are correct). The throw is qpu-core.mjs:98, not :97, and it fires only when the factor is absent or non-positive — a deployed factor of exactly 1.0 would still price at cost, so the precise statement is that the kernel refuses to DEFAULT to raw cost, which is how CLAUDE.md itself puts it. And only four of the five UI instances render today: line 786 is gated on `budget.walletCredits != null && !walletCanFund(budget)`, so it is dormant while metering is off. Conversely the finder understated the problem: the phrases are not merely unguarded, they are test-locked in place, so deleting them fails the suite.

**Impact.** Rule 5 says every metered surface debits at a shared markup and rule 9 supersedes the at-cost QPU decision; qpu-core.mjs:97 now THROWS RATE_FACTOR_REQUIRED rather than price at raw cost. The public claim is not merely unshipped, it is the opposite of what the code is built to do — and it is the sentence Google and every share card quote today.

**Evidence.** pricing/page.tsx:14 `"...One dollar-pegged credit wallet will meter AI tutoring and real quantum hardware, billed at cost with no markup."`. NEW since the prior audit: scripts/stripe/check-catalog-parity.mjs:104 now defines `const RETIRED_CLAIMS = [/\bno markup\b/i, /\bat cost\b/i, /\badd(?:s|ing)? nothing on top\b/i]` with the comment 'Retired commercial framing... they must not survive on a customer-facing Stripe product either' — the exact regex exists in the repo and is pointed only at Stripe product descriptions, never at the repo's own copy. None of the 13 UNDELIVERABLE_CLAIMS patterns in pricing-page.test.tsx matches 'at cost' or 'no markup'. Prior audit findings 1 and 2; tracked OPEN as #209 and #210; confirmed present today by reading both files.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, medium, observed live.** All six source citations are exact (web/src/app/pricing/page.tsx:14; qpu-submit-panel.tsx:305,313,348,355,786; hardware-panel.tsx:119), and the sentence is live and public right now: `curl -sL https://quantum.altivum.ai/pricing | grep -oiE '<meta name="description"[^>]*>|<meta property="og:description"[^>]*>|<meta name="twitter:description"[^>]*>'` returns all three tags ending "...billed at cost with no markup." The claim is contradicted by the code, not merely unshipped — lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:97 `creditsForMicros(micros, factor)` throws `TypeError(RATE_FACTOR_REQUIRED)` unless a deployed factor that "converts true AWS cost into the charged credit price" is injected, so the kernel is built to refuse rather than price at cost (rules 5/9). No guard covers it: `grep -niE "markup|at cost|nothing on top" web/__tests__/app/pricing-page.test.tsx` returns zero pattern hits across the 13 UNDELIVERABLE_CLAIMS, and the `RETIRED_CLAIMS` regex at scripts/stripe/check-catalog-parity.mjs:104 is applied only to Stripe product descriptions. Held at medium, not high: metering is off and the storefront is closed, so no learner is mischarged today — the damage is a false public promise on the sentence Google and every share card quote.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Confirmed, medium, ran locally.** Confirmed at web/src/app/pricing/page.tsx:14 — PAGE_DESCRIPTION ends "billed at cost with no markup" and feeds metadata/og/twitter descriptions (lines 17-27). It is false against the page's OWN table: web/src/lib/pricing.ts:85 publishes IQM Garnet at 0.163 credits/shot while lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:62 sets true cost IQM_PER_SHOT_MICROS = 1_450 (0.145 credits), and PRODUCT.md:144 already orders the QPU panel's "We add nothing on top." retired — so this is undone work, not intended behaviour. No guard catches it: `grep -niE "markup|at cost" web/__tests__/app/pricing-page.test.tsx` returns only comments, and the metadata-export test at :479 reuses the same 13 UNDELIVERABLE_CLAIMS patterns, none matching, while scripts/stripe/check-catalog-parity.mjs:103 aims the exact regexes only at Stripe products. Ran `cd web && npx jest __tests__/components/quantum/qpu-submit-panel.test.tsx __tests__/app/pricing-page.test.tsx` — 106 passed, proving the guard misses it AND that qpu-submit-panel.test.tsx:195,214,218,1413 actively assert the phrases are PRESENT (with a comment at :216 claiming the at-cost promise "must survive the switch"), the same inverted-guard failure mode that let the sponsorship copy outlive its withdrawal.

#### `f95b218a8` The billing cutover runbook's tutor section is written against Bedrock: it orders RATES corrected to Bedrock prices, passes template parameters that no longer exist, and its 'metering is on' check returns a false positive

`docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md:186-197`

**medium** · Partly true · docs-drift · ran locally · fix plan 08

**Claim.** Section 1.1d says RATES 'currently holds Anthropic first-party list prices as a documented placeholder. Bedrock is partner-priced... Confirm each against https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing/ and correct the table before any real money meters through it. Charging at cost is deliberate (margin is the subscription, not a markup on inference).' Section 2.1 lists 'Current parameters to preserve: ...FoundationModelId=..., ModelId=$ARN' and names exactly three parameters that 'turn metering on' — WalletTableName, UserPoolId, UserPoolClientId — then verifies with 'WALLET_TABLE / USER_POOL_ID / USER_POOL_CLIENT_ID present == metering on'.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the finder's impact framing are not supported. (a) The direction "every debit overcharges" is asserted, not shown — tutor-billing.mjs:56-59 states Bedrock's true Claude cost was never readable from that pricing page at all, so the defect is that the runbook points at the wrong provider and re-asserts a no-markup basis that rules 5/9 and the shipped RATE_CARD mechanism have since superseded, not that it demonstrably inflates the basis. (c) The wallet-without-RateCardSecret state fails SAFE, not open: index.mjs:193 logs the pinned RATE_CARD_INVALID line and lambda/tutor/template.yaml:525 alarms on it (`quantum-tutor-rate-card-invalid`), so the wrong outcome is a confused operator and a dark paid tier, never an incorrect charge. Sub-claim (b) also fails loudly at change-set creation rather than silently.

> **Correction.** The finder's impact (a) overstates the direction of the pricing error: it is not established that Bedrock's rates exceed Anthropic's first-party list (Bedrock's on-demand Claude pricing has historically matched it), and the paid models sonnet-5/opus-5/fable-5 were never invocable on Bedrock for this account at all — so "every debit overcharges, the direct inverse of rule 7" is speculation, not a verified consequence. The real §1.1d defect is that the runbook names the wrong provider as the cost basis and, more seriously, asserts a superseded doctrine — "Charging at cost is deliberate (margin is the subscription, not a markup on inference)" — which #237's shared rate factor and rules 5/9 (every metered surface debits at the same markup over true cost) replaced; index.mjs:186-190 now treats raw cost as itself a rule-5 violation. Also, the `sam deploy` block at :277 carries no `--parameter-overrides`, so the ModelId/FoundationModelId failure fires only when the operator acts on the "parameters to preserve" list rather than on the literal command.

**Impact.** Following it does three wrong things. (a) Rewriting RATES to Bedrock's resale prices raises the cost basis above the tutor's true provider cost, so every debit overcharges — the direct inverse of rule 7. (b) `sam deploy --parameter-overrides ModelId=... FoundationModelId=...` fails: neither parameter exists in lambda/tutor/template.yaml (Parameters are SecretId, AllowedOrigin, MaxConcurrency, FunctionUrlAuthType, LogRetentionInDays, AlertEmail, WalletTableName, UserPoolId, RateCardSecret, UserPoolClientId), and SecretId — the Anthropic key without which the tutor cannot answer at all — is never mentioned. (c) Setting those three parameters WITHOUT RateCardSecret produces exactly the state tutor/index.mjs:191 refuses: every paid-model request returns METERING_UNAVAILABLE_MESSAGE while the runbook's own check reports metering on.

**Evidence.** docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md:195 `table before any real money meters through it. Charging at cost is deliberate`; :279 `` `FoundationModelId=anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0`, ``; :303 `# WALLET_TABLE / USER_POOL_ID / USER_POOL_CLIENT_ID present == metering on`. Contradicted by lambda/tutor/index.mjs:191 `const factorUsable = Number.isFinite(rateFactor) && rateFactor > 0;` and :195 `const metering = wallet && factorUsable ? ... : undefined;`. Line 404 repeats it as a blocker: 'The Bedrock rate table is unverified... **Blocks re-opening the storefront.**' NEW drift, created by PRs #235/#237.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, medium, ran locally.** All three sub-claims reproduce. `sed -n '186,197p;276,305p' docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md` shows §1.1d still ordering RATES "confirmed against https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing/" with "Charging at cost is deliberate", §2.1 listing `FoundationModelId=` / `ModelId=$ARN` as parameters to preserve, and the check `# WALLET_TABLE / USER_POOL_ID / USER_POOL_CLIENT_ID present == metering on`. `awk '/^Parameters:/{f=1;next}/^[A-Za-z]/{f=0}f && /^  [A-Za-z]+:/{print}' lambda/tutor/template.yaml` returns exactly SecretId, AllowedOrigin, MaxConcurrency, FunctionUrlAuthType, LogRetentionInDays, AlertEmail, WalletTableName, UserPoolId, RateCardSecret, UserPoolClientId — no ModelId/FoundationModelId (template.yaml:28 says so outright), and `grep -n "SecretId" docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md` returns nothing, so the Anthropic key parameter is never mentioned. index.mjs:191-195 gates `metering` on `wallet && factorUsable`, and 297 refuses paid models with METERING_UNAVAILABLE_MESSAGE, so the runbook's three-parameter check is a genuine false positive; `git log -1 --date=short -- docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md` dates the file to 2026-08-16 (#233), i.e. before #235/#237, confirming this is new drift.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, medium, read.** All three drift claims hold. (a) docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md:186-197 still calls RATES an "Anthropic first-party list prices as a documented placeholder", orders it verified against aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing, and :404 repeats it as a storefront blocker — but lambda/tutor/tutor-billing.mjs:23-31 records the 2026-08-17 move to Anthropic's first-party API, and CLAUDE.md marks that item done. (b) `grep -n '^  [A-Za-z]*:' lambda/tutor/template.yaml` yields exactly SecretId, AllowedOrigin, MaxConcurrency, FunctionUrlAuthType, LogRetentionInDays, AlertEmail, WalletTableName, UserPoolId, RateCardSecret, UserPoolClientId — no ModelId/FoundationModelId (template.yaml:28 says so explicitly), while runbook:279-280 lists both as "parameters to preserve"; there is no samconfig.toml in lambda/tutor, and `grep -ni secret` on the runbook returns only quantum-stripe and quantum-qpu-edge-secret, so SecretId and the not-yet-created quantum-tutor secret are never mentioned. (c) template.yaml:151 gates RATE_CARD on RateCardSecret and index.mjs:191-195 makes metering the pair (wallet, factor), so index.mjs:297 refuses every paid model with METERING_UNAVAILABLE_MESSAGE under the runbook's three-parameter recipe while runbook:303 reports "metering on". `git log -- docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md` last touched it at 95dc909 (#233), before #235/#237, confirming new drift; nothing in the repo guards this prose.

#### `f6b4b0fa1` docs/pricing-cost-basis.md calls prompt caching 'the highest-leverage unshipped change in the repo' after it shipped, and cites an identifier that no longer exists

`docs/pricing-cost-basis.md:54-56`

**low** · Confirmed · docs-drift · ran locally · fix plan 08

**Claim.** '**Prompt caching is not optional.** ... so a `cachePoint` on the system block cuts cost dramatically. Not yet shipped; it is the highest-leverage unshipped change in the repo.' Prompt caching shipped in PR #206 and is live at lambda/tutor/index.mjs:375. `cachePoint` is Bedrock's parameter name; the code sends Anthropic's `cache_control`. Line 47 states output 'is capped at `MAX_TOKENS` (800)' — that constant was replaced by the per-model MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS map (tutor-billing.mjs:161). #### `f07ed1963` The cutover runbook's Phase 4 acceptance gate names 50.3 credits, a figure neither the client quote nor the server debit can ever produce

`docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md:378`

**low** · Partly true · correctness · ran locally · fix plan 06

**Claim.** Phase 4 step 3: 'submit 100 shots on IQM Garnet. The pre-flight quote and the wallet debit must be the **same number** (50.3 credits). A divergence here is the defect this whole change exists to remove.' 50.3 is the PUBLISHED sheet figure (TASK_FEE_CREDITS 34 + 0.163 x 100). Neither side computes it. The client (web/src/lib/qpu-budget.ts:34) returns Math.ceil(445000/10000) = 45. The server (lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:97) returns Math.ceil(445000 * factor / 10000), an integer for every factor.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the framing overstate it. (1) The step's actual pass condition is "the pre-flight quote and the wallet debit must be the **same number**" — that gate is achievable and would still function correctly (an operator sees 45 == 45); only the parenthetical expected value is unreachable. The step does not "fail 100% of the time as written" as a divergence check; it carries a wrong expected value that would trigger a false STOP. (2) It does not "silently pre-decide" the open sheet-vs-factor reconciliation. `git log -S "50.3 credits" -- docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md` shows the line landed in fc4d20b (#188), and `git show b6a06a1:lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs` shows `creditsForMicros(micros)` had no factor parameter then and already returned 45 for this input. 50.3 was wrong on both sides the day it was written; the RATE_CARD factor (dd7abb7) did not make it wrong. `git log --oneline -3 -- docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md` confirms the doc was last touched at 95dc909, before the factor existed — and it contains no occurrence of "factor" or "sheet" at all. This is staleness, not a decision.

> **Correction.** Two impact claims overreach. `git blame -L 377,379 docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md` dates the line to commit fc4d20b on 2026-07-26 — three weeks before the rate-card reconciliation was recorded as open on 2026-08-17, and before `creditsForMicros` existed at all (introduced in 4504100/#189). It is a figure the code later moved away from, not a decision quietly slipped past an open question. And the operative requirement in the sentence is "the pre-flight quote and the wallet debit must be the same number"; only the parenthetical expected value is unreachable, so the step does not "fail 100% of the time" on its own terms — a maintainer following it would compare 45 against the server figure and be confused by a third number, rather than see a hard failure.

**Impact.** The one step described as proving the whole change fails 100% of the time as written, and its stated pass condition is unreachable in principle — both sides ceil to integers, so no factor makes either equal 50.3. It also restates a published figure in prose, which the SAME document explicitly refuses to do 100 lines earlier ('Per CLAUDE.md rule 8 the figures are deliberately NOT restated here — a hardcoded number in this step has already gone stale across one repricing'), and it silently pre-decides the reconciliation CLAUDE.md still lists as open ('no single factor reproduces the sheet exactly').

**Evidence.** docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md:378 `wallet debit must be the **same number** (50.3 credits). A divergence here is`. Against qpu-core.mjs:103 `return IQM_PER_TASK_MICROS + IQM_PER_SHOT_MICROS * shots;` (300_000 + 1_450*100 = 445_000) and :97 `return Math.ceil((micros * factor) / MICROS_PER_CREDIT);`. Distinct from prior finding 10 (#216, CLOSED), which was about the section 1.3 subscription figures.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The arithmetic holds exactly as claimed. `node -e 'const m=300000+1450*100; console.log(m, Math.ceil(m/10000), [1,1.05,1.13,1.5].map(f=>Math.ceil(m*f/10000)), 34+0.163*100)'` prints `445000 45 [45,47,51,67] 50.3` — the client (web/src/lib/qpu-budget.ts:34 `Math.ceil(micros / MICROS_PER_CREDIT)`) always yields 45, and the server (lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:97 `Math.ceil((micros * factor) / MICROS_PER_CREDIT)`) is integer-valued for every factor, so no configuration produces the 50.3 the runbook names at docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md:378. The internal contradiction is also real: line 234 of the same file says "Per CLAUDE.md rule 8 the figures are deliberately NOT restated here — a hardcoded number in this step has already gone stale across one repricing," and 50.3 is exactly a restatement of `TASK_FEE_CREDITS = 34` (web/src/lib/pricing.ts:60) plus `creditsPerShot: 0.163` (:85). Severity is low, not medium: the runbook is an un-executed procedure for a cutover blocked behind rules 14/15, metering is off, and the wrong figure is a parenthetical an operator would catch pre-flight rather than a defect in any deployed path.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The number is genuinely unreachable. `node -e 'const m=300000+1450*100; console.log(m, Math.ceil(m/10000))'` prints `445000 45`, and every cited line checks out in the main tree: qpu-budget.ts:34 is `Math.ceil(micros / MICROS_PER_CREDIT)`, qpu-core.mjs:97-99 is `Math.ceil((micros * factor) / MICROS_PER_CREDIT)` — integer for every factor — while 50.3 is the published-sheet arithmetic from pricing.ts:60 (`TASK_FEE_CREDITS = 34`), :85 (`creditsPerShot: 0.163`) and :147 (`jobCredits`). qpu-submit-panel.tsx:918 quotes `creditsForMicros(costMicros(shots))` and the file never imports `@/lib/pricing`, so no client path can emit 50.3 either. Docs-only defect on a procedure that is deliberately not yet executed (storefront closed, metering off), which is why I lowered severity to low.

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*

Severity is low: this is a non-rendered code comment on a deliberately dormant path, the TUTOR_RATES figures themselves are unaffected (R8), and the conclusion it defends — no tier chips — is still correct under R13 because buildWallet()/buildVerifier() (index.mjs:458-459, 522-524) both return undefined under the deployed empty envs.

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*
