## 7. Build, CI, deploy, and drift

8 findings — 5 medium, 3 low. 55 files read.

### How it actually works

**The gate.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs four jobs — `python`, `web`, `lambdas` (matrix `[tutor, qpu, sync, review-email, stripe]`, ci.yml:122) and `build-smoke`. `main`'s protection requires exactly those eight contexts, app-pinned to GitHub Actions (app_id 15368), `strict: true`, `enforce_admins: true`, and no required PR reviews — so CI is the only gate. There is no `continue-on-error` and no `|| true` anywhere; every step runs under `bash -e`, so the shell assertions in `build-smoke` are real gates, not decoration. Two extra gates ride inside existing jobs rather than as new contexts: `node --test scripts/founding-credit/*.test.mjs` (ci.yml:108) and `python scripts/validate_runnable.py --check`.

**The deploy.** Amplify app `d1ao02to23x85y` (us-east-2) builds `main` per `amplify.yml`: preBuild runs `web/jupyterlite-build/build.sh`, then `npm ci`; build runs `npm run build` (whose `prebuild` regenerates `tutor-core.generated.ts` and stages Monaco). The branch env carries `NEXT_PUBLIC_{TUTOR,QPU,SYNC,REVIEW_PREFS}_URL` and — correctly — no `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_URL`: rule 15 holds in deployed configuration, not only on paper. Ten functions live in five SAM stacks plus two us-east-1 CloudFront/WAF edge stacks and one CodeBuild standby stack.

**Drift.** `make drift` and `drift.yml` run `scripts/check-lambda-drift.mjs` (downloads each package, byte-compares top-level `.mjs`) and `scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs` (compares deployed `RATE_CARD` across `quantum-tutor`/`quantum-qpu-submit`, value-blind). I ran both read-only: all ten functions match git; both surfaces report ABSENT. Live env keys confirm metering is genuinely off (`quantum-tutor` carries only `SECRET_ID`). The `quantum-tutor` secret now exists and the stack redeployed 2026-08-18T01:08, so CLAUDE.md's "create the secret and deploy the tutor" open item is stale.

The blind spot is everything that is not Lambda source. `check-lambda-drift.mjs:39-55` enumerates functions only; nothing compares CloudFormation templates, CodeBuild buildspecs, WAF ACLs or Amplify config — and two stacks are behind git right now.

**Hygiene.** `lambda/sync/node_modules` is 2,560 tracked files while the other four gitignore theirs. I diffed the committed tree against `package-lock.json`: zero version divergence today. The consequence is not divergence but invisibility — `npm ci` (CI, and the README's own deploy line) deletes and rebuilds it, so nothing ever executes or verifies the committed copy. It is 2,560 unreviewed third-party files published in a public repo that no gate can see. Eleven untracked entries (2.3 GB, incl. five full repo worktrees under `.claude/`) match no `.gitignore` rule.

### Done well

- scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs:74-76 — the deployed-config check is value-blind by construction: `const distinctValues = new Set(...).size; for (const r of rows) delete r.value;` compares in memory then deletes the values before anything prints. The header explains why not even a hash prefix is safe for a short low-entropy factor. This is rule 6 honored in a CI log, and it did not exist at the prior audit.
- web/__tests__/infra/rate-card-parity.test.ts:27-40 — pins the byte-identical `RATE_CARD: !If [HasRateCard, !Sub "{{resolve:secretsmanager:${RateCardSecret}:SecretString:factor}}", !Ref AWS::NoValue]` line in BOTH metered templates, and asserts `Default: ""` so a bare `sam deploy` cannot switch metering on from version control. The repo-visible half of rule 5, newly landed.
- '.github/workflows/ci.yml:266-286' — the KaTeX/highlight assertion greps the built artifact for `katex-html`, `katex-mathml`, a rendered `⟩`, `hljs`/`hljs-`, and fails on any shipped `katex-error` span. Every Jest suite mocks these plugins into no-ops, so this step is the only observation of the render pipeline anywhere in the project.
- .github/workflows/ci.yml:167-186 — the cache reasoning is unusually correct: `.jupyterlite.doit.db` is deliberately excluded because a restored build database without its outputs makes doit skip real work (it once emitted an empty `pyodideUrl`), and the key is bumped to `-v2` because dropping a path does not purge an existing archive.
- infra/ci-standby/failover.sh:39-54 — `gha_contexts()` now derives the Lambda matrix from `ci.yml` with `sed -n 's/^ *dir: \[\(.*\)\] *$/\1/p'` and cross-checks it against `find "$REPO_ROOT/lambda" -mindepth 1 -maxdepth 1 -type d`, failing loudly on mismatch. This closes the prior hole where `stand-down` restored a 7-context gate against an 8-job workflow.
- web/jupyterlite-build/build.sh:29-51 — `assert_wasm_under_ceiling` fails not only when a wasm crosses CloudFront's 10,000,000-byte compression ceiling but also when `count -eq 0`: "Zero wasm found means the runtime was not staged at all -- fail loudly rather than let the guard pass vacuously." A guard that refuses to pass on an empty input set is rarer than it should be.
- web/__tests__/infra/no-commercial-terms.test.ts:370-379 — the rule-6 guard verifies its own escape hatch, asserting `git check-ignore -q` succeeds for `samconfig.toml`, `samconfig.prod.toml`, `lambda/stripe/samconfig.dev.toml` and friends, so the `.gitignore` entries that keep deploy-time parameter overrides out of a public repo cannot be silently deleted.
- scripts/stripe/check-webhook-parity.mjs:70-77 — identity is verified before anything else (`if (account.id !== expectAccount) { ... process.exit(1) }`), and `--expect-account` is mandatory (line 53). Given that all three local `stripe` CLI profiles resolve to the wrong account, refusing rather than inferring is the correct default and it is enforced in code, not prose.

### Findings

#### `f9805323f` The CodeBuild standby webhook has no actor filter, so on this public repo a fork PR would run arbitrary code with a role that can enumerate the production Cognito user pool — and post the commit status that IS the merge gate

`infra/ci-standby/failover.sh:86-89`

**medium** · Confirmed · security · observed live · **split verdict** · fix plan 05

**Claim.** `engage()` creates the webhook with filter groups containing only `EVENT` and `HEAD_REF` patterns. There is no `ACTOR_ACCOUNT_ID` filter group, so every `PULL_REQUEST_CREATED/UPDATED/REOPENED` event — including pull requests from forks of this public repository — starts a build that executes repo-supplied code (`npm ci`, `npm test`, `bash web/jupyterlite-build/build.sh`) under the `quantum-ci-standby-codebuild` service role.

> **Correction.** Two corrections to the framing. (1) The merge-gate half is overstated: `app_id: -1` does not let an outsider post `CI (CodeBuild standby)` directly — writing a GitHub commit status requires repo write permission, which a fork contributor does not have. What actually happens is narrower: the attacker's own PR build executes attacker-supplied test code and therefore self-reports green, which is true of any CI that runs PR code, and a maintainer must still click merge. (2) The finder understates the blast radius: the same role also carries `codeconnections:GetConnectionToken` and `UseConnection` on the live connection `$ARN` (verified with `aws iam get-role-policy --policy-name github-connection`), reachable from the build container via the CodeBuild credential endpoint. A GitHub App installation token is the larger prize here, and it is what turns the gate concern from "self-green PR" into a real repo-write path.

> **Correction.** The finder's reasoning holds, with two refinements that shift emphasis rather than weaken it. First, it does not credit the inline buildspec: template.yaml:148-155 chose it deliberately, and AWS lists it as one of four recommended public-repo mitigations — so this is one missing control out of four, not zero controls. Second, the finder understates the blast radius. The same role also holds codeconnections:GetConnectionToken and UseConnection on the live connection ARN (verified via `aws iam get-role-policy --policy-name github-connection`), and build code can reach the role through the CodeBuild container credentials endpoint, so a fork PR could mint a GitHub token for the connection rather than only reading Cognito. Minor terminology note: ACTOR_ACCOUNT_ID must be added as a filter *inside* each existing group (filters within a group are AND-ed); adding it as a separate group would be OR-ed and fix nothing.

**Impact.** That role carries `cognito-idp:ListUsers` on `$ARN` (verified live: `aws iam list-role-policies --role-name quantum-ci-standby-codebuild` lists `founding-ten-verify`), i.e. the production learner directory including email addresses. The same build reports the commit status `CI (CodeBuild standby)`, and `engage()` sets that context with `app_id: -1` — "any source" — so during failover the merge gate is satisfiable by attacker-controlled output. Latent, not live: no webhook exists while disengaged. But failover is exactly the moment nobody is inspecting IAM.

**Evidence.** `--filter-groups '[ [{"type": "EVENT", "pattern": "PULL_REQUEST_CREATED, PULL_REQUEST_UPDATED, PULL_REQUEST_REOPENED"}], [{"type": "EVENT", "pattern": "PUSH"}, {"type": "HEAD_REF", "pattern": "^refs/heads/main$"}] ]'` (failover.sh:86-89); `'{strict: true, checks: [{context: $ctx, app_id: -1}]}'` (failover.sh:95); `Action: - cognito-idp:ListUsers` scoped to `userpool/$USER_POOL_ID` (infra/ci-standby/template.yaml:103-106). `grep -i 'fork\|actor\|ACTOR_ACCOUNT_ID'` across failover.sh, template.yaml and README.md returns nothing.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, medium, observed live.** The core defect reproduces. `infra/ci-standby/failover.sh:83-89` calls `aws codebuild create-webhook` with only EVENT/HEAD_REF filter groups — `grep -rniE 'ACTOR_ACCOUNT_ID|pull-request-build-policy|requiresCommentApproval' infra/ .github/ scripts/` returns nothing, and `aws codebuild create-webhook help` confirms the installed CLI does expose `--pull-request-build-policy` (the guard AWS added for exactly this), which engage() never passes; with no policy, builds trigger automatically. `gh repo view AltivumInc-Admin/quantum-computing --json visibility` returns PUBLIC, so any fork PR would build. The inline buildspec cannot be edited by a PR (template.yaml:148-156), but it still runs `npm ci`, `npm test`, `make test`, `bash web/jupyterlite-build/build.sh` from the PR tree, so it is fork-controlled code execution; `aws iam get-role-policy --role-name quantum-ci-standby-codebuild --policy-name founding-ten-verify` confirms `cognito-idp:ListUsers` on `userpool/$USER_POOL_ID` in account $AWS_ACCOUNT. AWS's own bulletin 2026-002-AWS (Jan 2026, Wiz research on aws-sdk-js-v3 et al.) is this exact class. It stays medium and not high because `aws codebuild batch-get-projects --names quantum-ci-standby --region us-east-2 --query 'projects[0].webhook'` returns `null` — nothing is exposed until an operator runs `engage`.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Confirmed, medium, observed live.** Verified every element. Source: failover.sh:86-89 has only EVENT/HEAD_REF filters (repo-wide `grep -rIn -iE 'ACTOR_ACCOUNT_ID|fork' infra/ .github/ scripts/` finds nothing), line 95 sets app_id -1, template.yaml:96-106 grants cognito-idp:ListUsers. Live read-only: `gh repo view AltivumInc-Admin/quantum-computing --json visibility` = PUBLIC; `aws iam get-role-policy --role-name quantum-ci-standby-codebuild --policy-name founding-ten-verify` returns ListUsers on $USER_POOL_ID (the production learner pool — it is the default UserPoolId in the tutor/sync/qpu/stripe/review-email templates); `gh api repos/.../branches/main/protection` shows NO required_pull_request_reviews at all, so status checks are the entire automated gate; `aws codebuild batch-get-projects --names quantum-ci-standby --region us-east-2` returns webhook:null, confirming the finder's own "latent, not live" caveat. AWS's "Best practices for using webhooks with AWS CodeBuild" recommends exactly the missing ACTOR_ACCOUNT_ID filter for public repos, and bulletin 2026-002-AWS records this class being exploited against AWS's own public repos. The inline buildspec (template.yaml:148-155) is a real, deliberate mitigation and is one of AWS's four recommendations, but it does not blunt the defect: npm ci lifecycle scripts, the `pretest` hook (node ../scripts/gen-tutor-core.mjs), npm test, make test and bash web/jupyterlite-build/build.sh all execute PR-supplied tree contents. Severity held at medium, not high, because the webhook genuinely does not exist until an operator runs `engage`.

*Split verdict: reproduces said Partly true; correct as written said Confirmed. This one needs a human.*

*Raised independently by 1 other reader(s) on a different dimension: `f66ea059e` (Identity, data, and the security posture).*

#### `fb8858b38` The deployed CodeBuild standby buildspec is missing the founding-credit money-path test step that is in the committed template

`infra/ci-standby/template.yaml:176`

**medium** · Confirmed · correctness · observed live · fix plan 10

**Claim.** The `quantum-ci-standby` stack was last updated 2026-07-29T16:21 UTC; `infra/ci-standby/template.yaml` was last committed 2026-08-03. Diffing the deployed inline buildspec (`aws codebuild batch-get-projects --names quantum-ci-standby --query 'projects[0].source.buildspec'`) against the block extracted from the committed template yields exactly one hunk: the deployed project has no `node --test scripts/founding-credit/*.test.mjs` command. Separately, neither version carries ci.yml:266's KaTeX/`katex-error` assertion, so the mirror has never covered that gate.

> **Correction.** Two calibrations to the impact, neither of which changes the verdict. (1) The gate is dormant right now, not merely hypothetically: `aws codebuild batch-get-projects --names quantum-ci-standby --region us-east-2 --query 'projects[0].webhook.url'` returns `None` (disengaged, per failover.sh's own status logic), and `gh api repos/AltivumInc-Admin/quantum-computing/branches/main/protection/required_status_checks` still requires the eight GitHub Actions contexts pinned to app_id 15368 — the standby context is not in the gate. The defect only bites during a `failover.sh engage` window. (2) "A build that never runs the credit-issuance tests" is slightly too strong: the deployed buildspec still runs `web` `npm test`, which includes web/__tests__/infra/credit-writers.test.ts — that reads scripts/founding-credit/issue.mjs and asserts the credit-writer allowlist and the never-write-a-tier invariant. What is actually lost is the 17 unit tests in issue.test.mjs, including the cohort ceiling, the double-issuance/ALREADY_ISSUED idempotency legs, and "the marker row carries NO expiresAt" — the exact DynamoDB-TTL trap CLAUDE.md flags. That is still the substantive half of the guard on the one R11 exception.

> **Correction.** Two calibrations on the finder's framing. First, severity: the standby is currently disengaged (`projects[0].webhook.url` returns `None`, i.e. idle at $0), so the live merge gate is GitHub Actions, where ci.yml:108-110 does run the step. The gap bites only inside a deliberately engaged failover. Second, "a build that never runs the credit-issuance tests" overstates the coverage loss — the standby's `cd web && npm test` step still executes `web/__tests__/infra/credit-writers.test.ts` (the credit-writer allowlist) and `web/__tests__/infra/wallet-ttl.test.ts`, so what is actually lost during a failover is the 19 issuance-correctness assertions in scripts/founding-credit/issue.test.mjs (no `expiresAt` on the marker row, the 21st-issuance ConditionExpression ceiling, `tier` never written), not the money-path guards wholesale.

**Impact.** `failover.sh engage` repoints main's entire merge gate onto this one project's commit status. If failover were engaged today, the gate would be a build that never runs the credit-issuance tests — the same tests ci.yml:108 justifies as "Operator scripts that move money" — and never runs the render-path assertion. The template's own Description (lines 3-5) claims it "Runs the same test/lint/build-smoke matrix as .github/workflows/ci.yml", which is false of the deployed project. Nothing in CI compares the buildspec to ci.yml; the README treats it as a manual redeploy chore.

**Evidence.** Committed line 176: `- cd "$CODEBUILD_SRC_DIR" && node --test scripts/founding-credit/*.test.mjs`. `diff -u` of git-extracted vs deployed buildspec: `-      - cd "$CODEBUILD_SRC_DIR" && node --test scripts/founding-credit/*.test.mjs` (only hunk). `aws cloudformation describe-stacks --stack-name quantum-ci-standby` -> `UPDATE_COMPLETE  2026-07-29T16:21:27`. `grep -n 'katex\|hljs' infra/ci-standby/template.yaml` -> no matches.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, medium, observed live.** Reproduced exactly. `aws codebuild batch-get-projects --names quantum-ci-standby --query 'projects[0].source.buildspec'` diffed (whitespace-normalized) against the `BuildSpec: |` block extracted from infra/ci-standby/template.yaml yields exactly one substantive hunk: the deployed project lacks `- cd "$CODEBUILD_SRC_DIR" && node --test scripts/founding-credit/*.test.mjs` (committed template line 176). `aws cloudformation describe-stacks --stack-name quantum-ci-standby` returns UPDATE_COMPLETE 2026-07-29T16:21:27, and `git diff c24b9dd HEAD -- infra/ci-standby/template.yaml` shows that 2026-08-03 commit (#204) added precisely that step and nothing else — so the deployed stack is one un-redeployed commit stale. The gap is unguarded: `grep -rn 'buildspec' .github/workflows/` finds only prose comments, and `make drift` (Makefile:40) runs only check-lambda-drift.mjs plus Stripe parity, never CodeBuild. `grep -c 'katex\|hljs' infra/ci-standby/template.yaml` returns 0, so ci.yml:270-286's KaTeX/`katex-error` render gate is absent from the mirror in both versions, as the finder stated.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Confirmed, low, observed live.** Reproduced exactly. `aws codebuild batch-get-projects --names quantum-ci-standby --region us-east-2 --query 'projects[0].source.buildspec'` diffed against the inline block extracted from infra/ci-standby/template.yaml (BuildSpec starts at line 156; the command is line 176) yields one substantive hunk — the deployed project lacks `- cd "$CODEBUILD_SRC_DIR" && node --test scripts/founding-credit/*.test.mjs` and its three comment lines. `aws cloudformation describe-stacks --stack-name quantum-ci-standby` returns UPDATE_COMPLETE 2026-07-29T16:21:27, while `git log -- infra/ci-standby/template.yaml` shows that step added 2026-08-03 in e0b9ec7 (whose own message says "Mirrored into the CodeBuild standby buildspec" — the mirror edit landed, the redeploy never did). The rest holds too: failover.sh:93-96 PATCHes main's required checks down to the single `CI (CodeBuild standby)` context, `grep -rn 'katex\|hljs' infra/ci-standby/` returns nothing against ci.yml:266-286, and nothing outside infra/ci-standby/ references the stack — `make drift` runs only scripts/check-lambda-drift.mjs (Lambda code) and check-rate-parity.mjs.

#### `f4b00f00a` The live tutor WAF is two merged PRs behind lambda/tutor/edge.yaml, and the daily drift job reports green because it inspects only Lambda function code

`lambda/tutor/edge.yaml:70-72`

**medium** · Confirmed · correctness · observed live · **split verdict** · fix plan 10

**Claim.** The `quantum-tutor-edge` CloudFormation stack has never been updated since creation (2026-06-25, `CREATE_COMPLETE`, no `LastUpdatedTime`), while `lambda/tutor/edge.yaml` has taken two commits since — 39e1009 (#176) and 2b2680e (#186, 2026-07-26, "browser-readable 429 — CORS on the WAF block + OPTIONS exemption"). The repo's drift guard cannot see this because `scripts/check-lambda-drift.mjs:39-55` enumerates Lambda function names only and compares nothing else.

> **Correction.** Two adjustments to the finder's framing. (1) `LastUpdatedTime` is not absent — it is 2026-06-25T16:29:33Z, six seconds after CreationTime, the normal create-time stamp; the substantive point (never updated since creation) still holds. (2) Severity is medium, not high. The rate rule still blocks, so no paid-inference fan-out escapes and the rule 12 spend bound is untouched; the entire user-visible harm requires one IP to exceed ~150-300 req/min (halved because uncounted OPTIONS now consume budget), and at that point the degradation is error-copy quality — `ask-tutor.tsx:344` shows "tutor unreachable" instead of `ask-tutor.tsx:78-79`'s "wait a minute". Worth noting as reinforcement, though: `lambda/tutor/README.md:186-193` asserts "429 = too fast, 403 = a signing/auth problem" as present truth, which the deployed ACL does not deliver.

> **Correction.** Three parts of the framing overstate it. (1) The protection itself is not degraded — the per-IP rate limit fires and blocks at 300/min/IP exactly as intended; what is two commits behind is the block's error ergonomics (readable 429 + CORS header + OPTIONS exemption), not the shed. (2) The OPTIONS clause has lost most of its weight: `aws lambda get-function-url-config --function-name quantum-tutor --region us-east-2` shows `MaxAge: 3600` is now deployed (my OPTIONS probe returned `access-control-max-age: 3600`), so preflights are roughly one per browser-hour, not the 1:1-with-POST the edge.yaml comment at lines 137-139 still assumes — that comment is itself stale. (3) Two smaller slips: the stack does carry a `LastUpdatedTime` (2026-06-25T16:29:33, six seconds after creation, i.e. the create itself), and `NEXT_PUBLIC_TUTOR_URL` is set at the Amplify APP level, not the branch env (branch `main`'s env is empty). Severity is medium, not high: nothing is exposed and no spend path is open — a throttled learner sees the wrong sentence in a condition requiring >300 requests/minute from one IP. The durable half of the finding is the guard gap, not the symptom.

**Impact.** The Amplify branch env sets `NEXT_PUBLIC_TUTOR_URL=https://$CF_DOMAIN/`, which is exactly this stack's `DistributionDomainName` output — so this is the live tutor front door. A rate-limited learner receives WAF's default 403 with no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, which the browser reports as an opaque network failure rather than the readable 429 that #186 shipped; OPTIONS preflights are still counted against the per-IP limit. Worse than the symptom is the signal: `gh run list --workflow=drift.yml` shows `success` every day through 2026-08-18, and `make drift` prints "All 10 functions match git", so the one mechanism built to answer "is what is running what is in git?" answers yes while a live internet-facing protection layer is 23 days behind.

**Evidence.** git `lambda/tutor/edge.yaml` carries `CustomResponseBodies:` (line 61) and inside the rule `CustomResponse: / ResponseCode: 429 / CustomResponseBodyKey: RateLimited` (lines 70-72) plus an `OPTIONS` exemption (`SearchString: OPTIONS`, line 151) — 15 matches for CORS/OPTIONS/CustomResponse tokens. The LIVE resource, read via `aws wafv2 get-web-acl --scope CLOUDFRONT --name quantum-tutor-edge`, returns a single rule `"Name": "PerIpRateLimit" ... "Action": {"Block": {}}` and `"CustomResponseBodies": null` — no custom response, no OPTIONS exemption. `aws cloudformation get-template --stack-name quantum-tutor-edge` returns a 115-line body with 1 such match against git's 15.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, medium, observed live.** `aws wafv2 get-web-acl --scope CLOUDFRONT --region us-east-1 --name quantum-tutor-edge --id b77fad07-0299-47b3-aab3-14f6cadbdec3` returns the single rule `PerIpRateLimit` with `"Action": {"Block": {}}`, no `CustomResponseBodies`, and no `ScopeDownStatement` — i.e. the c75eb16-era shape, while `lambda/tutor/edge.yaml:61-72,150-151` carries the 429 `CustomResponse`, the `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` response header, and the OPTIONS `NotStatement`; `aws cloudformation get-template --stack-name quantum-tutor-edge --region us-east-1` confirms the live body is 115 lines with `Block: {}` at line 45, and `describe-stacks` shows CreationTime and LastUpdatedTime both 2026-06-25T16:29 (never updated). It is the live front door: `aws amplify get-app --app-id d1ao02to23x85y --region us-east-2` sets `NEXT_PUBLIC_TUTOR_URL=https://$CF_DOMAIN/`, exactly this stack's `DistributionDomainName` output, and `quantum-tutor` was deployed 2026-08-18T01:08 with its `quantum-tutor` secret present, so the tutor really is serving through it. The blind spot is real: `scripts/check-lambda-drift.mjs:39-55` enumerates only Lambda function names, nothing in `scripts/` or `.github/workflows/` calls `cloudformation`, and I ran `node scripts/check-lambda-drift.mjs`, which printed "All 10 functions match git". This is a forgotten deploy rather than a posture — the sibling `quantum-qpu-edge` ACL already carries the 429 + ACAO + OPTIONS exemption live.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, medium, observed live.** The drift is real and I reproduced it exactly. `aws cloudformation get-template --stack-name quantum-tutor-edge --region us-east-1` returns a 115-line body; diffing it comment-stripped against `lambda/tutor/edge.yaml` yields ONLY the #176+#186 hunks — deployed has `Block: {}` with no `CustomResponseBodies`, no `ResponseHeaders: Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, and no `ScopeDownStatement`; everything else (OAC `SigningBehavior: always`, cache policy, WebACL association) is identical. `aws wafv2 get-web-acl --scope CLOUDFRONT --name quantum-tutor-edge --id b77fad07-...` confirms the live ACL: one rule `PerIpRateLimit`, `{"Block":{}}`, `CustomResponseBodies: None`, `RateBasedStatement` with no scope-down. `aws amplify get-app --app-id d1ao02to23x85y --region us-east-2` shows `NEXT_PUBLIC_TUTOR_URL=https://$CF_DOMAIN/`, matching the stack's `DistributionDomainName` output, and a read-only `curl -X OPTIONS` against it returns 200 — this is the live tutor front door. I ran `node scripts/check-lambda-drift.mjs`: "All 10 functions match git," and its `FUNCTIONS` array (lines 39-55) is Lambda names only, so nothing in the repo compares CloudFormation templates.

*Split verdict: reproduces said Confirmed; correct as written said Partly true. This one needs a human.*

*Raised as high, published at medium — the verification pass set this.*

#### `fb854583f` The read-only OIDC drift role can read plaintext Lambda environment secrets, contradicting its own comment — and the workflow that assumes it pins every third-party action by mutable tag

`infra/github-oidc-drift-role.yaml:59-63`

**medium** · Confirmed · security · observed live · **split verdict** · fix plan 05

**Claim.** The role grants `lambda:GetFunction` and `lambda:GetFunctionConfiguration` on `function:quantum-*`. `GetFunctionConfiguration` returns environment variable VALUES, and `quantum-qpu-submit` carries `EDGE_SECRET` as a deploy-time-resolved plaintext env var. The template's own comment two lines below asserts the opposite property for the adjacent grant: "it is read-only and returns metadata only (no code, no environment secrets)".

> **Correction.** The finder treated the comment as correct-but-misplaced ("asserts the opposite property for the *adjacent* grant"). It is actually false about its own grant, which makes this bigger than reported. `aws lambda list-functions --region us-east-2` returns full `Environment.Variables` including values — I got EDGE_SECRET's 64-char length straight out of the ListFunctions payload, never GetFunctionConfiguration. That grant is `Resource: "*"` (infra/github-oidc-drift-role.yaml:66-68), so it is not scoped to `quantum-*` at all: 268 functions are visible in us-east-2 alone, and 106 non-quantum ones expose secret-shaped env keys — `tj-scents-api-prod-webhooks` and `sales-crm-api-dev-handleWebhook` carry `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and `DB_PASSWORD` for unrelated live businesses in this shared account. Grepping the repo (`grep -rn "list-functions\|ListFunctions" --exclude-dir=node_modules`) finds hits only inside the template's own comment: no script calls ListFunctions, so the wildcard grant buys nothing and can simply be deleted. That also means the prior audit's "low, low-blast-radius" calibration for EDGE_SECRET (docs/codebase-audit-2026-08-16.md:245-248) does not carry over to this statement.

> **Correction.** Two links in the finder's chain are wrong, and both make the real problem look smaller than it is. (1) The finder treats the "no environment secrets" comment as an accurate statement about `ListFunctions` that merely sits next to a leaky grant. It is false about `ListFunctions` itself: `aws lambda list-functions --region us-east-2 --query "Functions[?FunctionName=='quantum-qpu-submit'].Environment.Variables.LEDGER_TABLE"` returns `["quantum-qpu-ledger"]`, i.e. ListFunctions returns Environment.Variables values — and that grant is `Resource: "*"`, not prefix-scoped. `--query "length(Functions[?!contains(FunctionName,'quantum') && Environment!=null])"` counts 188 unrelated functions in this shared account (vetroi-stripe-webhook-handler, tj-scents-api-prod-webhooks, sales-crm-api-*, amplify-*) whose env values the drift role can also read. The comment is not merely misplaced; it is wrong where it stands, and the blast radius is the whole account, not `quantum-*`. (2) Naming `lambda:GetFunctionConfiguration` as the culprit implies a fix by deleting it. It fixes nothing — `lambda:GetFunction` alone returns `Configuration.Environment.Variables` (command above), and GetFunction is the one permission the drift check cannot work without, since scripts/check-lambda-drift.mjs:75 uses it for `Code.Location`. This is an inherent property of the Lambda read APIs, not a trimmable over-grant; the only real remedies are scoping ListFunctions away or moving EDGE_SECRET/RATE_CARD to a runtime Secrets Manager fetch instead of a deploy-time `{{resolve:}}` env var (lambda/qpu/template.yaml:190). Also minor: rule 6 explicitly sanctions "deployed configuration" as a home for the markup factor, so a deployed RATE_CARD env var is not itself a rule 6 violation — the concern is only that CI credentials could print it into a public Actions log.

**Impact.** `EDGE_SECRET` is the `x-qpu-edge` shared header CloudFront injects (lambda/qpu/edge.yaml:177) and the API requires; possessing it is what lets a caller bypass the CloudFront WAF and per-IP rate limiting in front of the QPU submit endpoint. After the RATE_CARD cutover the same call also returns the markup factor that rule 6 says must never leave private notes and deployed config. Any code running in the drift job can print either — and drift.yml pins `actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-node@v4` and `aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@v4` by mutable tag, not commit SHA, so a compromised action release is sufficient. `scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs` being value-blind constrains what the *script* prints, not what the *credentials* can do.

**Evidence.** `Action: - lambda:GetFunction - lambda:GetFunctionConfiguration / Resource: !Sub $ARN}:function:${FunctionNamePrefix}-*` (lines 60-63), followed by "# ListFunctions cannot be resource-scoped; it is read-only and returns / # metadata only (no code, no environment secrets)." (lines 64-65). Template source: `EDGE_SECRET: !Sub "{{resolve:secretsmanager:${EdgeSecretName}:SecretString}}"` (lambda/qpu/template.yaml:190). Live: `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name quantum-qpu-submit --query 'keys(Environment.Variables)'` -> `LEDGER_TABLE  EDGE_SECRET  TASKS_TABLE  RESULTS_BUCKET`.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, medium, observed live.** Every element reproduces. `aws iam get-role-policy --role-name quantum-ci-drift-check --policy-name read-lambda-code-only` returns the template's policy verbatim (role is live), and `gh variable list --repo AltivumInc-Admin/quantum-computing` shows AWS_DRIFT_ROLE_ARN set, so the daily job assumes it rather than skipping. `aws lambda list-functions --region us-east-2 --query 'Functions[?FunctionName==`quantum-qpu-submit`].{fn:FunctionName,edgeSecretCharLen:length(Environment.Variables.EDGE_SECRET)}'` returns 64 — a real plaintext secret, from template.yaml:190. I looked for an exculpating caller and found a partial one: `scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs:40-52` reads `Environment.Variables.RATE_CARD`, so GetFunctionConfiguration's env-reading power is genuinely required by the workflow's second step and cannot be narrowed by IAM; that defends the `quantum-*` grant but not the wildcard one. Severity stays medium, not high: nothing is exposed today (no `pull_request` trigger, `aud`+`sub` both pinned to `repo:AltivumInc-Admin/quantum-computing:ref:refs/heads/main`, 1h max session), so exploitation needs a compromised action release first.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, medium, observed live.** The defect is real and live. `aws iam get-role-policy --role-name quantum-ci-drift-check --policy-name read-lambda-code-only` returns exactly the template at infra/github-oidc-drift-role.yaml:57-68, and `gh variable list -R AltivumInc-Admin/quantum-computing` shows AWS_DRIFT_ROLE_ARN is set, so the daily job at .github/workflows/drift.yml really does mint these credentials. `aws lambda get-function --function-name quantum-qpu-submit --region us-east-2 --query 'keys(Configuration.Environment.Variables)'` returns `["LEDGER_TABLE","EDGE_SECRET","TASKS_TABLE","RESULTS_BUCKET"]` — plaintext, and EDGE_SECRET is the header the gate at lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs:757 compares against. I hold it at medium rather than high: exploitation needs a compromised action release or a push to main, and the endpoint EDGE_SECRET fronts still sits behind the Cognito JWT authorizer (lambda/qpu/template.yaml:166-173) with the spend fences in the handler, so the secret buys WAF/rate-limit bypass, not spend.

*Split verdict: reproduces said Confirmed; correct as written said Partly true. This one needs a human.*

*Raised independently by 1 other reader(s) on a different dimension: `f50e54cb6` (Identity, data, and the security posture).*

#### `f049f7b89` The Pyodide runtime shipped to every learner is fetched with no integrity check, cached across builds, and installed by an unpinned pip resolve

`web/jupyterlite-build/build.sh:66-70`

**medium** · Partly true · security · observed live · fix plan 10

**Claim.** `fetch_pyodide_core` downloads the release tarball over HTTPS with no checksum or signature verification, and `fetch_closure_wheels` (line 88) does the same for every wheel from jsdelivr. The tarball is then persisted as `.cache/pyodide-core-${version}.tar.bz2` and that directory is restored by both `actions/cache@v4` (ci.yml:180-186) and `amplify.yml`'s `jupyterlite-build/.cache/**/*` cache path, so a single bad fetch survives subsequent builds. The build environment itself is not reproducible: `requirements.txt` leaves `jupyter-server>=2.14` and `build>=1.2` unbounded, and `pyproject.toml` specifies every dependency as an open floor with no lockfile in CI.

> **Correction.** Two claims in the finding are false. (1) The `immutable` framing: both paths the finder names are explicitly bounded, not immutable. `awk` over `customHttp.yml` maps `/pyodide/**` and `/lab/static/pyodide/**` to `public, max-age=86400`, each with a long comment explaining why immutable was deliberately withheld (an Emscripten JS/wasm mismatch after a version bump). Live-verified read-only: `curl -sSI https://quantum.altivum.ai/pyodide/pyodide.js` and `.../lab/static/pyodide/pyodide.asm.wasm` both return `cache-control: public, max-age=86400`. Only `/lab/build/**`, `/lab/files/wheels/**`, `/_next/static/**` and `/monaco/**` get the year-long immutable grant, and all four are content-hashed or version-stamped by construction. (2) The reproducibility claim overreaches: `requirements.txt` pins `jupyterlite-core==0.7.6` and `jupyterlite-pyodide-kernel==0.7.0` exactly — those determine the entire lab JS payload — plus `comm==0.2.3`, and both Pyodide versions are pinned (parsed from `pyodide-runtime.ts` and the kernel constant), with the wheel closure computed deterministically from the lock inside the pinned tarball. Only `jupyter-server>=2.14` and `build>=1.2` float. `pyproject.toml`'s `pennylane`/`openfermion` floors govern the pytest curriculum env installed at `ci.yml:52` and never touch the deployed browser artifact, so citing them as making the shipped runtime irreproducible is a category error. The finder also missed the cheapest available fix, which the prior audit's verifier already identified: `pyodide-lock.json` arrives inside the GitHub tarball (a different origin from jsdelivr) and carries a sha256 for every package — I counted 294 of 294 populated via `python3 -c` over `static/pyodide/pyodide-lock.json` — so the wheel half is verifiable today with no new pin.

> **Correction.** Two claims in the impact do not survive checking. (1) "Served `immutable` for a year under `customHttp.yml`" is false and inverted: customHttp.yml:91-94 serves `/pyodide/**` and :117-120 serves `/lab/static/pyodide/**` at `public, max-age=86400` — one day — and lines 79-90 and 103-110 are a long comment explaining that `immutable` was deliberately withheld from exactly these two trees because they carry unhashed names, to be restored only once build.sh stages a version-stamped path. So a bad byte revalidates daily, it is not pinned in learners' browsers for a year. (2) The reproducibility half overreaches. `pyproject.toml` does not "specify every dependency as an open floor" — `ruff>=0.5,<0.16` is explicitly bounded with a comment — and more importantly those deps (`pennylane`, `openfermion`) are the pytest/notebook environment installed by ci.yml:52 `pip install -e ".[dev,full]"`; they contribute zero bytes to `web/public/pyodide/*`. The packages that actually determine the shipped lab, `jupyterlite-core` and `jupyterlite-pyodide-kernel`, are pinned with `==` in requirements.txt:1-2, and both Pyodide versions are read from pinned constants. The drifting inputs are `jupyter-server` and `build` only.

**Impact.** These bytes become `web/public/pyodide/*` and `web/public/lab/static/pyodide/*` — the wasm runtime executed in every learner's browser, served `immutable` for a year under `customHttp.yml`. Only the `-part`/`mv` rename and a `tar -xjf` success guard the content; a truncated-but-valid or substituted archive passes. Reproducibility compounds it: two builds a week apart can resolve different `jupyter-server`, `build`, `pennylane` and `openfermion` versions, so "CI was green on this commit" is not a statement about a fixed input set. An 840 KB `uv.lock` sits in the working tree, untracked and consumed by nothing. There is no `.github/dependabot.yml`. Prior audit raised the missing integrity check; still present.

**Evidence.** `curl -fsSL --retry 3 "https://github.com/pyodide/pyodide/releases/download/${version}/pyodide-core-${version}.tar.bz2" -o "${tarball}.part"` then `mv "${tarball}.part" "$tarball"` (build.sh:66-70) — no `sha256sum`, no `--fail-early` digest, no signature. `curl -fsSL --retry 3 "${cdn}/${whl}" -o "$dest/$whl"` (line 88) with only `test -s` after it. `web/jupyterlite-build/requirements.txt:3-4` `jupyter-server>=2.14` / `build>=1.2`. `pyproject.toml:6-13` and `:16-37` are floors only. `ls .github/` -> `workflows` only.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, medium, observed live.** The defect itself reproduces: `fetch_pyodide_core` (build.sh:60-77) curls the GitHub release tarball with only a `.part`/`mv` rename and a `tar -xjf` success test, `fetch_closure_wheels` (build.sh:81-88) curls each jsdelivr wheel with only `test -s`, `grep -rn 'sha256|shasum|checksum' web/jupyterlite-build/*.sh *.py` matches nothing, and both `amplify.yml:29` and `ci.yml:180-186` persist `.cache` across builds while the `[[ -s "$tarball" ]]` short-circuit reuses it on size alone. The path is live, not dormant: `git ls-files web/public/pyodide` and `web/public/lab` both return 0, so every Amplify deploy re-runs `build.sh` (amplify.yml:14) and those bytes are what learners execute. But the impact framing is wrong on the cache headers, and the reproducibility half is largely misattributed — see correction. This is already the prior audit's finding #6, still open as issue #221 (`gh issue view 221` -> state OPEN), so "still present" is fair; medium is the right level, since exploitation requires compromising GitHub releases, jsdelivr, or the build cache rather than anything reachable today.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, medium, read.** The core defect is real and exactly as described: web/jupyterlite-build/build.sh:66-69 curls the GitHub release tarball and mv's it into .cache/ with no digest check, line 88 curls each jsdelivr wheel with only a `test -s`, and line 63's `[[ -s "$tarball" ]]` short-circuits on size alone against a .cache restored by .github/workflows/ci.yml:180-186 and amplify.yml:28. requirements.txt:3-4 does leave `jupyter-server>=2.14` and `build>=1.2` unbounded, and .github/ contains only `workflows` (no dependabot.yml). This is also a repeat: docs/codebase-audit-2026-08-16.md:311-316 raised and confirmed the identical gap, still unfixed — and the fix is nearly free on the wheel half, since web/public/pyodide/pyodide-lock.json (which arrives inside the GitHub tarball, a different origin from jsdelivr) already carries a sha256 for every wheel and build.sh ignores it. Two parts of the framing are wrong, see correction.

#### `fdcc00445` The Makefile and provision-sandbox.mjs both document the superseded Stripe sandbox account id, so the documented sandbox-provisioning command refuses against the real sandbox

`Makefile:35`

**low** · Confirmed · rule-violation · ran locally · fix plan 03

**Claim.** `make stripe-parity`'s documented sandbox invocation and `scripts/stripe/provision-sandbox.mjs`'s usage header both name `$STRIPE_ACCOUNT`, which CLAUDE.md:24 records as "An older ... [account that] is NOT the one that is provisioned". The actual sandbox is `$STRIPE_ACCOUNT`, which only `scripts/stripe/e2e-sandbox.mjs:16` gets right. The two halves of the sandbox workflow — the script that builds it and the script that exercises it — disagree about which account it is.

> **Correction.** Severity lowered to low. The blast radius is one operator paste, and it fails loudly with the answer in hand: the guard's message prints `key belongs to <real acct id> (<display name>), expected <stale id>. Refusing to write.` (provision-sandbox.mjs:104-105), so the correct id is handed to the operator at the moment of refusal. Nothing at runtime, in CI, or on any money path reads these strings — they are comments in a Makefile recipe and a script header — and the sandbox is already provisioned and exercised (ac25c22 reports 6/6 against $STRIPE_ACCOUNT), so this blocks a re-run, not a first build.

> **Correction.** Category "rule-violation" is slightly off. The CLAUDE.md rule requires every script to take `--expect-account` and refuse a mismatch rather than trusting a written-down id, and all three scripts do exactly that (provision-sandbox.mjs:65 requires the flag, :101 refuses a mismatch). What is broken is the written-down id in a comment — precisely the failure mode the rule anticipates and already contains — so this is documentation drift caught by a working guard, not a missing or violated guard.

**Impact.** CLAUDE.md's Stripe rules make the sandbox the mandatory rehearsal surface before anything is believed about live, and `provision-sandbox.mjs` is the only thing that creates the `ql_credits` product and the nine-event webhook endpoint with a pinned `api_version`. Copying its own documented command aborts on the `--expect-account` guard against the real sandbox key. It fails safe rather than writing to the wrong account, so this is friction rather than loss — but the recorded id is the superseded one in both places an operator copies from, which is precisely the confusion `--expect-account` exists to prevent.

**Evidence.** Makefile:35 `@#   make stripe-parity ACCOUNT=$STRIPE_ACCOUNT KEYREF="$SECRET_REF Learner/Stripe Sandbox/Secret Key"`; scripts/stripe/provision-sandbox.mjs:40 ` *       --expect-account $STRIPE_ACCOUNT \`; scripts/stripe/e2e-sandbox.mjs:16 ` *       --expect-account $STRIPE_ACCOUNT \`. CLAUDE.md:23-25 names `$STRIPE_ACCOUNT` as the sandbox and `$STRIPE_ACCOUNT` as superseded (while CLAUDE.md:50 itself still repeats the superseded id — the same drift).

**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, low, ran locally.** Both cited lines carry the superseded id (`grep -rn "$STRIPE_ACCOUNT\|$STRIPE_ACCOUNT" --include='*' .` → Makefile:35, scripts/stripe/provision-sandbox.mjs:40, CLAUDE.md:50 vs. e2e-sandbox.mjs:16 and CLAUDE.md:23). `git log -1 ac25c22` settles which id is real: that commit's body says "THE SANDBOX. $STRIPE_ACCOUNT mirrors live's structure ... sandbox e2e 6/6", and `git log -p -S'$STRIPE_ACCOUNT' -- CLAUDE.md` shows the same commit rewriting CLAUDE.md's sandbox line from `$STRIPE_ACCOUNT` to `$STRIPE_ACCOUNT` while leaving three copies of the old id behind — so this is genuine same-commit drift, not a disagreement about which account exists. provision-sandbox.mjs:101-106 is an exact-equality check that calls `die(1, ...)`, so pasting its own header command with the sandbox key does abort before any write, exactly as claimed.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Confirmed, low, ran locally.** Ran `git show ac25c22 | grep -n "$STRIPE_ACCOUNT\|$STRIPE_ACCOUNT"`: that single commit rewrote CLAUDE.md:23 from "Sandbox is $STRIPE_ACCOUNT" to "Sandbox is $STRIPE_ACCOUNT … An older $STRIPE_ACCOUNT … is NOT the one that is provisioned", yet in the same diff added the stale id at Makefile:35, scripts/stripe/provision-sandbox.mjs:40 and CLAUDE.md:50 while giving only scripts/stripe/e2e-sandbox.mjs:16 the current one — so the superseded id is specifically the old SANDBOX id and the finder's reading is exact. The blast radius is bounded: provision-sandbox.mjs:101-107 dies with "WRONG ACCOUNT: key belongs to … Refusing to write" before any mutation, and check-webhook-parity.mjs:71 / check-catalog-parity.mjs:88 refuse identically, so copying the documented command aborts with a message naming the real account rather than writing anywhere. I could not confirm the key's account live (1Password is locked here: `op read "$SECRET_REF Learner/Stripe Sandbox/Secret Key"` failed), so the commit diff is the evidence. Severity lowered to low: this is stale comment text on a dormant workflow (storefront closed, metering off) that the working --expect-account guard converts into a self-correcting error message.

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*

#### `fadbcc77f` A script that deletes and recreates the LIVE Stripe webhook endpoint and rotates its signing secret exists only as an untracked working-tree file

`scripts/stripe/rotate-webhook-endpoint.mjs:1-36`

**low** · Partly true · hygiene · ran locally · fix plan 03

**Claim.** `git status` reports `?? scripts/stripe/rotate-webhook-endpoint.mjs`. The file recreates a Stripe webhook endpoint with the full `REQUIRED_WEBHOOK_EVENTS` set and a pinned `api_version`, pipes the new signing secret into Secrets Manager, forces `update-function-configuration` to recycle the warm Lambda, and sends a signed probe before retiring the old endpoint. Its read-only counterparts (`check-webhook-parity.mjs`, `check-catalog-parity.mjs`) are committed; the write half is not.

> **Correction.** Three claims overstate the case. (1) The script never deletes anything: lines 106-110 create a new endpoint alongside the old, lines 174-181 leave the old one ENABLED on probe failure, and line 184-186 only disables it ("kept, not deleted — rollback is one toggle"). It also gates on `--expect-account`, requires `--confirm-live` for any `sk_live_` key, never prints the secret, and refuses to retire the old endpoint until a signed probe returns 2xx — the title's "deletes and recreates the LIVE endpoint" describes a far more dangerous tool than the one on disk. (2) "Invisible to the rule-6 guard" is backwards: `web/__tests__/infra/no-commercial-terms.test.ts:180-188` enumerates via `git ls-files` deliberately, because "the disclosure surface is exactly what git publishes," and `.mjs` is in `SCAN_EXT` (line 43) — an untracked file discloses nothing and would be scanned the moment it is added. (3) The rotation is not an unreviewed-only-implementation: the procedure is tracked in `docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md` §1.1c. Note also that committing the script leaks no new secret — the webhook URL it references already appears in tracked docs at `docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md:52,147,210`.

> **Correction.** Four parts of the reasoning are wrong. (1) The 2.3 GB claim is false: `git add -A --dry-run` stages exactly 52 paths totalling ~1 MB, and `grep -c worktrees` over that output returns 0. The 2.3 GB lives in `.claude/worktrees/`, whose five directories are registered worktrees of this same repo (`git worktree list` lists all five), each carrying a `.git` file — git never stages a nested worktree's contents, so `git add -A` cannot sweep them in. (2) The title's "deletes and recreates" is wrong: the script creates a replacement alongside the old endpoint, probes it, and only then POSTs `disabled: "true"` (line 185, commented "kept, not deleted — rollback is one toggle"), and it refuses to retire the old endpoint at all if the probe fails (lines 172-179). (3) "Its read-only counterparts are committed; the write half is not" misstates the split — `provision-sandbox.mjs` is committed and writes products, prices and webhook endpoints; the real distinction is that it hard-refuses `sk_live_` at line 66 while the untracked script accepts live. (4) "Invisible to the rule-6 guard" inverts that guard's stated purpose: `no-commercial-terms.test.ts:180-186` documents the `git ls-files` choice deliberately ("The disclosure surface is exactly what git publishes"), an untracked file discloses nothing, and it enters the scan the instant it is `git add`ed — and the script contains no markup, cost basis, or margin math regardless. "Live-money rotation" also overstates: no money moves, and the probe is a synthetic unhandled event type routed to the handler's no-op default branch.

**Impact.** The only implementation of a live-money rotation was never reviewed, is not in the public repo, is invisible to the rule-6 guard (`web/__tests__/infra/no-commercial-terms.test.ts:189` enumerates via `git ls-files -z`, so untracked files are never scanned), and one `git clean -fd` destroys it. It sits among eleven untracked entries — `.agents/ .claude/ .continue/ .design-sync/ .factory/ .kiro/ 'Quantum Learner Design System.zip' results.json skills-lock.json uv.lock` — of which `git check-ignore` matches exactly zero, so a `git add -A` would also sweep 2.3 GB of `.claude/` (five full repo worktrees, each with its own copy of CLAUDE.md and the billing runbooks) into a public repository. This is the prior audit's "nine untracked entries, none in .gitignore" finding, still open and now eleven.

**Evidence.** Script header: "Recreate a Stripe webhook endpoint with the full required event set and a pinned API version, rotate its signing secret into Secrets Manager, recycle the function, and PROVE the new secret is live before retiring the old endpoint." and `--expect-account $STRIPE_ACCOUNT ... --confirm-live` (lines 29-35), importing `REQUIRED_WEBHOOK_EVENTS` from `../../lambda/stripe/index.mjs` (line 40). `git check-ignore -q` returns non-zero for all eleven untracked paths; `du -sh .claude` = 2.3G.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The untracked-file fact reproduces exactly: `git status --porcelain` shows `?? scripts/stripe/rotate-webhook-endpoint.mjs`, `git log --all --diff-filter=A --name-only | grep -c rotate-webhook` returns 0 (never tracked on any branch), `git check-ignore -q` returns non-zero for all eleven untracked paths, and `du -sh .claude` = 2.3G (5 worktrees, 5 CLAUDE.md copies). The script does mutate real state — `secretsmanager put-secret-value` (line 121), `lambda update-function-configuration` (line 133) — so an unreviewed, one-`git clean -fd`-from-gone automation is a genuine hygiene defect. But nothing is broken or exposed now, and the procedure itself is neither unreviewed nor absent from the public repo: `docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md:134` (§1.1c) documents the same create-pinned-with-nine-events → rotate-secret → disable-not-delete sequence, which is what `check-webhook-parity.mjs:119` cites as the remedy. Losing the file loses automation, not institutional knowledge, so I lowered medium to low.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The underlying fact is real: `git ls-files scripts/stripe/` returns only check-catalog-parity, check-webhook-parity, e2e-sandbox and provision-sandbox, while `rotate-webhook-endpoint.mjs` shows as `??`, and `git check-ignore -q` returns non-zero for all eleven untracked paths (no .gitignore rule covers any of them). The script is genuinely live-capable — it accepts an `sk_live_` key behind `--confirm-live` (line 66), POSTs a new webhook endpoint (line 108), pipes the signing secret to `aws secretsmanager put-secret-value` (lines 118-127) and forces `update-function-configuration` (lines 131-137) — and it is the only tool in the tree that can repin `api_version` and restore the full nine-event set on the live endpoint that CLAUDE.md records as subscribed to 4 of 9. It hardcodes no secret (grep for `sk_live_|whsec_|AKIA` returns nothing) and no tracked file references it (`git grep rotate-webhook-endpoint` is empty), so nothing is exposed and no clone is broken — the exposure is loss and lack of review, which is hygiene, not a live defect. I rated it low rather than medium because the finder's headline consequence is off by roughly 2300x, per the correction.

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*

#### `f975caec6` CI tests every Lambda on Node 20 while all ten deployed functions run nodejs22.x

`.github/workflows/ci.yml:28`

**low** · Partly true · correctness · observed live · fix plan 10

**Claim.** `NODE_VERSION: "20"` drives `actions/setup-node` for the `web`, `lambdas` and `build-smoke` jobs, and the CodeBuild mirror pins `nodejs: 20` too. Every SAM template declares `Runtime: nodejs22.x`, and `aws lambda list-functions` confirms all ten deployed functions are `nodejs22.x`. Nothing derives one number from the other; ci.yml:16 says to keep the pins "aligned with the Amplify build image (Amplify console ...)", i.e. the authoritative value lives outside the repo entirely.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the reasoning overreach. First, the risk direction is narrower than "Node 20 -> 22 changed node --test defaults, fetch/undici, and the OpenSSL/webcrypto surface": code using Node-22-only syntax or APIs makes CI **fail loudly** on Node 20, it does not pass silently. The only silent failure mode is a 20->22 behavior change or removal affecting code that already works on 20 — a far smaller surface than the three subsystems named, and empty today. Second, `NODE_VERSION: "20"` is not simply wrong. For the `web` and `build-smoke` jobs it is correctly anchored to the Amplify build image, which is genuinely their deploy target, so ci.yml:16's comment is right for two of its three consumers. The defect is that a single env var serves two deploy targets with different runtimes, and only the `lambdas` job is mismatched — the fix is to split the pin (a separate `LAMBDA_NODE_VERSION: "22"`), not to bump the shared one, which would silently move the web/static-export jobs off the Amplify image they are meant to mirror.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the framing overstate it. First, `NODE_VERSION` is not simply a wrong number to bump: `ci.yml:16` instructs that it track the *Amplify build image*, and it feeds `web` and `build-smoke`, where Node 20 is plausibly correct. The defect is that one variable serves two different authorities (Amplify's image and Lambda's runtime); the fix is a separate pin for the `lambdas` matrix, not aligning the shared one. Second, the Stripe mechanism is wrong — `lambda/stripe/index.mjs:884` verifies signatures via `stripe.webhooks.constructEventAsync` (HMAC-SHA256 through `node:crypto`), and Node 20 and 22 both ship OpenSSL 3.0.x, so there is no "OpenSSL/webcrypto surface" difference there. The risk is also asymmetric: Node-22-only syntax or APIs would turn CI red immediately rather than ship silently, so the only escaping class is code that works on 20 and breaks on 22.

**Impact.** The `lambdas` job is the one CI calls load-bearing — "the qpu Lambda's tests guard the real-money spend ledger ... and the stripe Lambda's tests guard the credit wallet ... nothing else in CI executes either" — yet it never exercises the runtime those handlers ship to. Node 20 -> 22 changed `node --test` defaults, `fetch`/undici, and the OpenSSL/webcrypto surface that Stripe signature verification touches. I grepped for Node-22-only syntax and found none today, so this is a latent gate weakness rather than an active bug; it is cheap to close and its absence means green tests are a claim about a runtime nobody deploys.

**Evidence.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:28` `NODE_VERSION: "20"`, consumed at lines 89, 132, 158. `Runtime: nodejs22.x` in lambda/stripe/template.yaml:134, lambda/qpu/template.yaml:179, lambda/sync/template.yaml:99, lambda/tutor/template.yaml:126, lambda/review-email/template.yaml:76. `infra/ci-standby/template.yaml:166` `nodejs: 20`. Live: `aws lambda list-functions` -> all ten `quantum-*` functions `nodejs22.x`. Note drift.yml:52 uses node 22, so the repo already disagrees with itself.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, observed live.** Every factual claim reproduces. `.github/workflows/ci.yml:28` pins `NODE_VERSION: "20"` (consumed at 89/132/158), `infra/ci-standby/template.yaml:166` mirrors `nodejs: 20`, all nine `Runtime:` lines across the five SAM templates say `nodejs22.x`, and `aws lambda list-functions --query "Functions[?starts_with(FunctionName,'quantum')].[FunctionName,Runtime]" --output text` returned all ten `quantum-*` functions as `nodejs22.x`. No guard closes it: `grep -rn -i runtime scripts/check-lambda-drift.mjs` returns nothing, so `make drift` never compares runtimes. The repo corroborates it harder than the finder noticed — `lambda/tutor/template.yaml:125` records "nodejs20.x reached end-of-life 2026-04-30 (no security patches)", so the load-bearing money-path job runs on an EOL Node major. I hold it at low rather than medium because nothing is broken today: `grep -rEn "process\.loadEnvFile|Promise\.withResolvers|Object\.groupBy|Map\.groupBy|glob\(" lambda/*/*.mjs` found no Node-22-only API, matching the finder's own "latent gate weakness rather than an active bug."

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, observed live.** The facts check out: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:28` pins `NODE_VERSION: "20"` (consumed at lines 89, 132, 158), `infra/ci-standby/template.yaml:166` mirrors `nodejs: 20`, all nine `Runtime:` lines across the five SAM templates say `nodejs22.x`, and `aws lambda list-functions --query 'Functions[?starts_with(FunctionName,`quantum`)].[FunctionName,Runtime]'` returned ten `quantum-*` functions, every one `nodejs22.x`. Nothing in the repo derives one pin from the other and no test asserts the alignment (grepped `web/__tests__ scripts tests Makefile` for `nodejs22|nodejs20|NODE_VERSION` — zero hits), so the gate genuinely never exercises the shipped runtime. But it is latent only, and the finder's own grep already said so: I ran `cd lambda/stripe && npm test` (74 pass) and `cd lambda/qpu && npm test` (88 pass) on local Node v26.3.0, four majors past production, with no failures. Given no active bug, and the money surfaces these suites guard being deliberately dormant (metering off, storefront closed), this is CI hygiene, not a medium.

*Raised as medium, published at low — the verification pass set this.*

## 8. Python, qcsim, and the curriculum

6 findings — 3 medium, 3 low. 88 files read.

### How it actually works

The Python half is three layers. `qcsim/` is a pure-NumPy Braket stand-in whose gates all originate in one registry, `_GATE_SPECS` (`qcsim/src/qcsim/circuits.py:124`), carrying matrix, Braket-capitalized name, render glyphs and constructor method per row; `_spec()` fails closed on an unregistered label. State evolution is `np.tensordot` + `moveaxis` over a register compacted to the distinct used qubits, mirroring Braket's `qubit_count`. `LocalSimulator.run` samples from a module-private `np.random.Generator` (`devices.py:30`) so `np.random.seed` does not make browser histograms reproducible — matching real Braket. I fuzzed 400 random circuits (1–4 qubits, all 15 gates, descending qubit order, partial `target_mapping`, `adjoint`) against the installed `amazon-braket-sdk` 1.125.0: state vectors agree elementwise to 1e-10 and `qubit_count`, `depth`, `operator.name` and instruction `target` agree on every case. Zero divergences.

`lib/` is the curriculum library: `circuits/common.py` (QFT in the `+2πijk/N` convention — I rebuilt the unitary for n=1..4 and it matches to 2e-15), `chemistry/ansatz.py` (a genuinely particle-conserving Givens rotation), `ml/feature_maps.py` (a correct Möttönen amplitude encoder — 200 random vectors reproduce `f/‖f‖` to 2e-13), `utils/cost.py` (`PRICING`, verified today against the live AWS Braket pricing page: every rate matches), and `hardware/devices.py`, whose `DEVICES` table is the single source for ARNs, gate-paradigm, the QPU allowlist and per-device shot bounds — those bounds also match the live Braket quotas page exactly.

The exercise machinery is the strongest part. `docs/exercise-convention.md` defines a three-cell unit; `tests/test_exercise_checks.py` parses structure, requires a `tests/solutions/<section>/<stem>.py` per notebook, then executes each notebook twice in a subprocess kernel — once with solutions injected under `QL_GRADING_STRICT=1`, once untouched. I reproduced both runs in-process for all 45 notebooks: solved, every check prints `correct.`; unsolved, every check prints `not attempted yet`.

The weak seam is everything outside `lib/` and `qcsim/`: the seven `NN-*/scripts/` directories are advertised in the GUIDEs, imported by no notebook and covered by no test, and that is where the real defects live — a Grover oracle that silently degrades to the identity above three qubits, a VQE expectation loop missing the bitstring-column guard its hardened twin in `06-hybrid-jobs/algorithms/` documents, and GUIDE inventories naming functions that were never written.

### Done well

- qcsim/src/qcsim/circuits.py:124 — `_GATE_SPECS` makes gate coverage structural rather than hand-maintained, and it holds up under real testing: I fuzzed 400 random circuits against the installed amazon-braket-sdk 1.125.0 and found zero divergences in state vector (atol 1e-10), qubit_count, depth, operator.name or instruction target, including non-contiguous registers (h(0).cnot(0,2) -> measured_qubits [0,2] on both) and partial target_mapping.
- lib/utils/cost.py:9-20 — every rate in PRICING matches the live AWS Braket pricing page as of today: IonQ Forte $0.30/task + $0.08/shot, IQM Garnet $0.30 + $0.00145, QuEra Aquila $0.30 + $0.01, Rigetti Cepheus $0.30 + $0.000425, SV1 $0.075/min. This is a verified cost basis, not a self-consistent one.
- lib/hardware/devices.py:60-69 — the per-device shot-bound comment cites the Braket quotas page and is correct against it today: SV1/DM1/Rigetti 50,000, IQM Garnet/Emerald 20,000, QuEra Aquila 1,000, IonQ minimum 100 on-demand and 2,500 for error mitigation, TN1 undocumented (hence the MAX_SHOTS backstop).
- tests/test_exercise_checks.py:273 — `test_checks_do_not_pass_unsolved` is a real gate, and the contract it guards actually holds. I executed all 45 notebooks unsolved and every single check cell reported `not attempted yet`; re-executed with tests/solutions/** injected, every check reported `correct.`. The fresh-names rule in docs/exercise-convention.md is being followed in practice, not just written down.
- tests/test_gate_fixture.py:80 — `test_fixture_covers_every_registered_gate` compares the committed gates.json against `_GATE_SPECS` itself rather than against the generator's own list, which is what makes the coverage claim non-circular. I re-ran both generators: gates.json and cost.json are byte-identical to a fresh build, so both two-sided drift locks are green for real.
- lib/utils/statevector.py:73-84 — the one genuine qcsim/real-SDK semantic divergence is handled correctly: instructions are replayed onto a fresh circuit (so the caller's circuit and any result types they attached are untouched) and the amplitudes are selected by result-type identity rather than by index. scripts/validate_runnable.py:100 denylists the raw `.state_vector()` call in browser-runnable notebooks, and tests/test_notebook_real_sdk.py executes a sample under the real SDK to catch the qcsim-only idiom.
- lib/chemistry/ansatz.py:117-129 — the UCCSD singles block is a correct Givens rotation, verified by hand on all four basis states: |00> and |11> are fixed, |10> -> cos(t/2)|10> + sin(t/2)|01>. The docstring's claim that the outer cnot(virt,occ) pair is what makes it particle-conserving is accurate, and it is honest that singles recover zero correlation energy for STO-3G H2 (-1.116151 vs -1.137117 Ha, which matches the committed h2_dissociation.json fixture at R=0.75).
- tests/test_qpu_devices.py:50-80 — a genuine cross-language guard: it parses lambda/qpu/qpu-core.mjs and asserts DEVICE is the cheapest allowlisted gate device, DEVICE_ARN equals DEVICES[device]['arn'], DEVICE_REGION is derived from that ARN, LIFETIME_CAP_MICROS is exactly 0, and the string `effectiveCap > 0` is still present. Nothing at runtime links the Python cost model to the Node ledger, and this is the only thing standing in the gap.

### Findings

#### `fe61f70c2` ~~grover_oracle and grover_diffusion silently become the identity for 4 or more qubits, so Grover's algorithm returns a uniform distribution with no error~~

`03-algorithms/scripts/oracles.py:63-69`

**medium** · Confirmed · correctness · ran locally · **split verdict** · **resolved 2026-08-18** · fix plan 12

> **Resolved 2026-08-18.** Fixed in the working tree (uncommitted): _apply_multi_controlled_z in 03-algorithms/scripts/oracles.py now handles every n >= 1 — Z / H-CNOT-H / H-CCNOT-H fast paths plus an exact phase-polynomial decomposition (a CNOT parity ladder and one Rz per non-empty qubit subset) for n >= 4. Verified by an independent adversarial pass that re-derived the decomposition and probed to n = 7 (max deviation 2e-15 from the exact diagonal, all 16 marked states correct at n = 4, n = 2/3 gate sequences byte-identical to before). Full Grover now amplifies to P(marked) = 1.0 / 0.947 / 0.963 / 0.999 at n = 2..5. New tests/test_oracles.py (15 tests) pins the unitary contract and the end-to-end amplification.

**Claim.** Both functions implement the multi-controlled Z as `H(n-1) ... H(n-1)` with only two hardcoded middle branches. There is no `else`, so for n_qubits >= 4 (and n_qubits == 1) nothing is placed between the two Hadamards, H*H cancels, the X flips are applied and undone, and both circuits reduce to the identity. The comment on the line directly above asserts the opposite of what the code does.

> **Correction.** The defect is real and reproduces, but it is not on the curriculum's Grover path, which the "Python, qcsim, and the curriculum" framing implies. `02-grovers-search.ipynb` never imports this module (`grep` over its source: no `oracles` reference; it pins `n = 3` and defines its own `apply_ccz` with an inline `assert np.allclose(_amps, expected)` that verifies the CCZ acts as diag(1,...,-1)). No notebook, and no file under `tests/`, imports `oracles.py` (0 hits). `03-algorithms/scripts/` is also not copied into `web/jupyterlite-build/files/` — only `notebooks/` and `lib/` ship — so the browser lab cannot reach it at all. The exposure is confined to a learner who reads the `scripts/oracles.py` line in `GUIDE.md:359` / `GUIDE.es.md:359` and imports the orphan helper locally, plus the public repo shipping visibly wrong quantum code. One symptom the finder missed strengthens it: the returned circuit's `qubit_count` silently collapses to 2 at n=4, so it does not even span the requested register.

**Impact.** A learner following the module's own advertised utility (`03-algorithms/GUIDE.md:359`, mirrored in GUIDE.es.md:359) builds a 4-qubit Grover search, runs it, and gets a flat histogram with no amplification and no exception, no warning, and no clue that the library did nothing. Nothing in `tests/` imports this module, so CI cannot see it.

**Evidence.** ```
    # Multi-controlled Z (implemented as H-MCX-H on last qubit)
    circuit.h(n_qubits - 1)
    # For 3+ qubits, decompose into Toffoli + CNOT
    if n_qubits == 2:
        circuit.cnot(0, 1)
    elif n_qubits == 3:
        circuit.ccnot(0, 1, 2)
    circuit.h(n_qubits - 1)
```
Identical block at lines 98-104 in `grover_diffusion`. Measured by running the full algorithm under qcsim: n=3, marked "101", 2 iterations -> P(marked)=0.9453; n=4, marked "1011", 3 iterations -> P(marked)=0.0625 (exactly uniform 1/16); n=5, 4 iterations -> P(marked)=0.03125 (exactly uniform 1/32).

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, medium, ran locally.** The code is exactly as quoted: `03-algorithms/scripts/oracles.py:63-69` and `:98-104` have `if n_qubits == 2 / elif n_qubits == 3` with no `else`, so the H...H sandwich cancels for n>=4. I reproduced it on the real Braket LocalSimulator with `./.venv/bin/python scratchpad/verify_grover.py` (imports the module directly, runs full Grover): n=3 marked "101" -> P=0.9453; n=4 "1011" -> P=0.0625 (=1/16, exactly uniform); n=5 -> P=0.03125 (=1/32) — matching the finder's numbers. A second run printed the n=4 oracle's instruction list as literally `['X','H','H','X']` with `qubit_count == 2`, and applying it to the uniform state leaves all 16 amplitudes at +0.25, i.e. the identity; n=2 at its optimal single iteration gives P=1.0, so the n==2 branch is genuinely correct.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Confirmed, low, read.** Source is exactly as quoted: `03-algorithms/scripts/oracles.py:61-69` and `:96-104` place only `if n_qubits == 2: cnot` / `elif n_qubits == 3: ccnot` between two Hadamards on qubit n-1, with no `else`, so every other n leaves H·H = I with the X flips self-cancelling. I reproduced it with `.venv/bin/python` scripts in my scratchpad (`grover_check.py`, `g3.py`): `Circuit.to_unitary()` on `grover_oracle`/`grover_diffusion` returns exact identity for n=1, 4 and 5 (and for n>=4 with an all-ones marked state the oracle circuit collapses to a single qubit, 2x2), while n=2 and n=3 are correct — full Grover on LocalSimulator gives P(marked)=0.9453 at n=3 and exactly 1/16 and 1/32 at n=4 and n=5. The comments overclaim in two places ("Multi-controlled Z" and "For 3+ qubits, decompose into Toffoli + CNOT" above a branch that only handles exactly 3). Severity is low rather than medium only because the module has zero call sites: nothing in `tests/`, no notebook (`03-algorithms/notebooks/02-grovers-search.ipynb` defines its own `apply_oracle` at n=3), and it is not shipped into the browser bundle (`web/jupyterlite-build/files/03-algorithms/` contains `notebooks` only) — the exposure is a reader of the public repo who takes `03-algorithms/GUIDE.md:359` ("Reusable functions...") at its word.

*Split verdict: reproduces said Partly true; correct as written said Confirmed. This one needs a human.*

#### `f1f6e3e71` ~~scripts/validate_runnable.py deletes any line beginning with % — including a printf-format continuation — which makes the cell unparseable and silently exempts it from the qcsim contract scan~~

`scripts/validate_runnable.py:150`

**medium** · Confirmed · correctness · ran locally · **split verdict** · **resolved 2026-08-18** · fix plan 12

> **Resolved 2026-08-18.** Fixed in the working tree (uncommitted): _strip_magics strips only genuine magics (sigil followed by an identifier character), so printf continuations survive, and an unparseable cell is now a contract violation rather than a stderr warning. All 32 runnable notebooks scan clean — the previously exempt 07-excited-states cell 10 now parses and passes — and content-manifest.json is byte-identical. An adversarial pass probed 23 IPython constructs (fail-closed on the exotic ones, none of which appear in any notebook) and confirmed a forbidden import hidden behind an unparseable construct is now flagged. New tests/test_validate_runnable.py (6 tests).

**Claim.** `_strip_magics` filters out every line whose first non-whitespace character is `%` or `!`, intending to drop IPython magics. A continuation line of a multi-line `print("..." % (...))` starts with `%` and is deleted too. `scan_notebook` then catches the resulting SyntaxError, prints a warning to stderr, and `continue`s past the whole cell; `main()` still returns 0 and `--check` still passes.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the framing overstate it. (1) Not "silent": the skip prints a warning to stderr on every run and is a documented deliberate choice — the comment at scripts/validate_runnable.py:177-184 says "the execution test is the backstop. Surface as a warning, not a hard violation." (2) The impact list is too broad. `tests/test_notebook_contract.py::test_runnable_notebook_executes_under_qcsim` executes every marked notebook under qcsim, so a denylisted import or a forbidden name hidden in this cell would still fail there — I confirmed pennylane is absent from both `.venv/bin/python` and `python3`, so `import pennylane` raises, and `AwsDevice` raises NameError. The class that genuinely evades every guard is the divergent `.state_vector()` call: it succeeds under qcsim, and `tests/test_notebook_real_sdk.py` samples only 3 notebooks (deutsch-jozsa, data-encoding, vqe-h2) — 07-excited-states is not among them.

**Impact.** `05-quantum-chemistry/notebooks/07-excited-states.ipynb` is marked `<!-- browser-runnable -->` and its cell 10 is currently unscanned. Any denylisted import (`pennylane`, `braket.aws`), forbidden name (`AwsDevice`), or divergent `.state_vector()` call placed in a cell with this shape passes the static gate unnoticed, and `_notebook_runnable()` still stamps the notebook `runnable: true` into content-manifest.json, which is what gates the portal's green Run button.

**Evidence.** `kept = [ln for ln in lines if not ln.lstrip().startswith(("%", "!"))]` (line 150), then lines 177-184 `except SyntaxError as exc: print(f"  warning: ... did not parse ..."); continue`. Running `python scripts/validate_runnable.py` today prints: `warning: 07-excited-states.ipynb cell 10 did not parse ('(' was never closed); relying on execution test` (twice — once in the scan loop, once in the manifest build) and exits 0. The offending source is `print("spectral gap          = %.6f   (exact %.6f)"` / `      % (E_excited_ssvqe - E_ground_ssvqe, ...))`.

**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, medium, ran locally.** Reproduced: `python3 scripts/validate_runnable.py` prints `warning: 07-excited-states.ipynb cell 10 did not parse ('(' was never closed); relying on execution test` twice and exits 0; the source is the `print("spectral gap = %.6f ..."` / `      % (...))` continuation at 05-quantum-chemistry/notebooks/07-excited-states.ipynb cell 10. scripts/validate_runnable.py:150 `kept = [ln for ln in lines if not ln.lstrip().startswith(("%", "!"))]` deletes that continuation, and scan_notebook:177-184 swallows the SyntaxError with `continue`, so the whole cell is exempt; I confirmed with a node-equivalent python3 heredoc that injecting `import pennylane`, `circuit.state_vector()` and `AwsDevice(...)` into a cell of that exact shape yields zero violations, and `content-manifest.json` still carries `{'filename': '07-excited-states.ipynb', 'runnable': True}`. The named backstops do not close it: tests/test_notebook_real_sdk.py:27 samples only 3 notebooks (this is not one), and .github/workflows/ci.yml:50 installs `.[dev,full]`, so `import pennylane`/`openfermion` succeed under CPython in CI — making this static scan the sole gate for both the denylisted-import and the `.state_vector()` divergence classes here. Medium, not high: cell 10's actual content is clean numpy/print today, so nothing user-facing is broken now — this is a latent hole in a CI gate triggered by a common `%`-format idiom.

I proved the false-negative by copying the notebook to the scratchpad, appending `import pennylane`, `AwsDevice(...)` and `circuit.state_vector()` to that same cell, and calling `vr.scan_notebook` on both it and a control cell without the `%` line: the poisoned cell returned `[]`, the control returned all three violations. *Split verdict: reproduces said Confirmed; correct as written said Partly true. This one needs a human.*

#### `f8e75412a` ~~Two GUIDE script inventories advertise capabilities the shipped scripts do not contain, in both locales~~

**Claim.** The `03-algorithms` script inventory promises `variational_utils.py` wraps "COBYLA, SPSA, Adam"; the file (104 lines) defines exactly `optimize_cobyla` and `optimize_spsa` and contains no Adam and no logging. The `05-quantum-chemistry` inventory promises `vqe_runner.py` is an "End-to-end VQE runner with energy vs. geometry scanning"; the file defines a single `run_vqe(qubit_hamiltonian, ansatz_fn, ...)` that takes a pre-built Hamiltonian and has no geometry parameter, no bond-length loop and no scan of any kind.

> **Correction.** Two qualifiers on the finder's framing. (1) "no logging" is an overreach — `variational_utils.py` returns a per-iteration `history` list and accepts an optional `callback(params, cost)` in both optimizers, which is a fair reading of "with logging"; the load-bearing defect is the absent Adam, not the logging. (2) The failure is misdirection, not a live break: nothing in the repo imports `optimize_adam` or calls for a scan, so no shipped notebook errors today. I checked the strongest refutation — that this "Scripts:" list is an aspirational roadmap rather than an inventory — and it does not hold: every notebook listed above it exists, `oracles.py` matches its description exactly, and `hamiltonians.py`/`ansatz.py` are re-export shims whose descriptions are backed by real `lib/chemistry/` implementations. Only these two entries overclaim.

> **Correction.** Two parts of the framing overstate it. (1) "no Adam and no logging" — the logging half is wrong: both wrappers append a per-iteration `{params, cost}` record to `history` and invoke an optional `callback(params, cost)` each iteration (variational_utils.py:26-31, 88-91), and return `history` in the result dict, so "with logging" is an accurate description. Only "Adam" is false. (2) "End-to-end VQE runner" is likewise accurate for `run_vqe` — only the trailing "with energy vs. geometry scanning" clause is false. Each line is one bad clause inside an otherwise correct description, not a wholesale fabricated capability. The geometry scan also exists in the curriculum, just not in the script: `05-quantum-chemistry/notebooks/04-vqe-lih.ipynb` and `08-hybrid-chemistry-job.ipynb` both loop over `bond_lengths`, so a learner following the module (rather than the one-line script blurb) does reach a real scan.

Both statements are duplicated verbatim in the Spanish GUIDEs (03-algorithms/GUIDE.es.md:360, 05-quantum-chemistry/GUIDE.es.md:328), and `tests/test_notebook_links.py` only walks notebook markdown cells, so nothing checks a GUIDE's claims against the scripts it names.
