**Verifier (reproduces) — Confirmed, low, ran locally.** Both claims reproduce at the exact cited lines. `03-algorithms/GUIDE.md:360` advertises "COBYLA, SPSA, Adam"; `03-algorithms/scripts/variational_utils.py` (104 lines) defines only `optimize_cobyla:7` and `optimize_spsa:47`, and `grep -rniE "optimize_adam|adam" --include="*.py" --include="*.md" --include="*.ipynb" .` returns zero real hits repo-wide (every match is a substring of "Hadamard"). `05-quantum-chemistry/GUIDE.md:328` advertises "energy vs. geometry scanning"; `vqe_runner.py` defines only `run_vqe(qubit_hamiltonian, ...)` :8 plus two private helpers (`grep -n "^def " 05-quantum-chemistry/scripts/vqe_runner.py`) — no geometry arg, no loop, and `grep -rn "def .*scan" lib/` finds no scan helper anywhere. #### `f39a705fb` ~~vqe_runner indexes the raw measurement bitstring by absolute qubit index with no measured-qubits validation, the exact hazard its hardened twin routes through parse_counts to prevent~~

`05-quantum-chemistry/scripts/vqe_runner.py:104`

**low** · Partly true · correctness · ran locally · **resolved 2026-08-18** · fix plan 12

> **Resolved 2026-08-18.** Fixed in the working tree (uncommitted): vqe_runner.py routes counts through lib.utils.results.parse_counts (the same construction as its hardened twin), validates the ansatz spans exactly 0..n-1 with an instructive error, and rejects out-of-range Hamiltonian terms before the optimizer spends anything. An adversarial pass probed every mislabeling scenario on the live LocalSimulator — all fail loudly now, legitimate paths compute exactly, and the measurement-rotation widening path cannot re-open the hazard because term qubits are validated at entry. New tests/test_vqe_runner.py (5 tests); the end-to-end assertion was rerun 27x with zero flakes.

**Claim.** `_expectation_from_pauli_counts` reads `bitstring[q]` where `q` is a Pauli-term qubit index, but the bitstring's column j is the j-th qubit of Braket's COMPACTED measured register, not qubit j. `run_vqe` takes `ansatz_fn` from the caller and never checks that the ansatz spans qubits 0..n_qubits-1. The sibling implementation of the identical loop in `06-hybrid-jobs/algorithms/vqe_chemistry_job.py:70-79` goes through `lib.utils.results.parse_counts` precisely because, in its own words, "indexing a key by absolute qubit index is valid only when the measured qubits are exactly 0..n-1 in order".

> **Correction.** The finder's named trigger is wrong: `uccsd_singles_circuit` cannot skip a qubit. `itertools.product(occupied, virtual)` over `occupied=0..n_e-1` / `virtual=n_e..n_q-1` touches every index in 0..n-1, and `hardware_efficient_ansatz` loops `range(n_qubits)` with `n_qubits>=1`/`n_layers>=1` guards. I proved this exhaustively for n_qubits 2..6 and all valid n_electrons — both repo builders always span 0..n-1. So no in-repo caller can trigger the bug; it requires a learner-written frozen-core/active-space ansatz, and nothing in the repo imports or tests `vqe_runner` (the `run_vqe` in notebook 08 is an unrelated local line-search toy). It is a latent hazard in a GUIDE-advertised teaching script, not a live miscomputation.

> **Correction.** The finder's concrete triggers are wrong for this repo. `uccsd_singles_circuit` cannot skip a qubit at any physical electron count: its Givens block emits `cnot(virt, occ)`/`ry(virt,·)`/`cnot(occ, virt)` for every occupied-virtual pair (lib/chemistry/ansatz.py:117-129), so I verified across n_qubits 2..6 that its qubit span is exactly 0..n-1 for every n_electrons >= 1 — the only gap is the degenerate n_electrons=0 (empty circuit). `hardware_efficient_ansatz` likewise loops `range(n_qubits)` unconditionally. Active-space/frozen-core reduction in this curriculum yields a smaller Hamiltonian over 0..n-1, not an index-skipping ansatz. So this is a latent contract gap reachable only by a learner-supplied `ansatz_fn` (which the docstring constrains not at all), not a bug any shipped ansatz triggers today.

**Impact.** An ansatz that leaves any qubit untouched (a frozen-core or active-space ansatz, or `uccsd_singles_circuit` with an occupied/virtual split that skips a qubit) makes every Pauli expectation read the wrong column and the reported ground-state energy is silently wrong; a high-index term raises a bare IndexError instead. Nothing imports or tests this module, and `05-quantum-chemistry/GUIDE.md:328` advertises it to learners.

**Evidence.** `counts = result.measurement_counts` (line 62 — the raw Counter, bypassing parse_counts) and `parity = sum(int(bitstring[q]) for q in relevant_qubits) % 2` (line 104). Contrast `vqe_chemistry_job.py:74` `counts = parse_counts(result)`, whose comment at lines 70-73 names this exact failure mode: "parse_counts owns the bitstring-column contract this loop depends on ... and raises when they are not instead of silently mislabelling every outcome."

**Verifier (reproduces) — Partly true, low, read.** The core defect reproduces exactly. Ran `.venv/bin/python` against `05-quantum-chemistry/scripts/vqe_runner.py:95-108`: for a circuit spanning qubits {0,1,3}, Braket's LocalSimulator returns `measured_qubits == [0, 1, 3]` and 3-character keys, so `_expectation_from_pauli_counts(counts, ((2,"Z"),), 4)` returned **-1.0 where the true <Z2> is +1.0** (silently wrong), and a term on qubit 3 raised a bare `IndexError: string index out of range`. A full `run_vqe(H(), ansatz_skipping_a_qubit, n_qubits=4, ...)` call returned an energy with no warning. Line 62 does take `result.measurement_counts` raw, bypassing the `parse_counts` guard at `lib/utils/results.py:41-47` that the sibling `06-hybrid-jobs/algorithms/vqe_chemistry_job.py:70-74` routes through for exactly this reason.

**Verifier (correct as written) — Partly true, low, ran locally.** The defect at 05-quantum-chemistry/scripts/vqe_runner.py:104 is real and I reproduced both halves on LocalSimulator (script at scratchpad/probe.py, run with `.venv/bin/python .../probe.py`). Braket compacts the measured register — `Circuit().h(0).x(2)` returns `measured_qubits = [0, 2]` with width-2 keys — so with an ansatz touching only qubits {1,2}, term Z0 returned -1.0 where the truth is +1.0 (silent, no error), and with an ansatz on {0,1}, term Z2 raised a bare `IndexError: string index out of range`. Line 62 does take `result.measurement_counts` raw, bypassing `lib/utils/results.py:parse_counts`, whose docstring and `measured_qubits != range(n)` check (results.py:20-46) encode exactly the contract the sibling at 06-hybrid-jobs/algorithms/vqe_chemistry_job.py:70-79 routes through; `_expectation_from_pauli_counts` even accepts `n_qubits` and never uses it. No in-repo importer or test exists (grep for `from vqe_runner|import vqe_runner` outside .claude/worktrees returns nothing), and it is a curriculum script off every money path, so low.

## The fix plan

59 surviving findings, grouped into 12 pieces of work, ordered by what to do first.
One defect appearing in six files is one item of work, not six.

### 01. Stop shipping the "at cost, no markup" promise the code is built to break — effort S

This is the only cluster whose damage is live right now: the pricing page's meta description is what Google and every share card quote today, and rules 5/9 mean the kernel throws rather than price at cost. It is also the cheapest cluster in the audit (pure text plus test patterns), and you cannot reopen a storefront whose page states the opposite of what the server will debit.

1. Delete the 'at cost / no markup' clause from PAGE_DESCRIPTION in web/src/app/pricing/page.tsx (meta, og, twitter all derive from it) and replace with language that survives any deployed factor.
2. Rewrite the five QPU-panel strings (qpu-submit-panel.tsx lines 305, 313, 348, 355, 786) and hardware-panel.tsx:119 so no surface asserts the debit equals the raw Braket price.
3. Add /no markup|at cost|sin margen|al costo/ to the pricing-page copy-honesty ban list in both locales, so the phrase cannot return the way the sponsorship copy did.
4. Sweep README.md: replace the four Bedrock references, correct 'Four serverless backends' to five, add lambda/stripe to the architecture tree and table, and withdraw the line-7/line-34 sponsored-QPU headlines that were retired 2026-07-28.
5. Correct PRODUCT.md: drop the 'no verified Bedrock inference rates' storefront blocker (closed 2026-08-17), the 'real Bedrock token usage' metering sentence, and the 'charged at cost, the margin is the subscription' claim.

**First commit.** Remove the 'billed at cost with no markup' clause from PAGE_DESCRIPTION in web/src/app/pricing/page.tsx and add the phrase to the pricing-page copy-honesty ban list in both locales.

**Covers.** `f6ba1ef91` The pricing page's meta description and five shipped UI strings still promise 'at cost, no markup' — the mechanism that landed 2026-08-17 throws rather than charge at cost · `f5a2a88f1` The QPU panel tells the learner the credit debit is the AWS price converted at the peg — the exact formula the metering kernel is built to override · `f2d21e271` The public repo's front page and PRODUCT.md still sell a Bedrock tutor and name a blocker that CLAUDE.md records as closed · `fc5a367e4` README.md still describes the tutor as a Bedrock Lambda in four places and omits lambda/stripe entirely from the architecture it documents

### 02. Fix the three ways the wallet can be wrong in the learner's disfavour — effort M

Each of these silently produces a wrong balance in the direction rule 7 forbids: double reclaim on refund-then-dispute, a negative balance that reads as 'metering unconfigured' and permanently locks spending, and a cancellation that frees a still-paying subscriber to the free tier. None can fire while the storefront is closed, which is exactly the window in which to fix them — all three are offline-testable in lambda/stripe/index.test.mjs.

1. In reclaim(), bound the SUM of refundedCredits and disputedCredits by `purchased`: compute target against the remaining headroom (purchased minus the other counter's reclaimed amount), not against purchased alone. Add a refund-then-dispute test.
2. Move the zero-floor into the transaction: add a ConditionExpression on the wallet leg for the clawback path (mirroring qpu-core.mjs:445's `credits >= :need`) so a concurrent clawback fails and retries instead of writing a negative balance. Keep the shortfall accounting in clawbackOwedCredits.
3. Add a concurrency test that fires two clawbacks against different receipts on one wallet and asserts credits never goes below zero.
4. Gate /checkout on existing subscription state: refuse a second `mode: subscription` session when hasPaidTier(sub) is already true, and have the pricing page read wallet tier so CheckoutButton renders 'current plan' / 'manage' rather than a second buy.
5. Make customer.subscription.deleted conditional: only setTier free / setSubStatus canceled when no other active subscription remains for that customer.
6. Decide and record the upgrade path (Stripe subscription modification vs cancel-and-resubscribe) before either of the above ships.

**First commit.** In lambda/stripe/index.mjs reclaim(), cap the joint total of refundedCredits + disputedCredits at `purchased`, with a regression test covering full refund followed by charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn.

**Covers.** `f0187a23b` Refund and dispute clawbacks are each capped at the purchase but never capped jointly, so a refunded-then-disputed charge reclaims up to twice what was bought · `f3c1bf7e2` The clawback's zero-floor on the balance is computed from a read outside the transaction and written with no condition, so a concurrent clawback drives the wallet negative · `f552e4181` Nothing prevents a second concurrent subscription, and cancelling either one unconditionally resets the still-paying subscriber to the free tier

### 03. Make rule 14 true: actually buy something end to end, and fix the tooling that provisions the accounts — effort L

Rule 14 says buying must work before metering turns on, and today nothing exercises the buy. The sandbox harness hand-stamps the metadata rather than driving /checkout, so the one line that turns CATALOG credits into the granted number never runs; the migration runbook would rebuild the exact 4-of-9 webhook gap found in production; and the only live-money rotation script is untracked, unreviewed, and writes a key it never identifies.

1. Add a /checkout step to scripts/stripe/e2e-sandbox.mjs that posts a real CATALOG lookup key to the deployed sandbox handler, then asserts the created session carries the client_reference_id and the subscription_data.metadata.credits the CATALOG defines — replacing the hardcoded '1900'.
2. Add top-up and custom-top-up steps so price_data.product: CUSTOM_TOPUP_PRODUCT is actually purchased, proving the literal `ql_credits` product id exists.
3. Have check-catalog-parity.mjs assert the ql_credits product id alongside the prices and descriptions.
4. Commit scripts/stripe/rotate-webhook-endpoint.mjs, and make it verify the 1Password-sourced secret key's account with a GET /v1/account before writing it to Secrets Manager — refusing on any mismatch with --expect-account.
5. Correct the superseded sandbox account id in Makefile:35 and provision-sandbox.mjs's usage header to $STRIPE_ACCOUNT, matching e2e-sandbox.mjs (and fix the same id where CLAUDE.md is internally inconsistent).
6. Rewrite Phase 3.7 step 27 of docs/account-migration-runbook.md to call provision-sandbox.mjs / the API with all nine REQUIRED_WEBHOOK_EVENTS and a pinned api_version, and to finish with check-webhook-parity.mjs.
7. Add .gitignore entries for the eleven untracked working-tree paths (.agents/, .claude/, .continue/, .design-sync/, .factory/, .kiro/, the design-system zip, results.json, skills-lock.json, uv.lock) so no `git add -A` sweeps 2.3 GB and private runbooks into a public repo.

**First commit.** Add a step to scripts/stripe/e2e-sandbox.mjs that POSTs a CATALOG lookup key to the sandbox /checkout route and asserts the returned session's subscription_data metadata credits equals CATALOG[key].credits, replacing the hand-stamped literal.

**Covers.** `faecc93d8` The sandbox e2e harness never calls /checkout and hardcodes the grant, so the CATALOG-to-Stripe-metadata stamp and the custom top-up product remain unexercised end to end · `f9059a4d2` The account-migration runbook still prescribes 4 of the 9 required webhook events and no pinned api_version — it reproduces the exact production gap found on 2026-08-17 · `fe7c51b2f` The webhook-rotation script writes a 1Password-sourced API key into Secrets Manager without verifying which Stripe account that key belongs to, and the script is untracked in git · `fadbcc77f` A script that deletes and recreates the LIVE Stripe webhook endpoint and rotates its signing secret exists only as an untracked working-tree file · `fdcc00445` The Makefile and provision-sandbox.mjs both document the superseded Stripe sandbox account id, so the documented sandbox-provisioning command refuses against the real sandbox

### 04. One price, one source of truth — and a test that can actually see a divergence — effort M

Rule 8 asserts a lockstep test exists; it does not. Meanwhile three separate price tables ship: pricing.ts's published sheet, CATALOG's grants, and the QPU panel's raw AWS table — and they already disagree (197 vs 175 credits for a 1,000-shot IQM run, 1 vs 0 credits for a Haiku question). Every finding in cluster 1 recurs until a test compares the published sheet to what each server actually does.

1. Write an offline test that imports TIERS/HARDWARE_RATES from web/src/lib/pricing.ts and CATALOG from lambda/stripe/index.mjs and asserts credits and amounts match, wired into `npm test` and ci.yml — replacing the hand-copied literals in index.test.mjs:122-131.
2. Delete the duplicate hardware price table: have web/src/components/quantum/cost.ts derive IQM figures from HARDWARE_RATES (or have the panel quote the published sheet directly) so /pricing and the run surface cannot disagree.
3. Decide whether Haiku is free on paid tiers or costs the published 1 credit, then make the tutor handler and TUTOR_RATES agree, and add a test comparing typicalCreditsPerQuestion to what the handler would actually debit for a typical exchange.
4. Add the resulting cross-file assertions to the rule-13 guard set so a repricing that touches one side turns CI red rather than shipping a stale grant beside a new price.

**First commit.** Add web/__tests__/lib/catalog-parity.test.ts asserting every TIERS entry's monthlyCredits and price equals the corresponding CATALOG entry in lambda/stripe/index.mjs, and run it in `npm test`.

**Covers.** `fd662bc2b` CLAUDE.md rule 8 says a test asserts TIERS/CATALOG lockstep; no such test exists — the only real comparison needs a live Stripe key · `fe95b2089` The pricing page and the QPU run surface publish two different credit prices for the same IQM Garnet run · `fb40af359` The pricing page charges 1 credit per Haiku question; the server charges zero for Haiku on every tier, including Pro

### 05. Close two live AWS over-grants and the unfiltered failover webhook — effort S

These are cheap, deployed, and blast-radius-shaped. The drift role's own comment claims ListFunctions returns no environment secrets — it does, and EDGE_SECRET (the WAF bypass header) and UNSUB_SECRET are readable through it today, with RATE_CARD joining them at cutover. The failover webhook has no ACTOR_ACCOUNT_ID filter on a public repo, so engaging it during an outage hands any fork PR code execution under a role holding cognito-idp:ListUsers on the live learner directory.

1. Delete the lambda:ListFunctions statement from infra/github-oidc-drift-role.yaml — neither check-lambda-drift.mjs nor check-rate-parity.mjs calls it — and correct the false comment on the adjacent GetFunctionConfiguration grant.
2. Decide whether the drift role still needs GetFunctionConfiguration given it returns plaintext env values; if it does, scope it to the named quantum functions and record the accepted exposure explicitly rather than denying it.
3. Pin actions/checkout, actions/setup-node and aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials by commit SHA in .github/workflows/drift.yml.
4. Add ACTOR_ACCOUNT_ID (or GITHUB_ACTOR_ACCOUNT_ID) filter groups to the webhook created by infra/ci-standby/failover.sh engage(), and stop setting the commit-status app_id to -1.
5. Narrow the quantum-ci-standby-codebuild role: the founding-ten-verify cognito-idp:ListUsers grant does not need to be attached to the project that runs untrusted PR code.
6. Audit CloudTrail for prior reads of EDGE_SECRET/UNSUB_SECRET through these grants before deciding whether rotation is required.

**First commit.** Remove the account-wide lambda:ListFunctions statement from infra/github-oidc-drift-role.yaml and fix the comment that claims the adjacent grant returns no environment secrets.

**Covers.** `f50e54cb6` The CI drift role grants account-wide `lambda:ListFunctions` on a false premise — the API does return `Environment.Variables`, and nothing in the repo calls it · `fb854583f` The read-only OIDC drift role can read plaintext Lambda environment secrets, contradicting its own comment — and the workflow that assumes it pins every third-party action by mutable tag · `f66ea059e` `failover.sh engage` arms a CodeBuild webhook with no contributor filter on a PUBLIC repo, giving any fork PR code execution under a role that can list the live Cognito user pool · `f9805323f` The CodeBuild standby webhook has no actor filter, so on this public repo a fork PR would run arbitrary code with a role that can enumerate the production Cognito user pool — and post the commit status that IS the merge gate

### 06. Make the QPU spend surface tell the truth the server already knows — effort M

The founder recorded that the confirm step quotes a client-derived figure; what the audit adds is that the fix is already server-side and simply unparsed — creditsCharged, fundedBy and clawbackOwedCredits all arrive in responses the client discards. Until the panel renders the server's numbers, the RATE_CARD cutover cannot happen without the confirm screen understating every debit and the affordability gate approving runs the server will 402.

1. Extend SubmitOutcome and QpuTask in web/src/lib/qpu-client.ts to parse creditsCharged and fundedBy from the 202 and from taskSummary, and render the charged figure on the receipt and history rows.
2. Add clawbackOwedCredits to the Budget interface and getBudget() mapping, and surface a distinct 'account has outstanding debt' state instead of the generic insufficient-credits message.
3. Publish a server-computed quote (a priced pre-flight endpoint or a quote field on the budget response) and have qpu-submit-panel.tsx use it for both the displayed price and the walletCovers gate; remove the factor-less creditsForMicros from qpu-budget.ts once nothing depends on it.
4. Extend scripts/check-rate-parity.mjs to compare WALLET_TABLE alongside RATE_CARD across FUNCTIONS, so a deploy that meters one surface and leaves the other free cannot report MATCH; derive FUNCTIONS from the templates rather than a hand-maintained list.
5. Wrap the per-row body of lambda/qpu/reconcile.mjs's sweep in try/catch so one throttle, transaction conflict, or unset WALLET_TABLE cannot abandon every subsequent row, and always emit the summary log.
6. Only then rewrite the cutover runbook's Phase 4 acceptance gate to an assertion the arithmetic can satisfy (server quote == server debit, integers), removing the unreachable 50.3 figure.

**First commit.** Add creditsCharged and fundedBy to SubmitOutcome and QpuTask in web/src/lib/qpu-client.ts, parse them from the 202 and from history rows, and render the server's charged figure on the run receipt.

**Covers.** `fcb02bbf1` The wallet-funded confirm step quotes a client-derived credit figure while the server's authoritative charge, already returned on the 202, is discarded by the client · `fceac83b1` GET /qpu/budget publishes clawbackOwedCredits and no client code reads it, so a debt lockout still surfaces only as "insufficient credits" · `f251543c8` The deployed rate-card guard checks only RATE_CARD, so a deploy where one surface is metered and the other is free reports MATCH · `ff69eae9c` The reconcile sweep has no per-row error isolation, and wallet-funded rows widen what a single failure can abort · `f07ed1963` The cutover runbook's Phase 4 acceptance gate names 50.3 credits, a figure neither the client quote nor the server debit can ever produce

### 07. The tutor: an entire metered path that never runs, and a billing bug that would bill it wrong — effort L

AskTutor is mounted propless outside AuthWall, so the model picker, the auth header, the meta opt-in and the cost trailer are unreachable in production — the one surface that costs real money per use is the one the sign-up wall does not cover, with no tier gate and no free-trial counter. And when metering does turn on, a message_delta carrying null input legs would price generations at output-only while the alarm built for exactly that shape stays silent.

1. Null-guard each usage leg in the message_delta merge in lambda/tutor/index.mjs (mirror the SDK's own accumulator) and add a handler test where message_delta carries input_tokens: null; assert USAGE_SHAPE_UNRECOGNIZED fires rather than a zero-priced input leg.
2. Move <AskTutor /> inside AuthWall and pass wallet/authToken from useAuth, so the metered branch is reachable and the pill does not paint over the gate screen for signed-out visitors.
3. Implement the rule-2 free-trial counter (10 lifetime Haiku questions, its own counter, never a wallet grant) and gate the picker on tier; drop `free` from the tutor ROSTER.
4. Delete resolveModel and TIER_DEFAULT from tutor-billing.mjs along with the tests that pin the silent-downgrade policy the handler explicitly refuses — two contradictory policies in one module, one of them green, is how the wrong one gets 'restored'.
5. Correct RATES for sonnet-5 to the rate actually in force, or gate the entry on the introductory period; charging against a basis 50% above true cost is rule 7 inverted, not the safe direction.
6. Create the quantum-tutor secret and deploy the stack so git and the deployed function stop disagreeing, then exercise a real streamed request and observe the usage shape before trusting any of the above.

**First commit.** Null-guard input_tokens, cache_creation_input_tokens and cache_read_input_tokens in the message_delta usage merge in lambda/tutor/index.mjs, with a test asserting a null-carrying delta does not erase the message_start input legs.

**Covers.** `f04de5935` AskTutor is mounted propless outside AuthWall, so the entire metered client path is unreachable in the shipped app · `f07ce0f4d` The usage merge overwrites the input legs with nulls, pricing a generation at output-only, and readUsage's `?? 0` keeps the migration alarm silent · `f25112b4a` resolveModel/TIER_DEFAULT are dead code that encode the opposite policy from the handler, kept alive only by their own tests · `fcbbf15c1` RATES overstates sonnet-5's current cost, and the comment labels the resulting overcharge "the safe direction"

### 08. Sweep the Bedrock-era prose out of the documents that are load-bearing — effort L

The 2026-08-17 migration changed the code and not the prose, and the stale prose is not decoration: two runbooks would break a deploy or overcharge if followed, pricing.ts's docblock is the recorded justification for a rule-13 copy decision, and three copy-honesty ban patterns carry rationales that grep now disproves — inviting a correct-looking removal of the guards that stop the pricing page selling model unlocks.

1. Rewrite docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md sections 1.1d and 2.1: drop the Bedrock pricing link and the at-cost margin sentence (a commercial claim in a public repo, and the exact string check-catalog-parity.mjs bars from Stripe descriptions), replace ModelId/FoundationModelId with the real parameter set including SecretId, and make the 'metering is on' check require RateCardSecret so it cannot report a false positive.
2. Rewrite docs/pricing-cost-basis.md: delete the Cost Explorer / Bedrock derivation section, mark prompt caching shipped (cache_control, not cachePoint), correct MAX_TOKENS(800) to the per-model MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS map, and add the per-model non-monotonic minimum cacheable prefix — the one fact that decides whether the cache engages at all.
3. Rewrite the TUTOR_RATES docblock in web/src/lib/pricing.ts so it describes the current tree, and restate the reason the tier field is omitted in terms of the DEPLOYED function rather than a code shape that no longer exists.
4. Update the three copy-honesty rationales in web/__tests__/app/pricing-page.test.tsx to cite what is true today (the deployed tutor is free, unmetered and single-model), so the bans survive a maintainer who follows the file's own re-verify instruction.
5. Delete lambda/tutor/policy.json and remove the --policy-document fallback from lambda/tutor/README.md; it grants dead Bedrock permissions, no secretsmanager:GetSecretValue, and publishes account $AWS_ACCOUNT in a public repo.
6. Add a grep-based guard (or extend the no-commercial-terms test) that fails on /bedrock/i outside historical changelogs, so the next provider move cannot leave this residue.

**First commit.** Rewrite sections 1.1d and 2.1 of docs/billing-cutover-runbook.md against the Anthropic tutor: remove the Bedrock pricing link and the at-cost margin sentence, replace ModelId/FoundationModelId with the real parameter list including SecretId, and add RateCardSecret to the metering-on check.

**Covers.** `f95b218a8` The billing cutover runbook's tutor section is written against Bedrock: it orders RATES corrected to Bedrock prices, passes template parameters that no longer exist, and its 'metering is on' check returns a false positive · `f5029e6b8` The cutover runbook's rate-verification step still asserts the retired at-cost margin framing and points at Bedrock pricing, in a public repo · `fe64ef2d0` docs/pricing-cost-basis.md — CLAUDE.md's designated cost-driver source of truth — still teaches Bedrock cost derivation and calls prompt caching "not yet shipped" · `f6b4b0fa1` docs/pricing-cost-basis.md calls prompt caching 'the highest-leverage unshipped change in the repo' after it shipped, and cites an identifier that no longer exists · `ffc520829` pricing.ts's stated justification for omitting tier mapping describes a tutor that no longer exists in git · `f3b8514db` The TUTOR_RATES docblock in pricing.ts states three things about the tutor path that are all false since the migration · `f10ee9469` pricing.ts documents a tutor architecture that the 2026-08-17 Anthropic migration deleted, and that stale claim is the recorded justification for a rule-13 copy decision · `f4c03161b` web/src/lib/pricing.ts — the file rule 8 names as the pricing source of truth — documents a tutor path that no longer exists, and uses that description to justify omitting the tier field · `f82984d24` Three copy-honesty patterns are justified by rationales that grep now disproves, and the guard's own doctrine says such patterns should be deleted · `fd41306fa` policy.json is still an un-migrated Bedrock IAM policy with the production account id hardcoded, and the tutor README documents deploying with it · `f54ce715f` lambda/tutor/policy.json still publishes the production AWS account id in a public repo, and is now a dead Bedrock policy the README tells you to attach

### 09. Identity and data lifecycle: an unverified email that gets mailed, and rows that outlive the account — effort M

The repo already contains the correct pattern for each of these — qpu-core.mjs checks email_verified on the spend gate, and the Stripe template deliberately refuses DeleteItem — but neither is applied where it matters. Together they mean the platform can mail an address it never verified from its production SES domain, and 'delete my account' leaves the wallet, the debt, and the lifetime QPU ledger behind while telling the learner otherwise.

1. Gate on claims.email_verified in lambda/sync/index.mjs before persisting the email, matching qpu-core.mjs:763, and correct the comment that calls it 'the verified email claim'.
2. Decide the deletion policy for financial rows (delete, anonymise, or retain for accounting with a stated retention period) and implement it — a scoped delete/anonymise path for the WALLET# and ledger rows rather than a broad DeleteItem grant.
3. Update the disclosure list in web/src/components/auth/delete-account.tsx to name every row the flow does and does not remove, including the balance and any outstanding clawback debt.
4. Decide whether purchased credits survive deletion at all: rule 10 promises they never expire, and today a re-registration mints a new sub that can never reach them.
5. Re-evaluate the Founding Ten registry: an unsalted SHA-256 of a low-entropy email published beside the holder's real name, in the repo and in the client bundle, is a confirmation oracle. Move the check server-side, or key the hash, or get explicit consent — and apply the same decision to scripts/founding-credit/cohort-2026-08.json.

**First commit.** Require claims.email_verified in lambda/sync/index.mjs before writing the email onto the progress row, with a handler test covering an unverified claim.

**Covers.** `f150cdd31` lambda/sync persists an unverified `email` claim, and lambda/review-email mails it — Cognito's email attribute is Mutable and SRP sessions can change it · `f6abb88d4` Account deletion leaves the wallet and QPU ledger rows behind, and the UI's list of what will be deleted omits them · `f428e799a` The Founding Ten registry ships a real person's full name beside an unsalted SHA-256 of their email, in a public repo and in the public JS bundle

### 10. Make the parity guards cover the surfaces they claim to cover — effort M

Every item here is a green signal over a real gap: `make drift` says all functions match git while the live tutor WAF sits two merged PRs behind, the failover buildspec silently drops the money-path tests it claims to mirror, CI tests Lambdas on a runtime nobody deploys, and the Pyodide runtime shipped to every learner has no integrity check. The cost of each is small; the cost of trusting a guard that cannot see the defect is the whole point of the drift work.

1. Redeploy the quantum-tutor-edge stack so the browser-readable 429 and the OPTIONS exemption from #186 are actually live, then extend scripts/check-lambda-drift.mjs (or add a sibling) to diff deployed CloudFormation templates for the edge/WAF stacks, not just Lambda function code.
2. Redeploy quantum-ci-standby from the committed template and add a CI step that diffs the deployed inline buildspec against ci.yml's job list, so the mirror cannot silently drop the founding-credit tests or the KaTeX assertion.
3. Raise NODE_VERSION to 22 in ci.yml and the CodeBuild mirror to match the nodejs22.x runtime every template declares, and derive it from one place.
4. Pin the Pyodide tarball and wheel checksums in a committed manifest, verify them in web/jupyterlite-build/build.sh before extraction, and fail the build on mismatch — the cache in ci.yml and amplify.yml means one bad fetch persists.
5. Commit a lockfile for the build environment (the untracked uv.lock is consumed by nothing) and bound jupyter-server/build in requirements.txt so 'CI was green on this commit' is a statement about a fixed input set.
6. Add .github/dependabot.yml.

**First commit.** Extend scripts/check-lambda-drift.mjs to compare the deployed quantum-tutor-edge CloudFormation template against lambda/tutor/edge.yaml and report it in `make drift`.

**Covers.** `f4b00f00a` The live tutor WAF is two merged PRs behind lambda/tutor/edge.yaml, and the daily drift job reports green because it inspects only Lambda function code · `fb8858b38` The deployed CodeBuild standby buildspec is missing the founding-credit money-path test step that is in the committed template · `f975caec6` CI tests every Lambda on Node 20 while all ten deployed functions run nodejs22.x · `f049f7b89` The Pyodide runtime shipped to every learner is fetched with no integrity check, cached across builds, and installed by an unpinned pip resolve

### 11. Web app: silent data loss, silent screen-reader failures, and a CSP a test forbids fixing — effort M

These are ordinary front-end defects, but two of them lose the learner's work or feedback silently — a discarded render destroys the playground handoff, and four live regions mounted with their content may never be announced — and one is a test that locks a security gap in place, the same anti-pattern that let the sponsorship copy outlive its withdrawal.

1. Move consumeHandoff() out of the useState initializer into an effect (or make it non-destructive until commit), so a discarded or repeated mount cannot silently replace the learner's circuit with PRESETS[0].
2. Replace the negative CSP assertions in custom-http.test.ts with a positive assertion of the intended policy, then ship script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' 'wasm-unsafe-eval' blob: (which satisfies both Pyodide and Next.js) plus default-src and style-src.
3. Keep the four role="status" regions permanently mounted and write text into them, following ask-tutor.tsx's sr-only pattern: review-card, the quiz schedule note, the /review completion banner, and the auth resend confirmation.
4. Add app/error.tsx, global-error.tsx and not-found.tsx, and wrap the lazy widgets in review-dashboard.tsx in the existing WidgetErrorBoundary.
5. Memoise or cache the localStorage scans behind dueCount()/use-workspace's getSnapshot so a render pass does not trigger four full scans and 2N+M parses on the main thread.
6. Replace the hardcoded English string in top-up.tsx with t("pricingUi.checkoutFailed"), and add a rendered-error-state case to the locale copy guard.
7. Give /workspace a metadata export (extract the client body into a child component) so it carries a title and the noindex directive auth-wall.tsx claims for it.

**First commit.** Keep the review-card's role="status" schedule note permanently mounted and write its text in, matching the sr-only live-region pattern already used in ask-tutor.tsx.

**Covers.** `f0d2975d1` consumeHandoff() runs inside a render-phase useState initializer, so a discarded render silently destroys the playground-to-workspace circuit · `f63d74755` A test asserts the CSP must NOT contain script-src, style-src or default-src, so hardening it turns CI red · `f0f7d9816` Four status live regions are mounted at the same moment their content appears, so screen readers may never announce them · `fc25d9412` src/app contains no error.tsx, global-error.tsx or not-found.tsx, and the code relies on that absence being known rather than fixed · `f65b9f6f4` The nav due-count badge runs a full localStorage scan plus a JSON.parse per card inside getSnapshot, on every page of the site · `ffbc65b61` The top-up card's checkout-failure message is hardcoded English while its Spanish translation exists and the sibling button uses it · `f3f8d7a2e` /workspace is the only walled route with no metadata at all, and auth-wall.tsx documents a noindex protection it does not have

### 12. Curriculum correctness: code that teaches the wrong answer without erroring — effort M

Grover silently becomes the identity at 4+ qubits, the browser widget renders a different unitary from the library the next notebook uses (fidelity 0.746 on the GUIDE's own worked point), and the VQE runner reads the wrong bitstring column for any ansatz that skips a qubit. None raise; all are advertised in the GUIDEs; and the two guards that should catch this class — the runnable-contract scanner and the fence test — are themselves defeated.

1. Add the general multi-controlled-Z branch to grover_oracle and grover_diffusion in 03-algorithms/scripts/oracles.py (or raise on unsupported widths rather than returning the identity), with a pytest asserting amplification at n=4.
2. Route _expectation_from_pauli_counts in 05-quantum-chemistry/scripts/vqe_runner.py through lib.utils.results.parse_counts, as its hardened twin in 06-hybrid-jobs already does, and validate the ansatz's measured qubits.
3. Bring tests/ coverage to the scripts the GUIDEs advertise — neither oracles.py nor vqe_runner.py is imported by any test today, which is why CI cannot see either bug.
4. Fix _strip_magics in scripts/validate_runnable.py to strip only genuine IPython magics (first line of a cell / not inside a continuation), and make scan_notebook fail rather than warn-and-continue on a SyntaxError, so an unparseable cell cannot exempt itself from the qcsim contract.
5. Decide which IQP encoding is canonical, then make lib/ml/feature_maps.py and web/src/components/quantum/encoding.ts agree, and add a test pinning the two state vectors to each other.
6. Import qcsim before braket in tests/conftest.py (or isolate the fence test's interpreter) so test_guide_runnable_fences actually exercises qcsim, and correct the GUIDE script inventories in both locales to describe what the scripts contain.

**First commit.** Add the general multi-controlled-Z decomposition to grover_oracle and grover_diffusion in 03-algorithms/scripts/oracles.py, with a pytest asserting amplitude amplification for a 4-qubit marked state.

**Covers.** `fe61f70c2` grover_oracle and grover_diffusion silently become the identity for 4 or more qubits, so Grover's algorithm returns a uniform distribution with no error · `f39a705fb` vqe_runner indexes the raw measurement bitstring by absolute qubit index with no measured-qubits validation, the exact hazard its hardened twin routes through parse_counts to prevent · `f1f6e3e71` scripts/validate_runnable.py deletes any line beginning with % — including a printf-format continuation — which makes the cell unparseable and silently exempts it from the qcsim contract scan · `f676158b9` The IQP feature map the browser lesson widget renders is a different unitary from the one lib.ml.feature_maps.iqp_encoding builds, and the GUIDE describes them as the same map · `f644dc136` test_guide_runnable_fences executes the GUIDE sandbox fences against the real Braket SDK, not qcsim, so the guarantee in its own docstring is not being tested · `f8e75412a` Two GUIDE script inventories advertise capabilities the shipped scripts do not contain, in both locales

## What this audit could not settle

- The founder's own open item: does the published hardware sheet get re-derived from the factored server formula, or does the factor get chosen so every debit stays at or under the sheet? This audit can show the two are irreconcilable under one scalar, but not decide which side moves — and TUTOR_RATES' typicalCreditsPerQuestion goes stale either way. (qualifies `fe95b2089` — The pricing page and the QPU run surface publish two different credit prices for the same IQM Garnet run)
- Is Haiku intended to be free on every paid tier (what the handler does) or to cost the published 1 credit (what the sheet says)? This is a pricing decision, not a bug, and it interacts with the rule-2 free-trial counter that does not yet exist. (qualifies `fb40af359` — The pricing page charges 1 credit per Haiku question; the server charges zero for Haiku on every tier, including Pro)
- What should happen when a subscriber buys a second plan — refuse at /checkout, modify the existing Stripe subscription, or allow both and take the max tier? The correct cancellation semantics depend entirely on this answer. (qualifies `f552e4181` — Nothing prevents a second concurrent subscription, and cancelling either one unconditionally resets the still-paying subscriber to the free tier)
- Does the live Stripe account's webhook endpoint currently carry all nine events with a pinned api_version? The audit read the code and the runbooks, not the live Dashboard — check-webhook-parity.mjs against $STRIPE_ACCOUNT is the only way to know, and api_version is creation-only if it is wrong.
- Does Anthropic's streaming API ever emit a message_delta whose usage carries null input legs? The SDK's own types allow it and its accumulator guards it, but only a live streamed request against the deployed tutor proves whether the output-only pricing path is reachable in practice. (qualifies `f07ce0f4d` — The usage merge overwrites the input legs with nulls, pricing a generation at output-only, and readUsage's `?? 0` keeps the migration alarm silent)
- Have EDGE_SECRET or UNSUB_SECRET already been read through the over-granted drift role, and has the failover webhook ever been armed with a fork PR in flight? Only CloudTrail and the CodeBuild build history can answer, and the answer decides whether rotation is required or only the grant needs narrowing. (qualifies `f50e54cb6` — The CI drift role grants account-wide `lambda:ListFunctions` on a false premise — the API does return `Environment.Variables`, and nothing in the repo calls it)
- What is the retention policy for wallet and ledger rows after account deletion — delete, anonymise, or retain for accounting? And should purchased credits (which rule 10 says never expire) survive a delete-and-re-register at all? Both are founder/legal decisions this audit cannot make. (qualifies `f6abb88d4` — Account deletion leaves the wallet and QPU ledger rows behind, and the UI's list of what will be deleted omits them)
- Did the named Founding Ten holder consent to their full name being published in a public repo and client bundle beside a hash of their email? Whether the fix is consent, a keyed hash, or a server-side check depends on that answer and on whether client-side comparison is a hard requirement. (qualifies `f428e799a` — The Founding Ten registry ships a real person's full name beside an unsalted SHA-256 of their email, in a public repo and in the public JS bundle)
- Does the migrated tutor actually work end to end? The quantum-tutor secret does not exist and the stack is not redeployed, so no evidence in this repo — green offline tests included — establishes that a real question reaches api.anthropic.com and streams back. (qualifies `f04de5935` — AskTutor is mounted propless outside AuthWall, so the entire metered client path is unreachable in the shipped app)
- Which IQP encoding is pedagogically canonical — the library's rz(x_i) form or the widget's Havlicek ZZFeatureMap? Changing either alters published lesson output and the kernel matrix a learner is shown, so this is a curriculum decision before it is a code fix. (qualifies `f676158b9` — The IQP feature map the browser lesson widget renders is a different unitary from the one lib.ml.feature_maps.iqp_encoding builds, and the GUIDE describes them as the same map)

## Delta

A prior audit of this repository was published on 2026-08-16 and is committed at `docs/codebase-audit-2026-08-16.md`. It raised
60 findings; 22 were filed as tracked issues, of which 4
are now closed and 18 remain open.

Closed since:

- #230 — charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn claws back the whole purchase regardless of the disputed amount
- #218 — invoice.paid grants subscription credits without paying down clawback debt, so a renewing subscriber accumulates credits they can never spend
- #217 — A reinstated dispute returns the credits but never clears the debt it created, permanently locking the learner out of every metered surface
- #216 — The billing cutover GATE step verifies against the previous price and grant ($18 / +1890 credits)

No machine-readable twin of that audit exists, so this delta is computed from its finding titles and from issue
state, not from a structured diff. **This run establishes that baseline** — the redacted data twin beside this file
is what the next run diffs against.

## Method

8 readers, one per dimension, each given its file scope, the sixteen monetization rules and the
operational constraints extracted verbatim from `CLAUDE.md` and `PRODUCT.md`, and an instruction to read the code
rather than guess at it.

118 verification runs across 59 findings — two independent lenses each, *does this reproduce*
and *is the code correct as written*, both told to default to refuted when uncertain. Published severity is the
verifier's; the reader's claim is kept wherever the two differ.

Evidence mix: 28 observed live, 30 ran locally, 1 read.

**Coverage bounds — what was left out:**

- The 2,560 vendored files committed under one Lambda were not read as source; they are assessed as a supply-chain and hygiene question.
- 295 distinct repository paths were opened across the eight readers, out of 854 tracked source files.
- The 45 notebooks were read, not executed. Their outputs are stripped by an `nbstripout` filter and were not available to read.
- No third-party account was inspected. Findings about the Stripe Dashboard, deployed Lambda configuration, or live AWS state rest on the repository's own parity and drift guards unless a finding's evidence class says otherwise.

127 agents, three phases — read, refute, cluster. Rendered from one verified data file into this document and a published page, both
through one shared redactor that throws rather than writes — the repository is public and a published page is a URL
that can be shared onward.

---

*This document is a snapshot of one commit and it rots. Treat a finding older than a few weeks as a lead, not a fact:
re-open the file before acting on it.*
